## I

===Original===
I trust that, before relating the important events of which I have been an eyewitness, I may be allowed to say a few words about my early life and to explain the singular accidents and circumstances which resulted in my being present at our great naval catastrophe.

In speaking of my birth I cannot follow the example of most writers who narrate the facts of their own lives, and who begin by naming their ancestry—usually of noble rank, hidalgos at the very least, if not actually descended from some royal or imperial progenitor. I cannot grace my opening page with high-sounding names, for, excepting my mother whom I remember for some few years, I know nothing of any of my forefathers, unless it be Adam from whom my descent would seem to be indisputable. In short, my history began in much the same way as that of Pablos, the brigand of Segovia; happily it pleased God that it should resemble it in no other particular.

I was born at Cádiz in the notorious quarter De la Viña, which was not then, any more than at the present day, a good school of either morals or manners. My memory does not throw any light on the events of my infancy till I was six years old, and I remember that, only because I associate the idea of being six with an event I heard much talked about, the battle of Cape St. Vincent, which took place in 1797.

===Chinese===
我想，在讲述我亲眼见证的那些重大事件之前，应可容我先讲几句自己的早年经历，说明一下是怎样的奇缘凑巧与种种境遇，使我得以亲临我们那场惨痛的海上浩劫。

说到自己的出身，我无法效仿大多数记述自身生平的作家——他们开篇必先历数家世，纵然不是哪位帝王的后裔，至少也得是个低级贵族（hidalgo）。我没法用响当当的名字来装点开卷第一页，因为除了母亲——我对她尚有几年记忆——之外，我对自己的祖辈一概不知，除非把亚当（Adam）算上，我自他一脉相传，这一点似乎无可置疑。总而言之，我这部历史的开端，同塞哥维亚（Segovia）的强盗帕布洛斯（Pablos）如出一辙；所幸上帝开恩，让我与他在别的方面再无半点相似。

我出生在加的斯（Cádiz）一个声名狼藉的街区拉维尼亚（De la Viña），那里无论当年还是如今，都算不上修习德行或礼貌的好学堂。我的记忆对自己六岁以前的幼年经历一片昏暗；之所以单单记得六岁，只因我把六岁这件事同当时听人津津乐道的一件大事联系在了一起——一七九七年打的圣文森特角（Cape St. Vincent）海战。

===Original===
Endeavoring to see myself as I was at that time, with the curiosity and interest which must attach to self-contemplation, I am aware of a dim and hazy little figure in the picture of past events, playing in the creek with other small boys of the same age, more or less. This was to me the whole of life—as it was, at any rate, to our privileged class; those who did not live as I did appeared to me exceptional beings. In my childish ignorance of the world I firmly believed that man was made for the sea, Providence having created him to swim as being the noblest exercise of his limbs and body, and to dive for crabs as the highest use of his intelligence—and especially to fish up and sell the highly-esteemed crustacean known as “Bocas de la Isla”—as well as for his personal delectation and enjoyment, thus combining pleasure with profit.

The society into which I was born was indeed of the roughest, as ignorant and squalid as can well be imagined; so much so that the boys of our quarter of the town were regarded as even lower than those of the adjoining suburb of Puntales, whose occupations were the same and who defied the elements with equal devilry; the result of this invidious distinction was that each party looked upon the other as rivals, and the opposing forces would meet from time to time for a pitched battle with stones, when the earth was stained with heroic blood.

When I was old enough to begin to think that I might go into business on my own account, with a view to turning an honest penny, I remember that my sharpness stood me in good stead on the quay where I acted as *valet de place* to the numerous English who then, as now, disembarked there. The quay was a free academy peculiarly fitted to sharpen the wits and make the learner wide-awake, and I was not one of the least apt of its disciples in that wide branch of human experience; nor did I fail to distinguish myself in petty thefts, especially of fruit, an art for which the Plaza de San Juan offered an ample field, both for the experiments of the beginner and the exploits of the adept. But I have no wish to enlarge on this part of my history, for I blush with shame now, as I remember the depth to which I had sunk, and I thank God for having released me from it at an early period, and directed me into a better path.

===Chinese===
怀着顾影自赏必然带来的好奇与兴致，我极力想看清自己当年的模样，于是在往事的画卷里，浮现出一个影影绰绰的小小身影，正同年纪不相上下的男孩们在河汊里玩耍。这在我看来就是生活的全部——至少对我们这个得天独厚的阶层而言如此；凡不这样过活的人，在我眼里都属异类。在我孩童式的蒙昧无知中，我坚信人生来是为大海设造的：天意造他出来，是要他游泳，以此作为四肢躯体的最高贵运动；是要他潜水捉蟹，以此作为智力的最高用途——尤其要他捕捞贩卖那种备受推崇、号称“博卡斯·德·拉·伊斯拉”（Bocas de la Isla）的甲壳之物——同时也要他聊以自娱、尽兴取乐，从而把营生与快活合而为一。

我生于其中的那个社会，实在粗鄙已极，其愚昧龌龊，可想而知；以致我们街区的男孩，竟被看得比毗邻的蓬塔莱斯（Puntales）郊区的男孩还要低贱——虽说两边干的营生一模一样，顶风犯浪时的那股泼皮邪劲也不相上下。这种招怨的分等，结果是两边互把对方视作仇雠，两军人马不时摆开阵仗，用石块打一场堂堂之阵，大地也就浸染了英雄的碧血。

等到上了点年纪，开始想着自己也该单干一桩营生、赚几个干净钱的时候，我记得我的机灵在码头上大有用场——我在那里给为数众多的英国人当*valet de place*（随行导游），当年一如如今，他们都在那里登岸。码头是一所不收学费的学堂，最宜磨快脑筋、练出机警，而在人类经验的这门大学科里，我也算弟子中不落人后的一个；此外，我在小偷小摸上也颇有建树，尤其是偷水果这门艺术——圣胡安广场（Plaza de San Juan）为此提供了广阔天地，初学者的试验与老手的大展身手，都不愁没有用武之地。不过我不愿多谈自己历史上的这一段，因为如今一想起我当年沉沦的深度，仍不免羞愧满面；我感谢上帝早早把我从那里解脱出来，引我踏上一条更好的路。

===Original===
Among the impressions which remain most vivid in my memory is the enthusiastic delight I felt at the sight of vessels of war, when they anchored outside Cádiz or in the cove of San Fernando. As I had no means of satisfying my curiosity, when I saw these enormous structures I conceived the most absurd and fanciful ideas about them, imagining them as full of mysteries.

Always eager to mimic the greater world around us, we boys too had our squadrons of little ships, roughly hewn in wood, with sails of paper or of rag, which we navigated with the greatest deliberation and gravity in the pools of Puntales or La Caleta. To make all complete, whenever a few coppers came into our hands, earned by one or another of our small industries, we bought powder of old “Aunt Coscoja” in the street Del Torno de Santa María, and with this we could have a grand naval display. Our fleets sailed before the wind in an ocean three yards across, fired off their cannon, came alongside of each other to mimic a hand-to-hand fight—in which the imaginary crews valiantly held their own, and swarmed into the tops unfurling the flag, made of any scrap of colored rag we could pick up in a dust-heap—while we danced with ecstasy on the shore at the popping of the artillery, imagining ourselves to be the nationalities represented by our respective standards, and almost believing that in the world of grown-up men and great events the nations too would leap for joy, looking on at the victories of their splendid fleets. Boys see things through strange windows.

Those were times of great sea-fights, for there was one at least every year and a skirmish every month. I thought that fleets met in battle simply and solely because they enjoyed it, or to prove their strength and valor, like two bullies who meet outside the walls to stick knives into each other. I laugh when I recollect the wild ideas I had about the persons and events of the time. I heard a great deal about Napoleon and how do you think I had pictured him to myself! In every respect exactly like the smugglers whom we not unfrequently saw in our low quarter of the town: *Contrabandistas* from the lines at Gibraltar. I fancied him a man on horseback, on a Xerez nag, with a cloak, high boots, a broad felt-hat, and a blunderbuss of course. With these accoutrements, and followed by other adventurers on the same pattern, I supposed this man, whom all agreed in describing as most extraordinary, to have conquered Europe, which I fancied was a large island within which were other islands which were the different nations: England, Genoa, London, France, Malta, the land where the Moors lived, America, Gibraltar, Port Mahon, Russia, Toulon and so forth. This scheme of geography I had constructed on the basis of the names of the places from which the ships came whose passengers I had to deal with; and I need not say that of all these nations or islands Spain was the very best, for which reason the English—men after the likeness of highwaymen—wanted to get it for their own. Talking of these and similar matters I and my amphibious companions would give vent to sentiments and opinions inspired by the most ardent patriotism.

===Chinese===
在我记忆中留得最鲜活的印象里，有一桩便是望见战舰时的欣喜若狂——它们或在加的斯外海抛锚，或停进圣费尔南多（San Fernando）的小湾。由于无从满足我的好奇，一见到这些庞然大物，我便对它们生出种种荒诞至极的奇想，把它们的肚肠想象成满盛奥秘。

我们这班男孩一心爱学周围大世界的样，也有自己的小船舰队——船是木头粗削出来的，帆是纸糊的或破布做的——我们驾着它们，怀着无上的郑重与庄严，航行在蓬塔莱斯或卡莱塔（La Caleta）的水塘里。为把一切置办齐全，每逢哪样小营生挣来的几枚铜子儿到手，我们就到托尔诺·德·圣玛丽亚街（Del Torno de Santa María）去找老“科斯科哈大婶”（Aunt Coscoja）买火药，有了它，便能来一场盛大的海军操演。我们的舰队在三码宽的大洋上乘风扬帆，鸣炮施放，彼此接舷，模拟一场白刃肉搏——幻想中的水手们个个死战不退，还攀上桅顶展开旗帜，那旗是用我们从灰土堆里捡来的什么彩色布头做成的——我们则伴着火炮的噼啪声在岸边欢喜得手舞足蹈，把自己当作各自旗帜所代表的那些国族，而且几乎当真相信：在成年人和大事纷呈的那个世界里，各国民众目睹自己堂皇舰队的凯旋，也会这样欢腾雀跃。孩子们看世界，隔的窗子可真古怪。

那是海战频仍的年月，一年至少一场大战，一月必有一场小接触。我那时以为，舰队交战无非因为它们乐此不疲，或是要较量气力与胆量，好比两个恶棍约在城墙外互捅刀子。想起当年我对那个时代的人物与事件怀有的种种狂想，我不禁失笑。拿破仑（Napoleon）的事我听得多了——你们猜猜，我把他想象成了什么模样！各个方面都同我们那下等街区里时不时出没的走私贩分毫不差：从直布罗陀（Gibraltar）防线那边来的*Contrabandistas*（走私贩）。我想象他是个骑马的汉子，骑一匹赫雷斯（Xerez）矮种马，身披斗篷，脚踏高筒马靴，头戴宽檐毡帽，腰间当然还挎着一支大口径短铳。凭着这身行头，身后再跟上一伙同一副路数的亡命徒，我以为这个众口交赞为旷世奇人的人物，就是这样征服了欧洲；至于欧洲，我把它想成一座大岛，岛里又嵌着许多岛，便是各个国家：英格兰、热那亚（Genoa）、伦敦（London）、法兰西、马耳他（Malta）、摩尔人（Moors）居住的地方、美洲、直布罗陀、马翁港（Port Mahon）、俄罗斯、土伦（Toulon），等等。这幅地理图景，是我拿轮船来处的地名拼搭而成的——船上乘客正是我要应付的主顾；不消说，在所有这些国家或岛屿之中，西班牙（Spain）是顶好的一个，正因如此，英国人——一伙活脱脱剪径强盗模样的人——才想把它夺到自己手里。谈论这些及诸如此类的话题时，我和我那班两栖的小伙伴，总要抒发由最炽热的爱国心肠里激发出来的高论与情操。

===Original===
However, I need not weary the reader with trifles which relate only to my personal fancies, so I will say no more about myself. The one living soul that made up to me for the wretchedness of life by a wholly disinterested love for me, was my mother. All I can remember of her is that she was extremely pretty, or at any rate she seemed so to me. From the time when she was left a widow she maintained herself and me by doing washing, and mending sailors’ clothes. She must have loved me dearly. I fell ill of yellow fever which was raging in Andalusia and when I got well she took me solemnly to mass at the old cathedral and made me kneel on the pavement for more than an hour, and then, as an *ex-voto* offering, she placed an image in wax of a child, which I believed to be an exact likeness of myself, at the foot of the altar where the service had been performed.

My mother had a brother, and if she was pretty, he was ugly and a cruel wretch into the bargain. I cannot think of my uncle without horror, and from one or two occurrences which I remember vividly I infer that this man must have committed some crime at the time I refer to. He was a sailor; when he was on shore and at Cádiz he would come home furiously drunk, and treat us brutally—his sister with words, calling her every abusive name, and me with deeds, beating me without any reason whatever.

My mother must have suffered greatly from her brother’s atrocities, and these, added to severe labor for miserable pay, hastened her death which left an indelible impression on my feelings, though the details dwell but vaguely in my memory. During this period of misery and vagabondage my only occupations were playing by the seashore or running about the streets. My only troubles were a beating from my uncle, a frown from my mother, or some mishap in the conduct of my squadrons. I had never felt any really strong or deep emotion till the loss of my mother showed me life under a harder and clearer aspect than it had ever before presented to me. The shock it gave me has never faded from my mind. After all these years I still remember, as we remember the horrible pictures of a bad dream, that my mother lay prostrate from some sickness, I know not what; I remember women coming and going, whose names and purpose I cannot recall; I remember hearing cries of lamentation, and being placed in my mother’s arms, and then I remember the shudder that ran through my whole body at the touch of a cold, cold hand. I think I was then taken away; but mixed up with these dim memories I can see the yellow tapers which gave a ghastly light at midday, I can hear the muttering of prayers, the hoarse whispers of the old gossips, the laughter of drunken sailors—and then came the lonely sense of orphanhood, the certainty that I was alone and abandoned in the world, which for a time absorbed me entirely.

===Chinese===
不过，这些只关乎我个人痴想的琐碎，我不必絮絮叨叨去烦扰读者，自己也就不再多说。在我凄凉的生涯中，以一份全然无私的爱弥补生活苦况的，唯有我母亲这一个活人。关于她，我记得的全部，就是她极其标致——至少在我眼里是如此。自打守寡，她便靠替人浆洗、缝补水手衣裤，挣出我们娘儿俩的嚼裹。她必定是疼我的。黄热病在安达卢西亚（Andalusia）肆虐那年，我染上了病；痊愈之后，她郑重其事地带我到老教堂望弥撒，叫我在石板地上跪了一个多钟头，随后，又把一尊蜡制的孩童小像供在举行过礼仪的祭坛脚下作为还愿（ex-voto）供品——我认定那像与我本人一般无二。

母亲有个兄弟；她若是标致，他却生得丑陋，还兼是个狠毒的恶棍。我一想起这位叔叔，便止不住心惊胆战；从一两件我记得真真切切的事推断，这个人在我所说的年月里必定作下过什么罪案。他是个水手；每逢上岸回到加的斯，总要喝得酩酊大醉地进门，对自家亲人横加荼毒——对姐姐动嘴，什么秽语恶名都骂得出；对我动手，不问青红皂白便是一顿好打。

母亲受她兄弟的暴虐，必定吃了大苦；这些苦楚，加上劳作极重、工钱极贱的活计，加速了她的死亡。她的死在我感情上留下不可磨灭的烙印，只是种种细节在记忆里早已昏昧不清。在那段凄惨流浪的岁月里，我唯一的事，是在海边玩耍或在街面上乱跑；唯一的烦恼，不过是挨叔叔一顿打、挨母亲一个蹙眉，或是我的舰队遭了什么不测。直到失去母亲，我才头一遭领略真正强烈而深刻的情感：它让我把生活看得比以往任何时候都更冷酷、更清晰。那一记震动，至今没有从我脑海里消褪。隔了这么多年，我仍然记得——就像记得一场噩梦里的可怖画面——母亲害了什么病直挺挺躺着，我至今不知那是什么病；我记得女人们进进出出，她们的名姓与来意我已无从追想；我记得听见哭嚎悲号，记得被人放进母亲怀里，随后记得一只冰凉冰凉的手一触，我浑身掠过一阵战栗。我想随后我就被领走了；可与这些昏昧记忆纠缠在一起的，还有正午时分发出惨淡光亮的黄蜡烛，有喃喃的祷词、老娘们儿嘶哑的耳语、醉水手的哄笑——接着便涌上孤儿的凄凉，确知自己在这世上孑然一身、被人抛下，这感觉一度把我整个吞没。

===Original===
I have no recollection of what my uncle was doing at that time; I only know that his brutality to me increased to such a point that, weary of his cruelty, I ran away, determined to seek my fortune. I fled to San Fernando and from thence to Puerto Real. I hung on to the lowest class that haunt the shore, which has always been a famous nest for gaol-birds. Why or wherefore I quite forget, but I found myself with a gang of these choice spirits at Medina-Sidonia when, one day, a tavern where we were sitting was entered by a press-gang and we promptly separated, each hiding himself as best he might. My good star led me to a house where the owners had pity on me, taking the greatest interest in me, no doubt by reason of the story I told, on my knees and drowned in tears, of my miserable plight, my past life and all my misfortunes.

These good people took me under their protection and saved me from the press-gang, and from that time I remained in their service. With them I went to Vejer de la Frontera where they lived; they had only been passing through Medina-Sidonia.

My guardian angels were Don Alonso Gutierrez de Cisniega, a ship’s captain, and his wife, both advanced in years. They taught me much that I did not know, and as they took a great fancy to me before long I was promoted to be Don Alonso’s page, accompanying him in his daily walks, for the worthy veteran could not use his right arm, and it was with difficulty that he moved his right leg. What they saw in me to arouse their interest I do not know; my tender years, my desolate circumstances and no doubt too my ready obedience may have contributed to win their benevolence, for which I have always been deeply grateful. I may also add—though I say it that should not—as explaining their kind feeling towards me, that although I had always lived among the lowest and most destitute class, I had a certain natural refinement of mind which enabled me very soon to improve in manners, and in a few years, notwithstanding I had no opportunities for learning, I could pass for a lad of respectable birth and training.

===Chinese===
叔叔当时在干些什么，我全无印象；我只知道他待我的凶暴愈演愈烈，我受够了这份残虐，终于出逃，打定主意去闯自己的运道。我先逃到圣费尔南多，再从那里逃到雷亚尔港（Puerto Real）。我厮混在滨海滋生的最下等人堆里——那向来是窝藏监狱常客的老巢。为的什么、如何去的，我全忘了；反正有一天，我发觉自己正同一帮这样的“人中翘楚”混在梅迪纳-西多尼亚（Medina-Sidonia），我们坐着的那家酒馆闯进来一支抓丁队（press-gang），众人当即作鸟兽散，各自寻处躲藏。我的福星把我引到一户人家，主人家对我心生怜悯，关切备至——毫无疑问，是因我跪在地上、涕泪滂沱地诉说了自己的苦境、身世和种种不幸。

这几位善心人把我收在他们的庇护之下，救我脱开了抓丁队，从那时起，我便留在他们家里当差。我随他们去了他们的定居之地韦赫尔-德·拉·弗龙特拉（Vejer de la Frontera）；他们原只是路过梅迪纳-西多尼亚而已。

我的守护天使，是舰长堂阿隆索·古铁雷斯·德·西斯涅加（Don Alonso Gutierrez de Cisniega）和他的夫人，两位都已年事渐高。他们教了我许多我不知道的事；由于他们十分喜欢我，不久我便升作堂阿隆索的贴身小僮，每天陪他散步，因为这位可敬的老兵右臂使唤不得，右腿挪动也很艰难。他们究竟看中了我哪一点，我说不上来；我年岁尚小、处境孤苦，多半还有我事事听话驯顺，大约都为我赢得了他们的仁慈，对此我始终铭感于心。我还可以添上一句——虽说不该自己这样说——以解释他们待我的这片好意：尽管我一直在最卑贱、最穷困的阶层里讨生活，我却天生心性里带几分清气，很快便在举止仪态上大有长进；不出几年，纵使没有机会读书受教育，我也能充得像个出身体面、受过调教的孩子了。

===Original===
I had spent four years in this home when the events happened which I must now relate. The reader must not expect an accuracy of detail which is out of my power when speaking of events which happened in my tender youth, to be recalled in the evening of my existence when I am near the end of a long and busy life and already feel the slow poison of old age numbing the fingers that use the pen; while the torpid brain strives to cheat itself into transient return of youth, by conjuring up the sweet or ardent memories of the past. As some old men strive to revive the warm delights of the past by gazing at pictures of the beauties they have known, I will try to give some interest and vigor to the faded reminiscences of my long past days, and to warm them with the glow of a counterfeit presentment of departed glories.

The effect is magical! How marvellous are the illusions of fancy! I look back with curiosity and astonishment at the bygone years, as we look through the pages of a book we were reading, and left with a leaf turned down to mark the place; and so long as the charm works I feel as if some beneficent genius had suddenly relieved me of the weight of old age, mitigating the burden of years which crushes body and spirit alike. This blood—this tepid and languid ichor, which now scarcely lends warmth and life to my failing limbs, grows hot again, flows, boils, and fires my veins with a swifter course. A sudden light breaks in upon my brain, giving color and relief to numberless strange figures—just as the traveller’s torch, blazing in some dark cavern, reveals the marvels of geology so unexpectedly that it seems as though they were then and there created. And my heart rises from the grave of past emotions—a Lazarus called by the voice of its Lord—and leaps in my breast with joy and pain at once.

I am young again; time has turned backwards, I stand in the presence of the events of my boyhood; I clasp the hands of old friends, the joys and griefs of my youth stir my soul once more—the fever of triumph, the anguish of defeat, intense delights, acute sorrows—all crowded and mixed in my memory as they were in life. But stronger than any other feeling one reigns supreme, one which guided all my actions during the fateful period between 1805 and 1834. As I approach the grave and reflect how useless I am among men—even now tears start to my eyes with the sacred love of country. I can only serve it with words—cursing the base scepticism which can deny it, and the corrupt philosophy which can treat it as a mere fashion of a day.

This was the passion to which I consecrated the vigor of my manhood, and to this I will devote the labors of my last years, enthroning it as the tutelary genius, the guiding spirit of my story as it has been of my existence. I have much to tell. Trafalgar, Bailén, Madrid, Zaragoza, Gerona, Arapiles!—I can tell you something of all these, if your patience does not fail. My story may not be as elegantly told as it should be but I will do my best to insure its being true.

===Chinese===
我在这个家里住满四年，便发生了我如今必须讲述的那些事件。读者不可指望从我这支笔下得到细节上分毫不差的准确——讲述的是柔弱少年时代的事，追忆却是在人生的暮年，我已临近漫长而劳碌的一生的尽头，早已感到老年的慢性毒药正麻着我执笔的手指；而迟钝的头脑还在竭力自欺，靠召唤往日或甘美或炽烈的记忆，骗取一段短暂的青春回返。正如有些老人凝望旧日相识的美人画像，借以重温当年温馨的欢愉，我也要设法给我久已褪色的陈年忆事添些生趣与气力，用一幅仿造的逝去荣光的暖辉，把它们烘暖。

效果真是神奇！幻想的错觉何等不可思议！我怀着好奇与惊异回望逝去的年华，就像翻检一本读过的旧书，书页里还折着角、标着停读的地方；只要这道魔咒灵验，我便觉得仿佛有一位慈善的精灵，忽然替我卸下老年的重负，减轻了那把肉体与精神一并压垮的岁月的重担。这血——这如今几乎再不能给我衰朽的肢体添些暖意与生气的、温吞而倦怠的灵液——重新热了起来，奔涌、沸腾，以更迅疾的流势点燃我的血管。一道突如其来的光亮照进我的脑海，给无数奇形怪状的身影敷上色彩、显出凹凸——恰似旅人的火把在幽暗的岩洞里熊熊燃起，把地底的奇观照得猝不及防，仿佛它们就在那一刻、那当场方才造出。我的心从往日情感的坟茔里升起——一个被主的声音唤起的拉撒路（Lazarus）——在我胸中伴着欢乐与疼痛一齐跃动。

我又年轻了；时光倒流，我重新站到了少年时代那些事件的面前；我握住老友们的双手，青春的欢乐与哀伤再度激荡我的灵魂——得胜的热狂，败北的锥心，酣浓的快意，尖锐的悲恸——全都挤挤挨挨、杂然并存于我的记忆，一如当年在生活里那样。然而有一种情感胜过其余一切、君临万情，正是它在一八〇五年到一八三四年那个关乎命运的时期里，指引了我的全部行动。如今我行将就木，思量着自己于人世如何无用——即便此刻，那神圣的爱国之情仍催我热泪夺眶。我只能以言语为它效力——诅咒那胆敢否定它的卑劣的怀疑论，诅咒那竟把它视作一时风尚的腐朽哲学。

这便是我把壮年的精力奉献于斯的激情；我还要把我晚年的辛劳奉献于它，把它奉为守护神，奉为我这部故事的指引之灵——一如它素来是我此生的指引。我有许多可讲的。特拉法尔加（Trafalgar）、拜伦（Bailén）、马德里（Madrid）、萨拉戈萨（Zaragoza）、赫罗纳（Gerona）、阿拉皮莱斯（Arapiles）！——只要你们的耐心不竭，这些我都能给你们讲上一些。我的故事也许讲得不够文雅，但我必尽力保证它的真实。

## II

===Original===
It was on one of the early days of October in that fatal year, 1805, that my worthy master called me into his room and looked at me with the severity that was habitual to him﻿—a severity that was only on the surface for his nature was gentleness itself﻿—he said:

“Gabriel, are you a brave man?”

I did not know what to answer, for, to tell the truth, in my fourteen years of life no opportunity had ever presented itself for me to astonish the world with any deed of valor; still, it filled me with pride to hear myself called a man, and thinking it ill-judged to deny myself the credit of courage before anyone who held it in such high estimation, I answered, with boyish boldness:

“Yes, sir, I am a brave man.”

At this the noble gentleman, who had shed his blood in a hundred glorious fights and who nevertheless did not disdain to treat a faithful servant with frank confidence, smiled at me kindly, signed to me to take a seat, and seemed on the point of informing me of some business of importance, when his wife, my mistress, Doña Francisca entered the study, and, to give further interest to the discussion, began to declaim with vehemence.

===Chinese===
那是一八〇五年——那个灾难深重的年头——十月初的某一天，我尊敬的主人把我叫进他的房间，用他惯有的严厉目光打量着我——那严厉不过浮在表面，因为他的天性再温厚不过——他说道：

“加布里埃尔（Gabriel），你是个勇敢的人吗？”

我不知道该怎么回答，因为说实话，在我十四年的生涯里，还从没有过什么机会让我以英勇的壮举震惊世界；不过，听人把我唤作“男子汉”，我心里不免得意，又想到在一位如此看重胆气的人面前否认自己有勇，实在不智，便带着少年的莽撞答道：

“是的，先生，我是个勇敢的人。”

这位高贵的绅士曾在上百场光荣的战斗中流过血，却仍不吝以坦率的信任对待一个忠仆。他听了，和蔼地朝我一笑，示意我坐下，眼看就要向我吐露一桩要紧事，恰在此时，他的妻子、我的女主人堂娜·弗朗西斯卡（Doña Francisca）走进书房，为了给这场谈论再添几分热闹，扯开嗓子激烈地数落起来。

===Original===
“You are not to go,” she said, “I declare you shall not join the fleet. What next will you be wanting to do?﻿—at your age and when you have long retired as superannuated! No, no, Alonso my dear. You are past sixty and your dancing days are over.”

I can see her now, that respectable and indignant dame﻿—with her deep-bordered cap, her muslin dress, her white curls, and a hairy mole on one side of her chin. I describe these miscellaneous details, for they are inseparable from my recollection of her. She was pretty even in old age, like Murillo’s Santa Anna, and her sober beauty would have justified the comparison if only the lady had been as silent as a picture. Don Alonso somewhat cowed, as he always was, by her flow of words, answered quietly:

“I must go, Paquita. From the letter I have just now received from my worthy friend Churruca, I learn that the united squadrons are either to sail from Cádiz and engage the English or to wait for them in the bay in case they are so bold as to enter. In either case it will be no child’s play.”

“That is well, and I am glad to hear it,” replied Doña Francisca. “There are Gravina, Valdés, Cisneros, Churruca, Alcalá Galiano, and Álava; let them pound away at the English dogs. But you are a piece of useless lumber who can do no good if you go. Why you cannot move that left arm which they dislocated for you at Cape St. Vincent.”

===Chinese===
“你不许去，”她说，“我明说了吧，你休想去参加舰队。你还想干什么？——都你这把年纪了，而且早就因年老退了役！不，不行，我亲爱的阿隆索（Alonso）。你已年过六十，跳舞的日子早到头了。”

如今我仍能想见她，那位端庄而怒气冲冲的老夫人——戴着镶宽花边的软帽，穿着细棉布衣裙，鬈发霜白，下巴一侧长着一颗带毛的痣。我絮絮叨叨描画这些零碎细节，只因它们与我对她的记忆密不可分。她纵然年迈依然标致，活像穆里略（Murillo）笔下的圣安娜（Santa Anna），倘若这位夫人能像画中人一样缄默不语，她那素雅的美倒真当得起这个比拟。堂阿隆索（Don Alonso）照例被她的连珠炮似的一串话震得有些发怵，只平心静气地答道：

“我非去不可，帕基塔（Paquita）。方才接到我敬重的朋友丘鲁卡（Churruca）的来信，得知联合舰队要么驶出加的斯（Cádiz）去迎战英国人，要么就守在海湾里等他们，倘若他们胆敢闯进来的话。无论哪种情形，都绝不是儿戏。”

“那敢情好，我听了高兴，”堂娜·弗朗西斯卡答道，“有格拉维纳（Gravina）、巴尔德斯（Valdés）、希斯内罗斯（Cisneros）、丘鲁卡、阿尔卡拉·加利亚诺（Alcalá Galiano），还有阿拉瓦（Álava）；让那些人去狠狠揍英国狗好了。可你呢，就是一段派不上用场的朽木，去了也顶不了事。瞧瞧，你那条左胳膊在圣文森特角（Cape St. Vincent）给他们打得脱了臼，如今还抬不灵便呢。”

===Original===
My master lifted his arm, with a stiff attempt at military precision, to show that he could use it. But his wife, not convinced by so feeble an argument, went on with shrill asseveration.

“No, you shall not go, what can they want of a piece of antiquity like you. If you were still forty as you were when you went to Tierra del Fuego and brought me back those green Indian necklaces.﻿—Then indeed! But now!﻿—I know, that ridiculous fellow Marcial fired your brain this morning with talking to you about battles. It seems to me that Señor Marcial and I will come to quarrelling.﻿—Let him go to the ships if he likes and pay them out for the foot he lost! Oh! Saint Joseph the blessed! If I had known when I was a girl what you seamen were! Endless worry; never a day’s peace! A woman marries to live with her husband and one fine day a dispatch comes from Madrid and he is sent off at two minutes notice to the Lord knows where﻿—Patagonia or Japan or the infernal regions. For ten or twelve months she sees nothing of him and at last, if the savages have not eaten him meanwhile, he comes back again the picture of misery﻿—so ill and yellow that she does not know what to do to restore him to his right color. But old birds are not to be caught in a trap, and then suddenly another dispatch comes from Madrid, with orders to go to Toulon or Brest or Naples﻿—go here and go there﻿—wherever it is necessary to meet the whims of that rascally First Consul﻿ … ! If you would all do as I say, you would soon pay out these gentlemen who keep the world in a turmoil!”

My master sat smiling and gazing at a cheap print, badly colored by some cheap artist, which was nailed against the wall, and which represented the Emperor Napoleon mounted on a green charger, in the celebrated redingote which was smeared with vermilion. It was no doubt the sight of this work of art, which I had seen daily for four years, which had modified my ideas with respect to the smuggler’s costume of the great man of the day, and had fixed his image in my mind as dressed something like a cardinal and riding a green horse.

“This is not living!” Doña Francisca went on, throwing up her arms: “God forgive me, but I hate the sea, though they say it is one of His most glorious works. What is the use of the Holy Inquisition, will you tell me, if it is not to burn those diabolical ships of war to ashes? What is the good of this incessant firing of cannon﻿—balls upon balls, all directed against four boards, as you may say, which are soon smashed to leave hundreds of hapless wretches to drown in the sea? Is not that provoking God?﻿—And yet you men are half-wild as soon as you hear a cannon fired! Merciful Heaven! my flesh creeps at the sound, and if everyone was of my way of thinking, we should have no more sea-fights, and the cannon would be cast into bells. Look here, Alonso,” she said, standing still in front of her husband, “it seems to me that they have done you damage enough already; what more do you want? You and a parcel of madmen like yourself﻿—had you not enough to satisfy you on the 14th?”1

===Chinese===
主人抬起那条胳膊，僵硬地摆出军人般的标准姿势，好证明它还能使唤。可他的妻子对这样软弱无力的证据根本不服，尖着嗓子继续赌咒发誓地数落下去。

“不行，你不许去，像你这么件老古董，人家还要你做什么？你要是还像当年去火地岛（Tierra del Fuego）那时才四十岁，还给我带回那些绿色的印第安项链——那时候倒也罢了！可如今呢！——我知道，准是马西亚尔（Marcial）那个可笑的家伙今儿早上跟你大谈什么战斗，把你脑子说热了。依我看，马西亚尔先生跟我早晚要吵一场。——他要乐意，就让他上船去，为他丢掉的那只脚讨还这笔账去！噢！蒙受祝福的圣约瑟（Saint Joseph）啊！我做姑娘的时候要是知道你们这些水手是什么货色！没完没了的操心，一天安生日子都没有！女人家结了婚，本指望和丈夫过活，哪天马德里一道公文来，他两分钟之内就被打发到天知道什么地方去了——巴塔哥尼亚（Patagonia）也罢，日本也罢，还是阴曹地府。十个月十二个月见不着人影，末了，若是没让野人吃掉，他总算回来了，却是一副惨相——病恹恹、黄蜡蜡的，弄得她不知如何是好，简直没法把他那张脸恢复成本来的颜色。可老鸟是轻易不会再落进圈套的，突然马德里又来一道公文，命令开拔去土伦（Toulon）啦、布雷斯特（Brest）啦、那不勒斯（Naples）啦——东跑西颠——全看那位恶棍第一执政（First Consul）的性子要往哪儿使唤！……你们要是都听我的，管保很快就能治治这帮搅得天下不宁的爷们！”

主人坐着，笑吟吟地望着墙上钉着的一幅廉价版画——出自哪个蹩脚画匠之手，着色俗劣——画的是拿破仑（Napoleon）皇帝骑着一匹绿色战马，身上穿着那件出了名的长礼服，被涂抹得一片朱红。四年来我天天看这幅艺术杰作，毫无疑问，正是它修正了我对这位当世伟人那身走私贩式装束的想头，把他的形象在我心里固定了下来：穿得多少像个红衣主教，骑着一匹绿马。

“这叫什么日子！”堂娜·弗朗西斯卡继续说道，双臂一扬，“上帝饶恕我，我恨透了大海，尽管人们说那是他最荣耀的造物之一。请问，神圣的宗教裁判所（Holy Inquisition）若不能把这些恶魔似的战船烧成灰烬，那它还有什么用处？这样没完没了地放炮又有什么好处——一发接一发的炮弹，可以说全都冲着几块木板轰去，转眼砸个稀烂，让几百个倒霉虫淹死在海里？这岂不是在招惹上帝发怒？——可你们这些男人，一听见炮响就半疯半狂！慈悲的老天爷！那声音听得我肉皮发麻，要是人人都跟我一个想法，世上就再也不会有海战，大炮统统回炉铸成钟。你听着，阿隆索，”她说着，在丈夫面前站定，“依我看，他们害你害得也够多了，你还想怎样？你和一帮跟你一样的疯子——十四号那天的苦头还没受够吗？”1

===Original===
Don Alonso clenched his fists at this bitter reminiscence, and it was only out of consideration for his wife, to whom he paid the utmost respect, that he suppressed a good round oath.

“I lay all the blame of your absurd determination to join the fleet to that rascally Marcial,” the lady went on, warming with her own eloquence; “that maniac for the sea who ought to have been drowned a hundred times and over, but that he escaped a hundred times to be the torment of my life. If he wants to join, with his wooden leg, his broken arm, his one eye, and his fifty wounds﻿—let him go, by all means, and God grant he may never come back here again﻿—but you shall not go, Alonso, for you are past service and have done enough for the King who has paid you badly enough in all conscience. If I were you, I would throw those captain’s epaulettes you have worn these ten years in the face of the Generalissimo of the land and sea forces. My word! they ought have made you admiral, at least; you earned that when you went on that expedition to Africa and brought me back those blue beads which I gave with the Indian necklace to decorate the votive urn to the Virgin del Cármen.”

“Admiral or not, it is my duty to join the fleet, Paquita,” said my master. “I cannot be absent from this struggle. I feel that I must pay off some of my arrears to the English.”

“Do you talk of paying off arrears!” exclaimed my mistress; “you﻿—old, feeble, and half-crippled.﻿ …”

“Gabriel will go with me,” said Don Alonso, with a look at me which filled me with valor.

===Chinese===
听到这段辛酸的旧事，堂阿隆索攥紧了拳头，全出于对妻子的顾念——他对她向来敬重之至——才硬生生咽下一句痛快淋漓的咒骂。

“你非要参加舰队这个荒唐主意，我全都怪在那个恶棍马西亚尔头上，”夫人接着说，自己的雄辩越说越让她浑身来劲，“那个海上的疯子，早该淹死一百回都不止，偏生一百回都逃了性命，来折磨我这辈子。他要去，就让他拖着那条木头腿、那条断过的胳膊、那只独眼，揣着他那五十处伤去——尽管去，上帝保佑他再也别回这个家——可你不能去，阿隆索，你早已过了服役的年纪，为国王效的力也够多了，而凭良心说，他给你的酬报也够刻薄的了。我要是你，就把这十年戴在肩上的海军上校肩章，一把摔到陆海军大元帅（Generalissimo）脸上去。老天作证！他们至少也该升你当海军上将（admiral）；当年你远征非洲，给我带回那串蓝珠子——我把它连同印第安项链一起，拿去装饰供奉卡门圣母（Virgin del Cármen）的还愿龛了——单凭这份功劳你就当得起。”

“当不当上将，参加舰队总是我的本分，帕基塔，”主人说，“这一仗我不能缺席。我觉得，我欠英国人的旧账，总得清偿几笔。”

“亏你还说清账！”我的女主人嚷道，“你——又老又弱，半个残废。……

“加布里埃尔跟我一同去，”堂阿隆索说着，朝我看了一眼，那一眼让我浑身是胆。

===Original===
I bowed to signify that I agreed to this heroic scheme, but I took care not to be seen by my mistress, who would have let me feel the full weight of her hand if she had suspected my bellicose inclinations. Indeed, seeing that her husband was fully determined, she was more furious than ever, declaring that if she had to live her life again nothing should induce her to marry a sailor. She cursed the Emperor, abused our revered King, the Prince of Peace, and all those who had signed the Treaty of Subsidies, ending by threatening the brave old man with punishment from Heaven for his insane rashness.

During this dialogue, which I have reported with approximate exactness as I have to depend on my memory, a loud barking cough in the adjoining room revealed the fact that Marcial, the old sailor, could overhear with perfect ease, my mistress’s vehement harangue, in which she had so frequently mentioned him in by no means flattering terms. Being now desirous of taking part in the conversation, as his intimacy in the house fully justified his doing, he opened the door and came into Don Alfonso’s room. Before going any farther I must give some account of my master’s former history, and of his worthy wife, that the reader may have a better understanding of what follows.

===Chinese===
我鞠了一躬，表示我赞同这个英勇的安排，不过我留神躲着不让女主人瞧见；她若是疑心我也有好斗的心思，准会让我结结实实尝尝她那只巴掌的分量。果然，眼见丈夫铁了心，她愈发怒不可遏，扬言这辈子若能从头再来，说什么也决不再嫁水手。她诅咒皇帝，痛骂我们敬爱的国王、和平亲王（Prince of Peace），以及所有在津贴条约（Treaty of Subsidies）上签字的人，末了还威胁这位勇敢的老人，说他这样丧心病狂地莽撞行事，上天定会降罚于他。

在这场对话进行期间——我全凭记忆复述，只能求个大致不差——隔壁房间里一阵响亮的、狗吠似的咳嗽声透露出：老水手马西亚尔把我女主人这番慷慨激昂的长篇训话听了个一清二楚，而她在话中屡屡用绝不算恭维的字眼提到他。他这时有意加入谈话——他在这家里熟不拘礼的身份也完全容许他这样做——便推开门，走进堂阿隆索的房间。在继续往下讲之前，我必须先交代一下我主人昔日的经历，以及他那位可敬的夫人，好让读者更清楚地理解后文。

## III

===Original===
Don Alfonso Gutierrez de Cisniega belonged to an old family of Vejer, where he lived. He had been devoted at an early age to a naval career and, while still quite young, had distinguished himself in defending Havana against the English in 1748. He was afterwards engaged in the expedition which sailed from Cartagena against the Algerines in 1775, and was present at the attack upon Gibraltar under the Duke de Crillon in 1782. He subsequently joined the expedition to the Straits of Magellan in the corvette *Santa María de la Cabeza*, commanded by Don Antonio de Córdova, and fought in the glorious engagements between the Anglo-Spanish fleet and the French before Toulon in 1793, terminating his career of glory at the disastrous battle of Cape St. Vincent, where he commanded the *Mejicano*, one of the ships which were forced to surrender.

From that time my master, whose promotion had been slower than his laborious and varied career had merited, retired from active service. He suffered much in body from the wounds he had received on that fatal day, and more in mind from the blow of such a defeat. His wife nursed and tended him with devotion though not in silence, for abuse of the navy and of seamen of every degree were as common in her mouth as the names of the saints in that of a bigot.

Doña Francisca was an excellent woman, of exemplary conduct and noble birth, devout and God-fearing﻿—as all women were in those days, charitable and judicious, but with the most violent and diabolical temper I ever met with in the whole course of my life. Frankly I do not believe that this excessive irritability was natural to her, but the result and outcome of the worries in her life arising out of her husband’s much-hated profession; it must be confessed that she did not complain wholly without reason, and every day of her life Doña Francisca addressed her prayers to Heaven for the annihilation of every fleet in Europe. This worthy couple had but one child, a daughter﻿—the incomparable Rosita, of whom more anon.

===Chinese===
堂阿隆索·古铁雷斯·德·西斯涅加（Don Alonso Gutierrez de Cisniega）出身韦赫尔（Vejer）的一个古老家族，自己就住在当地。他早年便投身海军生涯，年纪尚轻就在1748年抗击英国人、保卫哈瓦那的战事中崭露头角。此后他参加了1775年自卡塔赫纳启航讨伐阿尔及尔人的远征，又于1782年在德·克里永公爵麾下亲历进攻直布罗陀之役。随后他登上由堂安东尼奥·德·科尔多瓦指挥的巡防舰*圣玛丽亚·德拉卡贝萨号*（Santa María de la Cabeza）远赴麦哲伦海峡，并在1793年英西联合舰队与法国人对阵土伦的几场光荣海战中冲锋陷阵，最后在圣文森特角那场灾难性的海战里给自己的光荣生涯画上了句号——那一仗他指挥的是*墨西卡诺号*（Mejicano），那是被迫投降的战舰之一。

自那时起，我的主人便退出了现役——他的升迁本已慢得与其辛劳多艰、丰富多样的资历不相称。在那个不幸的日子里身受的创伤，使他肉体上吃了不少苦；而那场惨败的打击，更使他精神上备受煎熬。他的妻子一片至诚地看护照料他，却并不默不作声——因为咒骂海军、骂遍大小水手官兵的话，从她嘴里说出来，就像圣徒名号从虔信教徒嘴里念出来一样稀松平常。

堂娜·弗朗西斯卡（Doña Francisca）是位极好的妇人，品行堪为楷模，出身名门，虔敬而畏神——当年的女子大抵如此——乐善好施，处事明断，只是那副脾气之暴烈可怕，是我一生中所见最甚的。坦白说，我不相信这种过度的火气是她与生俱来的天性，倒以为是丈夫那份职业惹来她切齿痛恨、由此在生活中积下的种种烦忧所结出的果；但也必须承认，她的抱怨并非全然无理，堂娜·弗朗西斯卡每天都向上天祈祷，求把欧洲各国的舰队统统消灭干净。这对可敬的夫妇膝下只有一个孩子，是个女儿——无与伦比的罗西塔（Rosita）——此是后话，容后再表。

===Original===
The veteran, however, pined sadly at Vejer, seeing his laurels covered with dust and gnawed to powder by the rats, and all his thoughts and most of his discourse, morning, noon, and night, were based on the absorbing theme that if Córdova, the commander of the Spanish fleet, had only given the word “Starboard” instead of “Port” the good ships *Mejicano*, *San José*, *San Nicolás* and *San Isidro* would never have fallen into the hands of the English, and Admiral Jervis would have been defeated. His wife, Marcial, and even I myself, exceeding the limits of my duties﻿—always assured him that there was no doubt of the fact, to see whether, if we acknowledged ourselves convinced, his vehemence would moderate﻿—but no; his mania on that point only died with him.

Eight years had passed since that disaster, and the intelligence that the whole united fleet was to fight a decisive battle with the English had now roused my master to a feverish enthusiasm which seemed to have renewed his youth. He pictured to himself the inevitable rout of his mortal enemies; and although his wife tried to dissuade him, as has been said, it was impossible to divert him from his wild purpose. To prove how obstinate his determination was it is enough to mention that he dared to oppose his wife’s strong will, though he avoided all discussion; and to give an adequate idea of all that his opposition implied I ought to mention that Don Alonso was afraid of no mortal thing or creature﻿—neither of the English, the French, nor the savages of Magellan, not of the angry sea, nor of the monsters of the deep, nor of the raging tempest, nor of anything in the earth or sky﻿—but only of his wife.

===Chinese===
然而这位老兵在韦赫尔郁郁寡欢，眼看着自己的桂冠蒙尘落灰、被老鼠啃成齑粉。他朝思暮想，念叨最多的话，从早到晚都围着那个令他痴迷的题目打转：倘若西班牙舰队司令科尔多瓦当时下达的口令是「右转舵」而不是「左转舵」，那么*墨西卡诺号*、*圣何塞号*（San José）、*圣尼古拉斯号*（San Nicolás）、*圣伊西德罗号*（San Isidro）这几艘好船就决不会落入英国人之手，杰维斯海军上将也会吃一场败仗。他的妻子、马西亚尔，甚至连我——尽管这已越出了我的本分——也总向他担保事情毫无疑问正是如此，想看看一旦我们自认信服，他的激愤之情会不会平息几分——然而不成；他对这一点的执念，至死方才罢休。

那场灾难至今已过去八年。如今，整个联合舰队即将与英国人决一死战的消息，把我主人激得如痴如狂，仿佛青春重来。他心里已经描绘出不共戴天的仇敌必不可免的溃败；虽然如前文所说，他的妻子百般劝阻，却根本无法让他打消那个疯狂的念头。要说明他的决心何等顽固，只消提一件事就够了：他竟敢违逆妻子那股强硬的意志——尽管他避免一切争辩；而要充分掂量这种违逆意味着什么，我还该交代一句：堂阿隆索天不怕地不怕——不怕英国人、法国人，不怕麦哲伦的蛮人，不怕怒海狂涛，不怕深渊里的巨怪，不怕肆虐的风暴，不怕天上地下任何东西——唯独怕他的妻子。

===Original===
The last person I must mention is Marcial the sailor, the object of Doña Francisca’s deepest aversion, though Don Alonso, under whom he had served, loved him as a brother.

Marcial﻿—no one knew his other name﻿—called by all the sailors “the Half a Man,” had been boatswain on various men-of-war for forty years. At the time when my story begins this maritime hero’s appearance was the strangest you can imagine. Picture to yourself an old man, tall rather than short, with a wooden leg, his left arm shortened to within a few inches of the elbow, minus an eye, and his face seamed with wounds in every direction﻿—slashed by the various arms of the enemy; with his skin tanned brown, like that of all seafaring men, and a voice so hoarse, hollow and slow, that it did not seem to belong to any rational human creature, and you have some idea of this eccentric personage. As I think of him I regret the narrow limits of my palette, for he deserves painting in more vivid colors and by a worthier artist. It was hard to say whether his appearance was most calculated to excite laughter or command respect﻿—both at once I think, and according to the point of view you might adopt.

===Chinese===
我必须交代的最后一个人是水手马西亚尔（Marcial）。他是堂娜·弗朗西斯卡最深恶痛绝的人，而堂阿隆索——马西亚尔当年就在他麾下当差——却待他亲如兄弟。

马西亚尔——没人知道他的姓氏——被水手们众口一词地唤作「半个人」（the Half a Man），在各艘战舰上做了四十年水手长。在我的故事开场的时候，这位海上英雄的相貌之奇特，是你所能想象的最怪模样。请你想象这样一位老者：身材偏高而不显矮，装着一条木腿，左臂截得只剩肘上几寸，少了只眼睛，脸上纵横交错布满伤疤——全是被敌人的各式兵器砍出来的；皮肤同所有海上人一样晒成棕褐色，嗓音嘶哑、空洞而迟缓，听上去简直不像是任何一个有理性的人嘴里发出来的。这样，你对这位奇人便有了个大概的印象。此刻想起他，我不禁遗憾自己调色板的局限，因为他实在值得用更鲜亮的色彩、由更高明的画师来描画。很难说他的尊容究竟是更容易引人发笑，还是更令人肃然起敬——我想两者兼而有之，全看你取的是哪个视角。

===Original===
His life might be said to be an epitome of the naval history of Spain during the last years of the past century and the beginning of this﻿—a history in whose pages the most splendid victories alternate with the most disastrous defeats. Marcial had served on board the *Conde de Regla*, the *San Joaquin*, the *Real Cárlos*, the *Trinidad* and other glorious but unfortunate vessels which, whether honorably defeated or perfidiously destroyed, carried with them to a watery grave the naval power of Spain. Besides the expeditions in which my master had taken part Marcial had been present at many others, such as that of Martinica, the action of Cape Finisterre, and before that the terrible battle close to Algeciras in July, 1801, and that off Cape Santa María on the 5th of October, 1804. He quitted the service at sixty-six years of age, not however for lack of spirit but because he was altogether “unmasted” and past fighting. On shore he and my master were the best of friends, and as the boatswain’s only daughter was married to one of the servants of the house, of which union a small child was the token, Marcial had made up his mind to cast anchor for good, like a hulk past service, and even succeeded in making himself believe that peace was a good thing. Only to see him you would have thought that the most difficult task that could be set to this grand relic of a hero was that of minding babies; but, as a matter of fact, Marcial had no other occupation in life than carrying and amusing his grandchild, putting it to sleep with his snatches of sea-songs, seasoned with an oath or two﻿—excusable under the circumstances.

But no sooner had he heard that the united fleets were making ready for a decisive battle than his moribund fires rose from their ashes, and he dreamed that he was calling up the crew in the forecastle of the *Santísima Trinidad*. Discovering in Don Alfonso similar symptoms of rejuvenescence, he confessed to him, and from that hour they spent the chief part of the day and night in discussing the news that arrived and their own feelings in the matter; “fighting their battles o’er again,” hazarding conjectures as to those to be fought in the immediate future, and talking over their daydreams like two ship’s boys indulging in secret visions of the shortest road to the title of Admiral.

In the course of these tête-à-tête meetings, which occasioned the greatest alarm to Doña Francisca, the plan was hatched for setting out to join the fleet and be present at the impending battle. I have already told the reader what my mistress’s opinion was and all the abuse she lavished on the insidious sailor; he knows too that Don Alonso persisted in his determination to carry out his rash purpose, accompanied by me, his trusty page, and I must now proceed to relate what occurred when Marcial himself appeared on the scene to take up the cudgels for war against the shameful status quo of Doña Francisca.

===Chinese===
他的一生，可以说正是上世纪末到本世纪初西班牙海军史的缩影——在那部历史的书页里，最辉煌的胜利与最惨痛的败绩交替更迭。马西亚尔先后在*雷格拉伯爵号*（Conde de Regla）、*圣华金号*（San Joaquin）、*皇家卡洛斯号*（Real Cárlos）、*圣特立尼达号*（Trinidad）以及其他许多光荣而不幸的战舰上服役；这些战舰，无论是不愧荣光的战败，还是遭人背信弃义的毁灭，都把西班牙的海上力量一同带进了水底的坟场。除了我主人参加过的那几次远征，马西亚尔还亲历过许多别的战事，比如马提尼克远征、菲尼斯特雷角之战，再往前还有1801年7月阿尔赫西拉斯近旁那场恶战，以及1804年10月5日圣玛丽亚角外海的那一战。他六十六岁那年退出海军，倒不是缺了胆气，而是因为他整个人已经「折尽桅杆」，再也打不动了。上了岸，他同我主人成了莫逆之交；水手长唯一的女儿嫁给了宅子里的一名仆人，这段姻缘结下的凭证是一个小娃娃，于是马西亚尔打定主意就此抛锚落定，像一艘超龄退役的废船，甚至当真让自己相信了太平是件好事。光看他的模样，你会以为摆在这位宏伟的英雄遗老面前最艰难的差事，莫过于看孩子；可事实上，马西亚尔平生别无他业，就是抱着外孙逗他开心，用零零碎碎的水手号子哄他入睡——号子里还夹着一两句咒骂，处在这种情形下倒也情有可原。

然而，一听说联合舰队正准备打一场决定性的大海战，他那行将熄灭的炉火便从灰烬里重新燃起，他梦见自己正在*圣特立尼达号*（Santísima Trinidad）的艏楼上召集全体水手。他发现堂阿隆索身上也有同样返老还童的征候，便向他吐露了衷肠；从那时起，两人便把一昼夜的大半时光用来议论传来的种种消息和各自心中的感想，「把当年的仗再打一遍」，对不久将临的战事妄加揣测，畅谈各自的白日梦，活像两个船上的小见习水手，偷偷憧憬着通往海军上将头衔的最短捷径。

就在这些令堂娜·弗朗西斯卡惊惶万状的促膝密谈中，一个计划酝酿成形：动身去投奔舰队，亲临那场迫在眉睫的大海战。我已经告诉过读者，我的主母对此是何等看法，她对那个居心叵测的水手泼了多少咒骂；读者也知道，堂阿隆索执意要由我这个忠心耿耿的小侍童陪着，去实现他那桩鲁莽的心愿。现在，我得接着讲一讲，当马西亚尔本人登场亮相、为「开战」挥棒助阵、痛击堂娜·弗朗西斯卡那可耻的「苟安现状」时，究竟发生了怎样的事。

## IV

===Original===
“Señor Marcial,” she began, with increased indignation, “if you choose to go to sea again and lose your other hand, you can go if you like; but my husband here, shall not.”

“Very good,” said the sailor who had seated himself on the edge of a chair, occupying no more space on it than was necessary to save himself from falling: “I will go alone. But the devil may take me if I can rest without looking on at the fun!”

Then he went on triumphantly: “We have fifteen ships and the French twenty smaller vessels. If they were all ours we should not want so many. Forty ships and plenty of brave hearts on board!”

Just as the spark creeps from one piece of timber to the next, the enthusiasm that fired Marcial’s one eye lighted up both my master’s, though dimmed by age. “But the *Señorito*” (Lord Nelson), added the sailor, “will bring up a great many men too. That is the sort of performance I enjoy: plenty of timbers to fire at, and plenty of gunpowder-smoke to warm the air when it is cold.”

===Chinese===
“马西亚尔先生，”她开口说道，怒气更盛，“你要是想再下海，把你另一只手也搭上，随你的便；可我这位丈夫，不许去。”

“很好，”水手说。他在椅子边上落了座，占的地方不多不少，只求不摔下去。“那我就一个人去。可要是连这场热闹都瞧不上，叫我安生不下来，就让魔鬼把我抓去好了！”

接着他得意洋洋地往下说：“咱们有十五艘战舰，法国人有二十艘小些的船。这些船要是全归了咱们，咱们还用不着这么多呢。四十艘船，船上有的是勇士！”

正像火星从一根木头窜上另一根木头，燃起马西亚尔那只独眼的热劲，也点亮了我主人那两只虽已老花的眼睛。“不过*小老爷*（Señorito，指纳尔逊勋爵〔Lord Nelson〕）也会带一大批人来，”水手补充道，“这才是我爱的戏码：有的是木头船好打，火药烟又多，天冷时正好暖暖空气。”

===Original===
I forgot to mention that Marcial, like most sailors, used a vocabulary of the most wonderful and mongrel character, for it seems to be a habit among seamen of every nation to disfigure their mother tongue to the verge of caricature. By examining the nautical terms used by sailors we perceive that most of them are corruptions of more usual terms, modified to suit their eager and hasty temperament trained by circumstances to abridge all the functions of existence and particularly speech. Hearing them talk it has sometimes occurred to me that sailors find the tongue an organ that they would gladly dispense with.

Marcial, for instance, turned verbs into nouns and nouns into verbs without consulting the authorities. He applied nautical terms to every action and movement, and identified the ideas of a man and a ship, fancying that there was some analogy between their limbs and parts. He would say in speaking of the loss of his eye that his larboard porthole was closed, and explained the amputation of his arm by saying that he had been left minus his starboard cat-head. His heart he called his courage-hold and his stomach his breadbasket. These terms sailors at any rate could understand; but he had others, the offspring of his own inventive genius of which he alone understood the meaning or could appreciate the force. He had words of his own coining for doubting a statement, for feeling sad; getting drunk he always called “putting on your coat” among a number of other fantastical idioms; and the derivation of this particular phrase will never occur to my readers without my explaining to them that the English sailors had acquired among the Spaniards the nickname of “Greatcoats,” so that when he called getting drunk “putting your coat on” a recondite allusion was implied to the favorite vice of the enemy. He had the most extraordinary nicknames for foreign admirals; Nelson he called the *Señorito*, implying a certain amount of respect for him; Collingwood was *Tio Calambre*, (Uncle Cramp) which he believed to be an equivalent for the English name; Jervis he called—as the English did too—The Old Fox; Calder was known as *Tio Perol* (Uncle Boiler) from an association of the name Calder with *caldera*, a kettle, and by an entirely different process he dubbed Villeneuve, the Admiral of the united fleets, with the name of “Monsieur Corneta,” borrowed from some play he had once seen acted at Madrid. In fact, when reporting the conversations I can recall, I must perforce translate his wonderful phraseology into more ordinary language, to avoid going into long and tiresome explanations.

===Chinese===
我忘了交代，马西亚尔同大多数水手一样，说起话来用一套妙不可言、驳杂不堪的语汇，因为把本国语言糟蹋到近乎漫画的地步，似乎是世界各国水手的通病。细察水手们用的航海行话，便会发现其中大半是寻常字眼讹变来的，为的是迁就他们那急切毛躁的性子——那性子是境况磨出来的，凡事但求节略，说话尤其如此。听他们交谈时，我有时不免想：在水手们眼里，舌头这个器官，他们巴不得省了才好。

就拿马西亚尔来说，他常把动词当名词使、名词当动词使，全不请示什么权威。各种行动动作，他一概套用航海术语，把人和船看成一体，认定人的四肢五脏同船上的部件之间有某种对应。说到那只丢掉的眼睛，他说自己的左舷舷窗关死了；讲到那条截去的胳膊，他解释说自己「少了右舷的吊锚架」。心脏，他叫「勇气舱」；胃口，他叫「面包篮」。这些说法，水手们好歹还听得懂；可他还有些词，是他自家发明天才的产儿，只有他一个人明白意思、领略得了劲道。怀疑人家一句话，他有自铸的词；心里发闷，他也有自铸的词；喝醉酒，他老说成「披上大氅」——此外还有一串光怪陆离的说法。这一句的来历，读者们若非我点破，是无论如何猜不着的：英国水手在西班牙人中间得了个「大氅佬」（Greatcoats）的诨名，所以他把喝醉叫作「披上大氅」，里头便暗藏一层冷僻的典故，指向敌人最钟爱的那个恶习。他给外国海军将领起的诨名更是千奇百怪：纳尔逊（Nelson），他叫他*小老爷*，透着几分敬意；科林伍德（Collingwood）是*抽筋大叔*（Tio Calambre），他深信这跟英国名字正好对得上；杰维斯，他跟英国人一样，叫「老狐狸」；卡尔德（Calder）人称*锅炉大叔*（Tio Perol），因为他把卡尔德这名字跟*caldera*（大锅）联想到了一处；而给联合舰队统帅维尔纳夫（Villeneuve）安上「科尔内塔先生」（Monsieur Corneta）这个名号，路数又全然不同，是从他当年在马德里看的一出戏里借来的。总之，复述这些我还记得的谈话时，我只好把他的奇言妙语译成寻常话，免得啰啰嗦嗦解释个没完。

===Original===
To proceed, Doña Francisca, devoutly crossing herself, answered angrily:

“Forty ships! Good Heavens! it is tempting Providence; and there will be at least forty thousand guns for the enemies to kill each other.”

“Ah! but Monsieur Corneta keeps the courage-hold well filled!” exclaimed Marcial, striking his breast. “We shall laugh at the Greatcoats this time. It will not be Cape St. Vincent over again.”

“And you must not forget,” added my master eagerly recurring to his favorite hobby, “that if Admiral Córdova had only ordered the *San José* and the *Mejicano* to tack to port, Captain Jervis would not now be rejoicing in the title of Earl St. Vincent. Of that you may be very certain, and I have ample evidence to show that if we had gone to port the day would have been ours.”

“Ours!” exclaimed Doña Francisca scornfully. “As if you could have done more. To hear these fire-eaters it would seem as if they wanted to conquer the world, and as to going to sea—it appears that their shoulders are not broad enough to bear the blows of the English.”

===Chinese===
接下去说。堂娜·弗朗西斯卡虔敬地画了个十字，怒气冲冲地答道：

“四十艘船！老天爷！这是在招惹天罚；给两边仇人互相杀伐用的炮，少说也有四万尊。”

“啊！可是科尔内塔先生把勇气舱填得满满的！”马西亚尔嚷道，一拳捶在自己胸口。“这一回咱们要笑看大氅佬们栽跟头了。圣文森特角那一幕是不会重演的。”

“还有一条你可别忘了，”我主人接口道，急不可待地又捡起他那桩百谈不厌的心爱话题，“倘若科尔多瓦海军上将当时只消下令让*圣何塞号*和*墨西卡诺号*向左转舵，杰维斯舰长如今也就不会得意洋洋地顶着圣文森特伯爵的封号乐和了。这一点你尽可笃信无疑，我有充分的证据表明：当时咱们若是转到了左舷，那一天便全归咱们了。”

“咱们！”堂娜·弗朗西斯卡轻蔑地嚷道，“说得好像你们还能有多大作为似的。听这些好勇斗狠之徒的口气，仿佛要把全世界都征服了才罢休；至于下海么——瞧那副肩膀，怕还不够宽，接不下英国人的拳脚呢。”

===Original===
“No,” said Marcial resolutely and clenching his fist defiantly. “If it were not for their cunning and knavery …! We got out against them with a bold front, defying them like men, with our flag hoisted and clean hands. The English never sail wide, they always steal up and surprise us, choosing heavy seas and stormy weather. That is how it was at the Straits, when we were made to pay so dearly. We were sailing on quite confidingly, for no one expected to be trapped even by a heretic dog of a Moor, much less by an Englishman who does the polite thing in a Christian fashion.—But no, an enemy who sneaks up to fight is not a Christian—he is a highwayman. Well now, just fancy, señora,” and he turned to Doña Francisca to engage her attention and goodwill, “we were going out of Cádiz to help the French fleet which was driven into Algeciras by the English.—It is four years ago now, and to this day it makes me so angry that my blood boils as I think of it. I was on board the *Real Cárlos*, 112 guns, commanded by Ezguerra, and we had with us the *San Hermenegildo*, 112 guns too, the *San Fernando*, the *Argonauta*, the *San Agustín*, and the frigate *Sabina*. We were joined by the French squadron of four men-of-war, three frigates and a brigantine, and all sailed out of Algeciras for Cádiz at twelve o’clock at noon; and as the wind was slack when night fell we were close under Punta Carnero. The night was blacker than a barrel of pitch, but the weather was fine so we could hold on our way in spite of the darkness. Most of the crew were asleep; I remember, I was sitting in the fo’castle talking to the mate, Pepe Débora, who was telling me all the dog’s tricks his mother-in-law had played him, and alongside we could see the lights of the *San Hermenegildo*, which was sailing at a gunshot to starboard. The other ships were ahead of us. For the very last thing we any of us thought of was that the Greatcoats had slipped out of Gibraltar and were giving chase—and how the devil should we, when they had doused all their lights and were stealing up to us without our guessing it? Suddenly, for all that the night was so dark, I fancied I saw something—I always had a port-light like a lynx—I fancied a ship was standing between us and the *San Hermenegildo*, which was sailing at a gunshot to starboard. ‘José Débora,’ says I, ‘either I saw a ghost or there is an Englishman to starboard?’ José Débora looks himself, and then he says: ‘May the mainmast go by the board,’ says he, ‘if there is e’er a ship to starboard but the *San Hermenegildo*.’ ‘Well,’ says I, ‘whether or no I am going to tell the officer of the watch.’

===Chinese===
“不，”马西亚尔斩钉截铁地说，攥紧拳头，一副不服的架势。“要不是他们那帮子奸猾耍赖……！我们出海迎敌，昂首挺胸，像男子汉一样向他们叫阵，旗帜高扬，两手清白。英国佬从来不走大路，总爱偷偷摸上来打闷棍，还专拣浪大流急、风暴天候下手。海峡那一仗就是这么着，我们才吃了那么大的亏。我们一路航行，心里毫无戒备，因为谁也想不到会遭人暗算——哪怕是摩尔异教徒那样的狗东西设的套，更别提英国人了，人家可是按基督徒的规矩行事、客客气气的。——可不，偷偷摸上前来打仗的敌人不是基督徒——是拦路抢劫的强盗。您想想看，夫人，”他转向堂娜·弗朗西斯卡，好笼住她的注意和好感，“我们那次驶出加的斯，是去救援被英国人逼进阿尔赫西拉斯的法国舰队。——到如今四年过去了，一想起这事我还气得血往头上涌。我在*皇家卡洛斯号*上，一百一十二门炮，舰长是埃斯格拉（Ezguerra），同行的还有*圣埃尔梅内希尔多号*（San Hermenegildo），也是一百一十二门炮，再有*圣费尔南多号*（San Fernando）、*阿尔戈纳塔号*（Argonauta）、*圣阿古斯丁号*（San Agustín），外加*萨维娜号*（Sabina）巡防舰。法国分舰队来同我们会合——四艘战舰、三艘巡防舰、一艘双桅横帆船——正午十二点，全体从阿尔赫西拉斯起航开往加的斯；入夜风小了，我们就紧挨着卡内罗角（Punta Carnero）。夜黑得赛过一桶柏油，天气倒好，因此尽管天黑，我们还能照常赶路。船员大半都睡了；我记得我当时坐在艏楼里，同大副佩佩·德博拉（Pepe Débora）聊天，他正跟我数落他丈母娘使的那些狗肚肠伎俩；旁边望得见*圣埃尔梅内希尔多号*的灯火，它在我们右舷一炮之遥的地方行驶。其余的船都在我们前头。我们这些人谁做梦也想不到，大氅佬们已经溜出直布罗陀追上来了——我们又哪里想得到呢？他们把灯火全灭了，悄悄掩上来，我们竟毫无觉察。忽然，夜尽管那么黑，我觉出自己瞧见了什么——我这盏「舷灯」向来亮得赛山猫——我觉得有条船正插在我们同*圣埃尔梅内希尔多号*之间——它就在右舷一炮之遥处行驶。‘何塞·德博拉，’我说，‘要么我见了鬼，要么右舷有个英国人？’何塞·德博拉自己瞧了瞧，然后说：‘叫主桅倒进海里去，’他说，‘右舷要真有第二条船，不叫*圣埃尔梅内希尔多号*，我天打雷劈。’‘好吧，’我说，‘不管有没有，我这就去报告值更官。’

===Original===
“Well hardly were the words out of my mouth when, rub-a-dub! we heard the tune of a whole broadside that came rattling against our ribs. The crew were on deck in a minute, and each man at his post. That was a rumpus, señora! I wish you could have been there, just to have an idea of how these things are managed. We were all swearing like demons and at the same time praying the Lord to give us a gun at the end of every finger to fight them with. Ezguerra gave the word to return their broadside.—Thunder and lightning! They fired again, and in a minute or two we responded. But in the midst of all the noise and confusion we discovered that with their first broadside they had sent one of those infernal combustibles (but he called it ‘comestibles’) on board which fall on the deck as if it were raining fire. When we saw our ship was burning we fought like madmen and fired off broadside after broadside. Ah! Doña Francisca, it was hot work I can tell you!—Then our captain took us alongside of the enemy’s ship that we might board her. I wish you could have seen it! I was in my glory then; in an instant we had our axes and boarding-pikes out, the enemy was coming down upon us and my heart jumped for joy to see it, for this was the quickest way of settling accounts. On we go, right into her!—Day was just beginning to dawn, the yards were touching, and the boarding parties ready at the gangways when we heard Spanish oaths on board the foe. We all stood dumb with horror, for we found that the ship we had been fighting with was the *San Hermenegildo* herself.”

===Chinese===
“唔，我话音未落，咚咚咚！就听得一整舷齐射奏出的调子，噼里啪啦全砸在我们肋条上。船员一眨眼全上了甲板，各就各位。那阵乱子才叫可观呢，夫人！您真该在场，见识见识这种事是怎么个章法。我们个个恶魔似的咒骂，同时又祈求天主给咱们每根指头尖上安一尊炮，好拿来打他们。埃斯格拉下令回敬他们一舷齐射。——雷公电母！他们又打了，一两分钟后我们也回上了。可就在那一团嘈杂混乱之中，我们发现他们头一舷炮里，给我们船上送来了那种要命的燃烧弹（不过他管这玩意儿叫「吃食弹」）——落在甲板上，就像天上下火雨。眼见自己的船着了火，我们疯子一般苦斗，一舷接一舷地轰。啊！堂娜·弗朗西斯卡，那可是火热朝天的一场硬仗，我告诉您！——后来我们舰长把船开到敌舰旁边，好跳帮夺船。您真该瞧瞧那个场面！那时节我威风极了；眨眼间斧头、接舷长矛全抄在手里，敌人正朝我们扑过来，我瞧着心花怒放，因为这是清账最快的法子。我们上，直插过去！——天正蒙蒙亮，两船的帆桁都快搭上了，跳帮队已在舷梯口列队就位，忽听得敌船上骂的竟是西班牙脏话。我们全都吓呆了，僵在那儿：原来跟我们拼死拼活打了一夜的那条船，竟是我们自家的*圣埃尔梅内希尔多号*。”

===Original===
“That was a pretty state of things,” said Doña Francisca roused to some interest in the narrative. “And how had you been such asses—with not a pin to choose between you?”

“I will tell you. We had no time for explanations then. The flames on our ship went over to the *San Hermenegildo* and then, Blessed Virgin! what a scene of confusion. ‘To the boats!’ was the cry. The fire caught the *Santa Bárbara* and her ladyship blew up with loud explosion.—We were all swearing, shouting, blaspheming God and the Virgin and all the Saints, for that seems the only way to avoid choking when you are primed to fight, up to the very muzzle. …”

“Merciful Heavens how shocking!” cried my mistress. “And you escaped?”

“Forty of us got off in the launch and six or seven in the gig, these took up the second officer of the *San Hermenegildo*. José Débora clung to a piece of plank and came to shore at Morocco, more dead than alive.”

===Chinese===
“这可真出息，”堂娜·弗朗西斯卡说，故事让她生出几分兴致来。“你们怎么会蠢成这样——彼此之间连根针的差别都认不出？”

“我告诉您。当时哪有工夫解释。我们船上的火窜上了*圣埃尔梅内希尔多号*，那时节，蒙福的圣母啊！乱成了一锅粥。四下里只听得喊：‘快下小艇！’火苗烧着了「圣巴巴拉」火药舱（Santa Bárbara），这位夫人娘娘轰隆一声巨响炸上了天。——我们人人都在咒骂、叫喊、亵渎天主、圣母和全体圣徒，因为一个人憋足了劲要打仗、火药一直填到炮口的时候，这似乎是免得噎死的不二法门。……”

“老天爷慈悲！多吓人呐！”我的女主人嚷道，“你们逃出来了？”

“我们四十个人上了大艇，六七个人上了小艇，小艇还捞起了*圣埃尔梅内希尔多号*的二副。何塞·德博拉抱着一块木板，半死不活地漂到了摩洛哥上岸。”

===Original===
“And the rest?”

“The rest—the sea was wide enough to hold them all. Two thousand men went down to Davy Jones that day, and among them our captain, Ezguerra, and Emparán, the captain of the other ship.”

“Lord have mercy on them!” ejaculated Doña Francisca. “Though God knows! they were but ill-employed to be snatched away to judgment. If they had stayed quietly at home, as God requires. …”

“The cause of that disaster,” said Don Alonso, who delighted in getting his wife to listen to these dramatic narratives, “was this: The English emboldened by the darkness arranged that the *Superb*, the lightest of their vessels, should extinguish her lights and slip through between our two finest ships. Having done this, she fired both her broadsides and then put about as quickly as possible to escape the struggle that ensued. The two men-of-war, finding themselves unexpectedly attacked, returned fire and thus went on battering each other till dawn, when, just as they were about to board, they recognized each other and the end came as Marcial has told you in detail.”

===Chinese===
“其余的人呢？”

“其余的——海够宽敞，把他们全收下了。那一天，两千条汉子沉进了戴维·琼斯（Davy Jones）的海底柜子，里头就有我们的舰长埃斯格拉，还有对面那条船的舰长恩帕兰（Emparán）。”

“愿主可怜他们吧！”堂娜·弗朗西斯卡脱口喊道。“虽说天知道！他们干的那些营生本就算不得好差事，这样冷不丁被抓去听候审判，也不足惜。要是他们像天主要求的那样，安安分分待在家里。……”

“那场大祸的根由，”堂阿隆索说——他最喜欢让妻子听这些跌宕起伏的故事——“是这样的：英国人仗着天黑，安排他们船身最轻的*苏佩布号*（Superb）熄灭灯火，从我们两条最精良的战舰之间一钻而过。得手之后，它两舷齐发，随即尽快掉头逃走，避开随后而来的混战。那两条战舰冷不防挨了打，便朝对方还击，就这么你轰我、我轰你，一直打到天亮；正当要跳帮的时候，彼此认出了对方，结局如何，马西亚尔已经详详细细讲给您听了。”

===Original===
“Ah! and they played the game well,” cried the lady. “It was well done though it was a mean trick!”

“What would you have?” added Marcial. “I never loved them much; but since that night! … If *they* are in Heaven I do not want ever to go there. Sooner would I be damned to all eternity!”

“Well—and then the taking of the four frigates which were coming from Rio de la Plata?” asked Don Alfonso, to incite the old sailor to go on with his stories.

===Chinese===
“啊！这一手玩得漂亮，”夫人嚷道，“干得真漂亮——虽说是桩下三滥的伎俩！”

“您还能指望什么呢？”马西亚尔接口道，“我从来就没喜欢过他们；可自打那一夜之后！……要是*他们*能进天堂，我情愿永远别上那儿去。宁可永生永世下地狱！”

“后来呢——还有劫夺那四条从拉普拉塔河（Rio de la Plata）来的巡防舰又是怎么回事？”堂阿隆索问道，好撩得老水手把故事讲下去。

===Original===
“Aye—I was at that too,” said Marcial. “And that was where I left my leg. That time too they took us unawares, and as it was in time of peace we were sailing on quietly enough, only counting the hours till we should be in port, when suddenly—I will tell you exactly how it all happened, Doña Francisca, that you may just understand the ways of those people. After the engagement at the Straits I embarked on board the *Fama* for Montevideo, and we had been out there a long time when the Admiral of the squadron received orders to convoy treasure from Lima and Buenos Aires to Spain. The voyage was a good one and we had no mishaps but a few slight cases of fever which only killed off a few of our men. Our freight was heavy—gold belonging to the king and to private persons, and we also had on board what we called the ‘wages chest’—savings off the pay of the troops serving in America. Altogether, if I am not much mistaken, a matter of fifty millions or so of pesos, as if it were a mere nothing; and besides that, wolf-hides, vicuña wool, cascarilla, pigs of tin and copper, and cabinet woods. Well, sir, after sailing for fifty days we sighted land on the 5th of October, and reckoned on getting into Cádiz the next day when, bearing down from the northeast, what should we see but four frigates. Although, as I said, it was in time of peace, and though our captain, Don Miguel de Zapiain, did not seem to have any suspicion of evil, I—being an old sea-dog—called Débora and said to him that there was powder in the air, I could smell it. Well, when the English frigates were pretty near, we cleared the decks for action; the *Fama* went forward and we were soon within a cable’s length of one of the English ships which lay to windward.

===Chinese===
“可不是——那一回我也在场，”马西亚尔说，“我那条腿就撂在那儿了。那一回也是他们打我们个措手不及，何况当时是太平年月，我们一路航行得安安稳稳，只掰着钟点算还有几个时辰进港，谁知突然——我原原本本讲给您听，堂娜·弗朗西斯卡，好叫您明白明白那帮人的行事路数。海峡那一仗之后，我上了*法马号*（Fama）开往蒙得维的亚（Montevideo），在那边待了很久，后来分舰队的海军上将奉命护航，把利马（Lima）和布宜诺斯艾利斯（Buenos Aires）的一批财宝运送回西班牙。航程顺当，没出什么岔子，只有几起不重的热病，也只不过送掉我们几个弟兄。我们载的货沉得很——有国王的金子，也有私人的金子；船上还装着我们叫作‘军饷箱’的东西——是在美洲服役的部队从饷银里省下的积蓄。统共算来，要是我没大记错，总有五千万上下的比索，说得跟仨瓜俩枣似的；除此以外还有狼皮、骆马毛、金鸡纳树皮、锡锭铜锭，还有各色细木良材。就这样，先生，航行了五十天，十月五日我们望见了陆地，盘算着第二天就进加的斯，忽见从东北方向迎面压下来四条巡防舰。尽管如我所说，当时正逢太平，我们舰长堂米格尔·德·萨皮亚因（Don Miguel de Zapiain）看着也没有丝毫疑心，可我——到底是条老海狗——把德博拉叫过来，跟他说空中有火药味，我闻得出来。等到英国巡防舰逼得挺近，我们清理甲板准备战斗；*法马号*迎上前去，不多时就跟上风头的一条英国船相距不到一链了。

===Original===
“The English captain hailed us through his speaking-trumpet and told us—there is nothing like plain-speaking—told us to prepare to defend ourselves, as he was going to attack. He asked a string of questions, but all he got out of us was that we should not take the trouble to answer him. Meanwhile the other three frigates had come up and had formed in such order that each Englishman had a Spaniard to the leeward of him.”

“They could not have taken up a better position,” said my master.

===Chinese===
“英国舰长隔着传声筒朝我们喊话，告诉我们——还是直来直去的好——让我们准备自卫，说他就要进攻了。他一长串问题问下来，可我们回他的只有一句：请他不必费心等我们答话。与此同时，另外三条巡防舰也赶了上来，摆开的阵势恰是每个英国人的下风头都压着一个西班牙人。”

“这阵势占得不能再好了，”我主人说。

===Original===
“So say I,” replied Marcial. “The commander of our squadron, Don José Bustamante, was not very prompt; if I had been in his shoes. … Well, señor, the English commodore sent a little whippersnapper officer, in a swallowtail coat, on board the *Medea*, who wasted no time in trifling but said at once that though war had not been declared, the commodore had orders to take us. That is what it is to be English! Well, we engaged at once; our frigate received the first broadside in her port quarter; we politely returned the salute, and the cannonade was brisk on both sides—the long and the short of it is that we could do nothing with the heretics, for the devil was on their side; they set fire to the *Santa Bárbara* which blew up with a roar, and we were all so crushed by this and felt so cowed—not for want of courage, señor, but what they call demoralized—well, from the first we knew we were lost. There were more holes in our ship’s sails than in an old cloak; our rigging was damaged, we had five feet of water in the hold, our mizzenmast was split, we had three shots in the side only just above the water line and many dead and wounded. Notwithstanding all this we went on, give and take, with the English, but when we saw that the *Medea* and the *Clara* were unable to fight any longer and struck their colors we made all sail and retired, defending ourselves as best we could. The cursed Englishman gave chase, and as her sails were in better order than ours we could not escape and we had nothing for it but to haul our colors down at about three in the afternoon, when a great many men had been killed and I myself was lying half-dead on the deck, for a ball had gone out of its way to take my leg off. Those d——d wretches carried us off to England, not as prisoners, but as *détenus*; however, with despatches on one side and despatches on the other, from London to Madrid and back again, the end of it was that they stuck for want of money; and, so far as I was concerned, another leg might have grown by the time the King of Spain sent them such a trifle as those five millions of pesos.”

===Chinese===
“我也这么说，”马西亚尔答道，“我们分舰队的指挥官堂何塞·布斯塔曼特（Don José Bustamante）动作不够麻利；我要是处在他的位子上。……唉，先生，英国分舰队司令派了个穿燕尾服的毛头小子军官到*梅迪亚号*（Medea）上来，人家可不耽搁工夫扯闲篇，当场就说：仗虽还没宣，司令奉命把我们拿下。这就是英国人的做派！于是我们当即交上了火；我们巡防舰的左后舷挨了头一排齐射；我们客客气气地回敬了这份敬礼，两边炮打得都欢——长话短说，跟那些异教徒我们是没有辙，魔鬼站在他们那边；他们打着了「圣巴巴拉」火药舱，轰隆一声炸了，这一下我们全都懵了，全都泄了气——不是缺胆量，先生，是人家说的那个「士气崩了」——唉，打头一炮起我们就知道完了。我们船帆上的窟窿比老斗篷上的还多；索具打烂了，舱底积了五英尺深的水，后桅裂了，舷侧水线上头不远处挨了三发炮弹，死伤枕藉。尽管这样，我们还是同英国人你一炮我一炮地周旋着；可等我们看见*梅迪亚号*和*克拉拉号*（Clara）再打不动了、降了旗，我们便张满帆退出战斗，尽力自卫。那可咒的英国船穷追不舍，它的帆具比我们齐整，我们逃不脱，到下午三点上下，除了降旗再没有别的路——那时节已经死了好多好多人，我自己半死不活地躺在甲板上，因为一发炮弹宁可绕了道，也要把我的腿带走。那帮该死的恶棍把我们掳到了英国，不算俘虏，算作*被扣留者*（détenus）；可是这边公文来、那边公文去，从伦敦到马德里、再从马德里回伦敦，末了的结局是：他们为了等钱僵住了；至于我呢——等西班牙国王把那五百万比索的区区小数给他们送去时，另一条腿只怕都重新长出来了。”

===Original===
“Poor man!—and it was then you lost your leg?” asked Doña Francisca compassionately.

“Yes, señora, the English, knowing that I was no dancer, thought one was as much as I could want. In return they took good care of me. I was six months in a town they called ‘Plinmuf’ (Plymouth) lying in my bunk with my paw tied up and a passport for the next world in my pocket.—However, God A’mighty did not mean that I should make a hole in the water so soon; an English doctor made me this wooden leg, which is better than the other now, for the other aches with that d——d rheumatism and this one, thank God, never aches even when it is hit by a round of small shot. As to toughness, I believe it would stand anything, though, to be sure, I have never since faced English fire to test it.”

“You are a brave fellow,” said my mistress. “Please God you may not lose the other. But those who seek danger. …”

===Chinese===
“可怜的人！——你的腿就是那时候丢的？”堂娜·弗朗西斯卡动了恻隐之心，问道。

“是的，夫人。英国人心知我跳舞原是不成的，便想两条腿我也嫌多，一条尽够使了。作为报答，他们倒把我照料得好好的。我在一个他们叫作‘普林穆夫’（Plinmuf，即普利茅斯〔Plymouth〕）的镇子上躺了六个月，躺在我的吊铺上，爪子扎着绷带，兜里揣着一张去往阴世的护照。——不过，全能的上帝没打算叫我这么早就到水里去凿个洞；一位英国医生给我做了这条木腿，如今倒比那条真腿强：真腿害着那该死的风湿病，这条呢，托上帝的福，哪怕挨上一发小霰弹也从不知道疼。论结实，我看它什么都经得住——不过说实话，打那以后我再没顶着英国人的炮火试过它。”

“你是个勇敢的汉子，”我的女主人说，“愿上帝保佑你别把另一条也丢了。不过，自寻危险的人呐。……”

===Original===
And so, Marcial’s story being ended, the dispute broke out anew as to whether or no my master should set out to join the squadron. Doña Francisca persisted in her negative, and Don Alonso, who in his wife’s presence was as meek as a lamb, sought pretexts and brought forward every kind of reason to convince her.

“Well we shall go to look on, wife—simply and merely to look on,” said the hero in a tone of entreaty.

“Let us have done with sightseeing,” answered his wife. “A pretty pair of lookers-on you two would make!”

“The united squadrons,” added Marcial, “will remain in Cádiz—and they will try to force the entrance.”

===Chinese===
马西亚尔的故事讲完了，于是那场争论又重新开了火：我主人到底该不该动身去投舰队。堂娜·弗朗西斯卡咬死一个「不」字不改口，堂阿隆索——他在妻子面前温顺得像只羔羊——则找种种托词，搬出各色道理来说服她。

“哎，我们就去看看，老婆——就只是去看看，”这位英雄用恳求的声调说。

“看什么看，”他妻子答道，“就你们俩，当看客倒是绝配的一对！”

“联合舰队，”马西亚尔补充道，“会留在加的斯——英国人会想法子强行闯进湾口来。”

===Original===
“Well then,” said my mistress, “you can see the whole performance from within the walls of Cádiz, but as for going out in the ships—I say no, and I mean no, Alonso. During forty years of married life you have never seen me angry (he saw it every day)—but if you join the squadron I swear to you … remember, Paquita lives only for you!”

===Chinese===
“那么，”我的女主人说，“这出戏你站在加的斯城墙里头就看得全乎了；至于上船出海——我说不行，就是不行，阿隆索。四十年的夫妻，你没见我动过火（他可是天天见）——可你要是参加了舰队，我对天发誓……记住，帕基塔活着全是为了你！”

===Original===
“Wife, wife—” cried my master much disturbed: “Do you mean I am to die without having had that satisfaction?”

“A nice sort of satisfaction truly! to look on at mad men killing each other! If the King of Spain would only listen to me, I would pack off these English and say to them: ‘My beloved subjects were not made to amuse you. Set to and fight each other, if you want to fight.’ What do you say to that?—I, simpleton as I am, know very well what is in the wind, and that is that the first Consul—Emperor—Sultan—whatever you call him—wants to settle the English, and as he has no men brave enough for the job he has imposed upon our good King and persuaded him to lend him his; and the truth is he is sickening us with his everlasting sea-fights. Will you just tell me what is Spain to gain in all this? Why is Spain to submit to being cannonaded day after day for nothing at all? Before all that rascally business Marcial has told us of what harm had the English ever done us?—Ah, if they would only listen to me! Master Bonaparte might fight by himself, for I would not fight for him!”

===Chinese===
“老婆，老婆——”我主人喊道，心烦意乱，“你是要叫我到死也没那份福可享么？”

“那份福也真叫福！眼睁睁看着一群疯子互相残杀！西班牙国王要是肯听我的，我就把这些英国人打发走，跟他们说：‘我亲爱的臣民不是生下来给你们解闷的。要打，你们自家打自家去。’您觉得这话怎么样？——我这么个傻婆子，风里也闻得出味儿来：那位第一执政——皇帝——苏丹——随你们叫他什么——想收拾英国人，自家又没有够胆的人手，就哄骗咱们的好国王，劝他把手下的人借给他；说实在的，他那些没完没了的海战早把我们腻味透了。你们倒说说，西班牙在这场买卖里能捞着什么？凭什么西班牙要日复一日地白白挨炮轰？马西亚尔方才讲的那桩腌臜买卖之前，英国人几时害过咱们？——唉，他们要是肯听我的！波拿巴（Bonaparte）老爷子要打自个儿打去，我才不替他打！”

===Original===
“It is quite true,” replied my master, “that our alliance with France is doing us much damage, for all the advantages accrue to our ally, while all the disasters are on our side.”

“Well, then, you utter simpletons, why do you encourage the poor creatures to fight in this war?”

===Chinese===
“确实不假，”我主人答道，“咱们同法国的结盟正大害其害，因为好处全归盟友进账，灾祸却全落在咱们这一边。”

“那么，你们这帮彻头彻尾的傻子，还撺掇那帮可怜虫打这场仗做什么？”

===Original===
“The honor of the nation is at stake,” replied Don Alonso, “and after having once joined the dance it would be a disgrace to back out of it. Last month, when I was at Cádiz, at my cousin’s daughter’s christening, Churruca said to me: ‘This French alliance and that villainous treaty of San Ildefonso, which the astuteness of Bonaparte and the weakness of our government made a mere question of subsidies, will be the ruin of us and the ruin of our fleet if God does not come to the rescue, and afterwards will be the ruin of the colonies too and of Spanish trade with America. But we must go on now all the same. …’”

===Chinese===
“国家的荣誉攸关于此，”堂阿隆索答道，“既已下场跳了这场舞，中途抽身便是奇耻大辱。上个月我在加的斯，参加一个表亲的女儿的洗礼，丘鲁卡对我说：‘同法国的这一联盟，还有圣伊尔德丰索（San Ildefonso）那纸卑劣条约——波拿巴的狡诈加上咱们政府的软弱，把它弄成了一桩单纯的津贴买卖——要是上帝不出手搭救，就会毁了咱们，毁了咱们的舰队，往后还要毁了殖民地，毁了西班牙同美洲的贸易。可如今咱们也只能硬着头皮走下去。……’”

===Original===
“Well,” said Doña Francisca, “what I say is that the Prince of Peace is interfering in things he does not understand. There you see what a man without learning is! My brother the archdeacon, who is on Prince Ferdinand’s side, says that Godoy is a thoroughly commonplace soul, that he has studied neither Latin nor theology and that all he knows is how to play the guitar and twenty ways of dancing a gavotte. They made him prime minister for his good looks, as it would seem. That is the way we do things in Spain! And then we hear of starvation and want—everything is so dear—yellow fever breaking out in Andalusia.—This is a pretty state of things, sir—yes, and the fault is yours; yours,” she went on, raising her voice and turning purple. “Yes, señor, yours, who offend God by killing so many people—and if you would go to church and tell your beads instead of wanting to go in those diabolical ships of war, the devil would not find time to trot round Spain so nimbly, playing the mischief with us all.”

===Chinese===
“依我看呐，”堂娜·弗朗西斯卡说，“和平亲王净在自己不懂的事情里插手。你们瞧瞧，一个没有学问的人就是这副样子！我那位做总铎（archdeacon）的哥哥——他是费尔南多王子（Prince Ferdinand）一边的——说戈多伊（Godoy）是个再平庸不过的灵魂，拉丁文没学过，神学也没学过，会的只是弹吉他，再加二十种跳加沃特舞的花样。照这样儿看，人家是凭一副好相貌当上的首相。咱们西班牙办事就是这个路数！接着就听说闹饥荒、闹匮乏——样样东西都贵——黄热病又在安达卢西亚闹起来。——好一派光景，先生——是的，这都是你们的不是；你们的，”她接着说，嗓门越提越高，脸涨得发紫，“是的，先生，你们的不是——你们杀了这么多人，冒犯了上帝——你们要是肯进教堂数念珠，而不是一心想上那些恶魔似的战舰，魔鬼也就腾不出工夫在西班牙满地撒欢儿，这么轻快地祸害我们大家了。”

===Original===
“But you shall come to Cádiz too,” said Don Alonso, hoping to light some spark of enthusiasm in his wife’s heart; “you shall go to Flora’s house, and from the balcony you will be able to see the fight quite comfortably, and the smoke and the flames and the flags.—It is a beautiful sight!”

“Thank you very much—but I should drop dead with fright. Here we shall be quiet; those who seek danger may go there.”

===Chinese===
“可你也一齐来加的斯呀，”堂阿隆索说，指望在妻子心里点起一星半点热乎劲儿，“你住到弗洛拉（Flora）家去，坐在阳台上就能舒舒服服观战，看硝烟，看火光，看军旗。——那可是壮丽的景象！”

“多谢多谢——我可要活活吓死在当场了。咱们在这儿清清静静；自寻危险的人只管去。”

===Original===
Here the dialogue ended, and I remember every word of it though so many years have elapsed. But it often happens that the most remote incidents that occurred even in our earliest childhood, remain stamped on our imagination more clearly and permanently than the events of our riper years when our reasoning faculties have gained the upper hand.

===Chinese===
谈话到此为止。虽已隔了这么多年，那天的每一句话我都记得。世间往往如此：连最早童年里发生的、最遥远的事，都清晰地、经久不灭地烙在我们的想象上；反倒是理智占了上风的成年岁月里发生的事，印得没这么牢。

===Original===
That evening Don Alonzo and Marcial talked over matters whenever Doña Francisca left them together; but this was at rare intervals, for she was suspicious and watchful. When she went off to church to attend vespers, as was her pious custom, the two old sailors breathed freely again as if they were two giddy schoolboys out of sight of the master. They shut themselves into the library, pulled out their maps and studied them with eager attention; then they read some papers in which they had noted down the names of several English vessels with the number of their guns and men, and in the course of their excited conference, in which reading was varied by vigorous commentary, I discovered that they were scheming the plan of an imaginary naval battle. Marcial, by means of energetic gymnastics with his arm and a half, imitated the advance of the squadron and the explosion of the broadsides; with his head he indicated the alternate action of the hostile vessels; with his body the heavy lurch of each ship as it went to the bottom; with his hand the hauling up and down of the signal flags; he represented the boatswain’s whistle by a sharp sibilation; the rattle of the cannon by thumping his wooden leg on the floor; he smacked his tongue to imitate the swearing and confusion of noises in the fight; and as my master assisted him in this performance with the utmost gravity I also must need take my share in the fray, encouraged by their example and giving natural vent to that irresistible longing to make a noise which is a master passion with every boy. Seeing the enthusiasm of the two veterans, I could no longer contain myself and took to leaping about the room—a freedom in which I was justified by my master’s kind familiarity; I imitated with my head and arms the movements of a vessel veering before the wind, and at the same time making my voice as big as possible I shouted out all the most sonorous monosyllables I could think of as being most like the noise of a cannon. My worthy master and the mutilated old sailor, quite as childish as I in their own way, paid no attention to my proceedings, being entirely preoccupied with their own ideas.

===Chinese===
那天晚上，只要堂娜·弗朗西斯卡一让他们单独待着，堂阿隆索和马西亚尔就凑在一起商议大事；可这种机会难得，因为她满腹狐疑，盯得很紧。等她上教堂去做晚祷——这是她虔敬的老习惯——两位老水手才重新畅快地喘气，活像两个躲开老师眼皮的小学生。他们把自己关进书房，摊开海图，如饥似渴地研读；又念几页纸，那上头记着几条英国船的名号和各自的炮数、人数；在他们那场兴致勃勃的会议里，念一段、评一段，我发现他们正在运筹谋划一场想象中的海战。马西亚尔凭他一条半胳膊做种种卖力的体操，模仿舰队的进击和齐射的爆响；用脑袋表示敌对两船你来我往的过招；用身子表现各船沉入海底时那沉重的一歪；用手比划信号旗的升升降降；水手长的哨子，他用一声尖利的咝音来学；大炮的轰响，他用木腿在地板上咚咚地跺；他还咂着舌头，学战斗里的咒骂和乱糟糟的响动；我主人呢，以十二分的庄严给他帮腔作势，我受了他们榜样的鼓舞，也非得在这场混战里凑上一份，痛痛快快发泄一通那股憋不住的吵闹欲——这可是每个男孩压倒一切的天性。眼看两位老将这股热劲，我再也按捺不住，满屋子乱蹦乱跳——主人素来宽厚随和，我这点放肆也不算僭越；我用脑袋和胳膊比划船只迎风转向的动作，同时把嗓门放到最大，把想得起来的最响亮的单音节字一股脑儿喊出来，因为它们最像炮声。我那位可敬的主人和这位缺胳膊少腿的老水手，各自用各自的方式孩子气得不输于我，对我的行径全不理会，一门心思扑在他们自己的念头上。

===Original===
How I have laughed since when I have remembered the scene! and how true it is—in spite of all my respect for my companions in the game—that senile enthusiasm makes old men children once more and renews the puerile follies of the cradle even on the very brink of the tomb!

They were deep in their discussion when they heard Doña Francisca’s step returning from church.

===Chinese===
后来每想起那场面，我笑得可不轻！这话又是何等真切——尽管我对那场游戏里的两位同伴满怀敬意——老年的热狂确能使老人重新变作孩童，即便在坟墓边上，也能把摇篮里的稚气痴愚重新鼓捣出来！

他们正谈得入港，忽听得堂娜·弗朗西斯卡从教堂回来的脚步声。

===Original===
“She is coming!” cried Marcial in an agony of alarm, and they folded up the maps and began to talk of indifferent matters. I, however, not being able to cool down my juvenile blood so rapidly or else not noticing my mistress’s approach soon enough, went on, down the middle of the room in my mad career, ejaculating with the utmost incoherence, such phrases as I had picked up: “Tack to starboard! Now Port! Broadside to the leeward! Fire! *Bang! bom! boom!* …” She came up to me in a fury and without any warning delivered a broadside on my figurehead with her right hand, and with such effect that for a few moments I saw nothing but stars.

“What! you too?” she cried, battering me unmercifully. “You see,” she added, turning on her husband with flashing eyes, “you have taught him to feel no respect for you!—You thought you were still in the *caleta* did you, you little ne’er do weel?”

The commotion ended by my running off to the kitchen crying and disgraced, after striking my colors in an ignominious manner, before the superior force of the enemy; Doña Francisca giving chase and belaboring my neck and shoulders with heavy slaps. In the kitchen I cast anchor and sat down to cry over the fatal termination of my sea-fight.

===Chinese===
“她来了！”马西亚尔惊慌失措地喊了一声，两人赶忙收起海图，谈起不相干的闲话来。可我呢，一身少年的热血没那么快凉下来，要不就是没及时觉察女主人逼近，照旧顺着屋子当间一路狂奔，语无伦次地扯着嗓子喊那些现学来的口令：“右转舵！现在左转舵！朝下风齐射！开火！*轰！嘭！乓！*……”她怒气冲冲地扑到我跟前，也不先打个招呼，抬起右手朝我的艏饰像就是一记齐射，火力之猛，害得我好一阵子眼前只见金星乱迸。

“什么！你也来凑热闹？”她嚷着，劈头盖脸毫不留情地打。“你瞧，”她接着说，眼光灼灼地转向丈夫，“你把他教得连你都不放在眼里了！——你还当自己在*卡莱塔*（caleta，海滩）呢，你这小游手好闲的货色？”

这场风波的收场是：我在占尽优势的敌军面前丢人现眼地降了旗，哭哭啼啼、狼狈不堪地逃进了厨房；堂娜·弗朗西斯卡一路追击，沉重的巴掌一下下落在我脖颈和肩膀上。到了厨房，我抛锚停泊，坐下来为这场海战的致命结局放声大哭。

## V

===Original===

In opposing her husband’s insane determination to join the fleet, Doña Francisca did not rely solely on the reasons given in the last chapter; she had another and more weighty one which she did not mention in the course of that conversation, perhaps because it was wiser not. But the reader does not know it, and must be told.

I have mentioned that my master had a daughter; this daughter’s name was Rosita; she was a little older than I was, that is to say scarcely fifteen, and a marriage had been arranged for her with a young officer of artillery named Malespina, belonging to a family of Medina-Sidonia and distantly related to my master. The wedding had been fixed for the end of October and, as may be supposed, the absence of the bride’s father on so solemn an occasion would have been highly improper.

I must here give some account of my young lady, of her bridegroom, her love-affairs and her projected marriage; and alas! my recollections take a tinge of melancholy, recalling to my fancy many troublesome and faraway scenes, figures from another world﻿—and stirring my weary old heart with feelings of which I should find it hard to say whether they were more pleasurable or sad. Those ardent memories which now lie withered in my brain, like tropical flowers exposed to a chill northern blast, sometimes make me laugh﻿—but sometimes make me grave. However, to my tale, or the reader will be tired of these wearisome reflections which, after all, interest no one but myself.

===Chinese===

在反对丈夫那要参加舰队的疯狂决心时，堂娜·弗朗西斯卡所倚仗的并不只是上一章里说到的那些理由；她另有一条更要紧的，只是在那场谈话里没有提起——也许因为不说出口更为明智。但读者并不知道这条理由，总得讲给读者听。

我已经提过，我的主人有个女儿；这女儿名叫罗西塔，年纪比我稍大，也就是说勉强十五岁；家里已为她定下一门亲事，对象是炮兵部队的一名年轻军官，姓马莱斯皮纳（Malespina），出身梅迪纳-西多尼亚（Medina-Sidonia）的世家，与我主人是远亲。婚期定在十月底；可以想见，在这么隆重的场合，新娘的父亲若是缺席，那是极不体面的事。

这里我必须讲一讲我的小姐，讲讲她的未婚夫、她的情事和她那门议定中的婚事；唉！一回忆起来，我的追念便染上一层忧郁，许许多多纷扰而遥远的旧日场景浮上我的心头，那是些来自另一个世界的人影——它们搅动我这颗疲惫的老心，激起种种滋味，究竟是甘甜多些还是辛酸多些，我实在难以说清。那些热烈的往事如今枯萎在我的脑中，像曝在寒冷北风里的热带花朵，有时让我发笑——有时又让我肃然。好了，言归正传，不然读者要厌倦这些乏味的感想了——毕竟它们除了我自己，谁也不感兴趣。

===Original===

Rosita was uncommonly pretty. I remember vividly how pretty she was, though I should find it difficult to describe her features. I fancy I see her now, smiling in my face; the curious expression of her countenance, unlike any other I ever saw, dwells in my mind﻿—from the perfect distinctness with which it rises before me﻿—like one of those innate ideas which seem to have come into the world with us from a former existence, or to have been impressed on our minds by some mysterious power while we were still in the cradle. And yet I cannot describe it, for what then was real and tangible remains now in my brain as a vague ideal; and while nothing is so fascinating as a beloved ideal, nothing so completely eludes all categorical description.

When I first went into the house I thought that Rosita belonged to some superior order of beings; I will explain my feelings more fully that you may form an idea of my utter simpleness. When we are little and a child comes into the world within our family the grown-up folks are apt to tell us that it has come from France, Paris, or England.
