“Board her! bring pikes!—axes!”

===Chinese===
我跑去依他的话办，取来水，他急切切地喝了。这似乎又给他添了一股劲；我们正要再装一回弹，一声天崩地裂的巨震把我们惊在了原地。主桅被连连的炮弹截断，朝船腰倒下来，又砸在残存的后桅上；整艘船被残骸盖了个严实，那一片混乱骇人听闻。

幸而我藏身的地方够偏，除了头上挨了轻轻一记，毫发无损；那一记虽叫我一时发懵，却没能拦住我掀开压在身上的断绳碎木。水手和陆战队员们正奋力清开那一大堆残木，可从此刻起，还能派上用场的就只剩下层甲板的炮了。我好不容易脱出身来，去找马西亚尔，却没找见；抬眼望向后甲板，我看见舰长已不在岗位上。他被一块碎片击中头部，伤得不轻，昏死过去，两名水手正要把他抬下去送进舱房。我正要跑上前帮忙，一块弹片打在我肩膀上，吓得我魂飞魄散——我认定自己受了致命伤，眼看就要咽气了。这惊吓也没拦住我进舱去；我因失血而摇摇晃晃，有那么几分钟人事不省地昏躺着。一阵下层炮位的轰鸣，随后一个声嘶力竭的喊声，把我从短促的昏厥里唤醒：

“跳帮！拿长矛！——斧头！”

===Original===
And then the confusion was so complete that it was impossible to distinguish human voices from the rest of the hideous uproar. However, somehow—I know not how—without thoroughly waking from my drowsy state, I became aware that all was given up for lost and that the officers had met in the cabin to agree to strike; nor was this the work of my fancy, bewildered as I was, for I heard a voice exclaiming: “The *Trinidad* never strikes!” I felt sure that it was Marcial’s voice; but at any rate someone said it.

When I recovered perfect consciousness, I saw my master sunk on one of the sofas in the cabin, his face hidden in his hands, prostrate with despair, and paying no heed to his wound.

I went to the heartbroken old man, who could find no way of expressing his grief but by embracing me like a father, as if we were both together on the brink of the grave. He, at any rate, was convinced that he must soon die of grief, though his wound was by no means serious. I comforted him as best I might, assuring him that if the battle were indeed lost it was not because I had failed to batter the English to the best of my power; and I went on to say that we should be more fortunate next time—but my childish arguments failed to soothe him.

===Chinese===
接着那混乱达到了极点，人声和其余种种骇人的喧嚣搅在一处，再也分不出来。然而不知怎么的——我说不上怎么——我并未从昏沉中全然醒转，便已明白一切都完了，军官们已聚在舱里，商定降旗投降；这也不是我神志迷糊生出的幻听，因为我听见一个声音高喊：“*圣特立尼达号*绝不降旗！”我敢肯定是马西亚尔的声音；不过无论如何，总有人喊出了这句话。

等我完全清醒过来，只见我主人瘫坐在舱里一张沙发上，双手掩面，绝望得全然垮了，对自己的伤全不理会。

我走到这位心碎的老人跟前，他找不出别的法子宣泄悲痛，只有像父亲一样把我搂在怀里，仿佛我们爷俩已一同站在了坟墓边上。他认定自己眼看就要悲伤致死了，尽管那伤远远说不上重。我竭力宽慰他，向他担保：这一仗纵然真的输了，也绝不是我轰击英国人时没尽全力；我接着还说，下一回我们就该走运了——可我这套孩子气的道理，没能抚慰得了他。

===Original===
Going out presently in search of water for my master, I witnessed the very act of lowering the flag which was flying at the gaff, that being one of the few spars, with the remains of the mizzenmast, that remained standing. The glorious flag, the emblem of our honor, pierced and tattered as it was, which had gathered so many fighting-men under its folds, ran down the rope never to be unfurled again. The idea of stricken pride, of a brave spirit giving way before a superior force, can find no more appropriate symbol to represent it than that of a flying standard which sinks and disappears like a setting sun. And our flag thus slowly descending that fatal evening, at the moment when we surrendered, seem to shed a parting ray of glory.

The firing ceased, and the English took possession of the conquered vessel.

===Chinese===
过了一会儿，我出去为主人找水，正撞见降旗的那一刻：旗子还挂在斜桁上——那是连同后桅残桩在内、硕果仅存的几根桁木之一。那面光荣的旗帜，我们荣誉的象征，早已弹孔累累、破碎褴褛，曾把多少战士收拢在它的褶皱之下，此刻顺着旗绳滑落下去，再也无缘重展。受挫的尊严、勇武之魂在优势之力面前俯首——这般意念，再找不到比一面前进中的军旗徐徐沉落、如落日般隐没更贴切的象征了。而我们这面旗，在那致命的黄昏、在我们投降的时刻缓缓降下时，仿佛洒下了最后一道光荣的余晖。

炮声止息了，英国人接管了这条被征服的战舰。

## XII

===Original===
When the mind had sufficiently recovered from the shock and excitement of battle, and had time to turn from “the pity of it” and the chill of terror left by the sight of that terrific struggle, those who were left alive could see the hapless vessel in all its majesty of horror. Till now we had thought of nothing but self-defence, but when the firing ceased we could turn our attention to the dilapidated state of the ship, which let in the water at a hundred leaks and was beginning to sink, threatening to bury us all, living and dead, at the bottom of the sea. The English had scarcely taken possession when a shout arose from our sailors, as from one man:

“To the pumps!”

All who were able flew to the pumps and labored hard at them; but these ineffectual machines turned out much less water than poured in. Suddenly a shriek even more appalling than any we had heard before filled us with horror. I have said that the wounded had been carried down into the hold which, being below the water line, was secure from the inroads of the cannon shot. But the water was fast gaining there, and some sailors came scrambling up the hatchways exclaiming that the wounded were being drowned. The greater part of the crew hesitated between continuing to pump and running down to rescue the hapless wretches; and God knows what would have happened if an English crew had not come to our assistance. They not only carried up the wounded to the second and third deck but they lent a hand at the pumps and their carpenters set to work to stop the leaks in the ship’s sides.

===Chinese===
等心神从战斗带来的震动与激荡中稍稍恢复，有了余裕从「可怜可叹」的痛惜、从目睹那场可怕厮杀后残留的恐惧寒栗中转过念头，劫后余生的人们才得以看清这条不幸战舰骇人惨象的全貌。直到此刻，我们心心念念的只有自保；待到炮声止息，我们才顾得上留意这船破败的光景：上百处漏洞往里灌水，船身已经开始下沉，威胁着要把我们所有人，不分死者生者，一股脑儿埋进海底。英国人刚刚接管完毕，我们的水手中间便爆出一声呐喊，万众一口：

“快去水泵！”

凡是还使得动气力的，都飞奔到水泵旁拼命苦干；可这些不中用的机器抽出去的水，远比灌进来的少。忽然，一声尖叫拔地而起，比我们此前听过的任何声音都更骇人，吓得我们魂飞魄散。我说过，伤员都被抬进了底舱——那里位于水线之下，炮弹打不进去。可水在那儿也飞快地往上涨，几个水手从舱口连滚带爬地钻上来，喊叫说伤员们要淹死了。大半船员进退两难：是接着抽水，还是奔下去救那些不幸的人？天知道，倘若不是一队英国船员赶来相助，会闹出什么局面。他们不但把伤员抬上了二层、三层甲板，还搭手帮着抽水，他们的木匠也动手堵起船帮上的漏洞。

===Original===
Utterly tired out, and thinking too that Don Alonso might need my services, I returned to the cabin. As I went I saw some Englishmen hoisting the English flag at the bows of the *Trinidad*. As I dare to believe that the amiable reader will allow me to record my feelings, I may say that this incident gave me something to think of. I had always thought of the English as pirates or sea-highwaymen, as a race of adventurers not worthy to be called a nation but living by robbery. When I saw the pride with which they hauled up their flag, saluting it with vociferous cheering; when I perceived the satisfaction it was to them to have made a prize of the largest vessel that, until then, had ever sailed the seas, it struck me that their country, too, was dear to them, that her honor was in their hands and I understood that in that land—to me so mysteriously remote—called England, there must be, as in Spain, honorable men, a paternal king, mothers, daughters, wives, and sisters of these brave mariners—all watching anxiously for their return and praying to God for victory.

I found my master in the cabin, somewhat calmer. The English officers who had come on board treated ours with the most distinguished courtesy and, as I heard, were anxious to transfer the wounded on board their own ship. One of these gentlemen went up to my master as if recognizing him, bowed to him, and addressing him in fairly-good Spanish, reminded him of an old acquaintanceship. Don Alonso responded gravely to his advances and then enquired of him as to some of the details of the battle.

===Chinese===
我累得浑身脱力，又想着堂阿隆索兴许用得着我，便回舱房去了。路上，我看见几个英国人正在*圣特立尼达号*的船头升起英国旗。谅可亲的读者会容我记下自己当时的心情——我得说，这一幕给了我不少可供思索的东西。我一向把英国人看作海盗、海上剪径的强盗，看作一伙不配称为民族的冒险家，全靠打劫过活。可当我看到他们把旗升上去时那副自豪的模样，欢呼雷动地向它致敬；当我体察到，俘获这条迄那时为止航行过海洋的最大船只，于他们是何等的快意，我猛然醒悟：他们的祖国于他们也是亲爱的，祖国的荣誉正攥在他们手里；我还懂得了，在那个在我看来神秘而遥不可及的国度——英格兰——里，必定也同西班牙一样，有正直体面的人，有慈父般的君王，有这些勇敢水手的母亲、女儿、妻子和姐妹——人人焦灼地守望他们归来，向天主祈求胜利。

我在舱房里找到主人，他已平静了些。登舰的英国军官待我方军官礼数极为周到，而且据我所闻，他们急于把伤员转到自家船上去。其中一位先生朝我主人走去，像是认出了他，向他一躬，用相当不错的西班牙语同他攀谈，提起一段旧日的相识。堂阿隆索神色庄重地应酬了几句，随后便向他问起这场战斗的若干细节。

===Original===
“But what became of our reserve? What did Gravina do?” asked my master.

“Gravina withdrew with some of his ships,” replied the English officer.

“Only the *Rayo* and *Neptuno* came to our assistance of all the front line?”

“Four French ships—the *Duguay-Trouin*, the *Mont Blanc*, the *Scipion*, and the *Formidable* were the only ones that kept out of the action.”

===Chinese===
“我们的预备队怎么样了？格拉维纳做了些什么？”我主人问。

“格拉维纳带着他一部分舰船撤走了，”英国军官答道。

“整个前队里，就只有*拉约号*和*内普图诺号*来援救过我们？”

“四艘法国军舰——*迪盖-特鲁安号*（Duguay-Trouin）、*蒙布朗号*（Mont Blanc）、*西皮翁号*（Scipion）和*福米达布尔号*——是仅有的置身战外、未曾接仗的几条船。”

===Original===
“But Gravina—where was Gravina?” Don Alonso persisted.

“He got off in the *Príncipe de Astúrias*; but as he was chased I do not know whether he reached Cádiz in safety.”

“And the *San Ildefonso*?”

“She struck.”

“And the *Santa Ana*?”

“Struck too.”

===Chinese===
“可是格拉维纳——格拉维纳当时在哪儿？”堂阿隆索追问。

“他乘*阿斯图里亚斯亲王号*脱了身；不过他遭到了追击，能否平安抵达加的斯，我就不得而知了。”

“*圣伊尔德丰索号*呢？”

“降了旗。”

“*圣安娜号*呢？”

“也降了。”

===Original===
“Good God!” cried my master, unable to conceal his indignation. “But you did not take the *Nepomuceno*?”

“Yes, that too.”

“Are you sure of that? With Churruca?”

“He was killed,” said the Englishman with sincere regret.

“Killed—Churruca killed!” exclaimed Don Alonso in grievous bewilderment. “And the *Bahama*—she was saved—the *Bahama* must have been able to reach Cádiz in safety.”

“She was taken too.”

===Chinese===
“天哪！”我主人嚷道，掩不住满腔愤懑。“可你们总没能拿下*圣胡安·内波穆塞诺号*吧？”

“拿了，那条也没能幸免。”

“此话当真？舰上可是有丘鲁卡啊！”

“他阵亡了，”英国人说，语气里透着真诚的惋惜。

“阵亡了——丘鲁卡阵亡了！”堂阿隆索喊道，痛心疾首，惶然失措。“那*巴哈马号*呢——它保住了吧——*巴哈马号*总该能平安抵达加的斯了吧。”

“它也被俘了。”

===Original===
“Taken! And Galiano? He is a hero and a cultivated gentleman.”

“He was,” said the Englishman sadly, “but he too is dead.”

“And the *Montañes* with Alcedo?”

“Killed, killed.”

===Chinese===
“被俘了！那加利亚诺呢？他是个英雄，还是位有学养的君子。”

“他确实是，”英国人凄然道，“可他也死了。”

“那*蒙塔涅斯号*（Montañes）呢？还有阿尔塞多（Alcedo）？”

“阵亡了，都阵亡了。”

===Original===
My master could not control his emotion and as, at his advanced age, presence of mind is lacking at such terrible moments, he suffered the slight humiliation of shedding a few tears as he remembered his lost friends. Nor are tears unbecoming to a noble soul; on the contrary, they reveal a happy infusion of delicate feeling, when combined with a resolute temper. My master’s tears were manly tears, shed after he had done his duty as a sailor; but, hastily recovering from this paroxysm of grief, and anxious to retort on the Englishman by some pain equal to that he had caused, he said:

“You too have suffered, no doubt, and have lost some men of mark?”

“We have suffered one irreparable loss,” said the English officer in accents as deeply sad as Don Alonso’s. “We have lost our greatest man, the bravest of the brave—our noble, heroic, incomparable Nelson.”

And his fortitude holding out no better than my master’s he made no attempt to conceal his anguish of grief; he covered his face with his hands and wept with the pathetic frankness of incontrollable sorrow for his leader, his guardian, and his friend.

===Chinese===
我主人再也把持不住自己的感情；他年事已高，逢此骇人时刻，心神本就难以镇定，想起那些殒命的旧友，竟流下几滴眼泪，受了一回小小的屈辱。眼泪于高贵的灵魂并非有失体面；相反，与坚毅的品性相配，倒显出细腻情感的美妙浸润。我主人的泪是男子汉的泪，是在尽完水职责分之后洒下的；可他急急从这阵悲恸的发作中挣脱出来，一心想回敬英国人一记同等分量的痛楚，好还报他加给自己的苦楚，便说道：

“你们想必也吃了亏，折了几位显赫的人物吧？”

“我们蒙受了一项无可弥补的损失，”英国军官说，语调同堂阿隆索一样沉痛。“我们失去了我们最伟大的人物，勇者中之勇者——我们高贵、英勇、无与伦比的纳尔逊。”

他的坚忍也并不比我主人更能撑持；他无意掩饰满腔的哀恸，双手掩面哭了出来，为他的统帅、他的守护者、他的朋友，怀着无法抑制的悲伤，哭得坦率动人。

===Original===
Nelson, mortally wounded at an early stage of the battle by a gunshot—the ball piercing his chest and lodging in the spine—had simply said to Captain Hardy: “They have done for me at last, Hardy.” He lingered till the evening, not losing any details of the battle, and his naval and military genius only failed him with the last breath of his shattered body. Though suffering agonies of pain, he still dictated his orders and kept himself informed of the maneuvers of both fleets; and when at length he was assured that victory was on the side of the English, he exclaimed: “Thank God, I have done my duty!” A quarter of an hour later the greatest sailor of the age breathed his last. The reader will forgive me this digression.

It may seem strange that we did not know the fate of many of the ships of the combined fleets. But nothing could be more natural than our ignorance, considering the great length of our front and the plan of isolated fights contrived and carried out by the English. Their vessels had got mixed up with ours and the ships fought at close quarters; the one which had engaged us hid the rest of the squadron from view, besides which the dense smoke prevented our seeing anything that was not quite close to us. Towards nightfall and before the firing had altogether ceased, we could distinguish a few ships in the offing, looking like phantoms; some with half their rigging gone, and others completely dismasted. The mist, the smoke and, indeed, our own wearied and bewildered brains, would not allow us to distinguish whether they were our own or the enemy’s, and as, from time to time the glare of a broadside in the distance lighted up the lugubrious scene, we could see that the fight was still going on to a desperate end between detached groups of ships, while others were flying before the wind without aim or purpose, and some of ours were being towed by the English to the South.

===Chinese===
纳尔逊在战斗初期便中了一枪，伤重致命——弹丸穿透胸膛，嵌在脊柱里——他只对哈迪（Hardy）舰长说了一句：“哈迪，他们这回总算把我要了。”他捱到了傍晚，战斗的细节一样也不曾漏过；直到那具残破的身躯咽下最后一口气，他海陆军略的天才才随之而竭。尽管痛楚钻心，他仍旧口授命令，随时掌握着两支舰队的动向；待终于确知胜利站在英国人一边，他喊道：“感谢上帝，我尽了我的职责！”一刻钟后，这位当世最伟大的水手溘然长逝。读者诸君还请宽恕我这一段题外之笔。

我们竟不知道联合舰队许多舰船的下落，这听来或许奇怪。可想想我们战线的漫长，想想英国人精心筹划并实施的那种各个孤立、分开接仗的打法，我们的懵然无知便再自然不过了。他们的船同我们的搅作一处，各舰近舷相搏；缠住我们的那一条，把分舰队其余的船全挡在了视线之外，更何况浓烟滚滚，稍远一点的东西便什么都望不见。将近黄昏、炮声尚未全歇时，我们能辨出远处海面上的几条船，影影绰绰如同鬼魅：有的索具去了半边，有的桅杆尽折。雾气、硝烟，还有我们自己疲惫惶乱的脑子，都叫我们分不清那些是自家的船还是敌船；远处一舷齐射的火光时不时照亮这片阴惨的景象，我们由此看得出，几群零散的船还在拼死缠斗到底，另一些船正漫无目标地随风逃窜，而我们这边有几条，正被英国人拖着往南去。

===Original===
Night fell, increasing the misery and horror of our situation. It might have been hoped that Nature at least would be on our side after so much disaster; but, on the contrary, the elements lashed us with their fury as though Heaven thought our cup of misfortune was not yet full. A tremendous storm burst and the winds and waves tossed and buffeted our ship in their fury and, as she could not be worked, she was utterly at their mercy. The rolling was so terrible that it was very difficult even to work the pumps, and this, combined with the exhausted condition of the men, made our condition grow worse every minute. An English vessel, which as we learned was the *Prince*, tried to take us in tow; but her efforts were in vain and she was forced to keep off for fear of a collision which would have been fatal to both. Meanwhile it was impossible to get anything to eat, and I was dying of hunger, though the others seemed insensible to anything but the immediate danger and gave no thought to this important matter. I dared not ask for a piece of bread even, for fear of seeming greedy and troublesome; but at the same time, I must confess—and without shame—I looked out sharply to see if there were any place where I might hope to find any kind of eatable stores. Emboldened by hunger, I made free to inspect the hold where the biscuit-boxes were kept, and what was my astonishment at finding Marcial there before me, stowing himself with everything he could lay his hands on. The old man’s wound was not serious, and though a ball had carried away his right foot, as this was only the lower end of his wooden leg the mishap only left him a little more halt than before.

“Here, Gabrielillo,” he said, giving me a heap of biscuits, “take these. No ship can sail without ballast.” And then he pulled out a bottle and drank with intense satisfaction. As we went out of the biscuit-room we saw that we were not the only visitors who had made a raid upon it; on the contrary, it was very evident that it had been well pillaged not long since.

===Chinese===
夜幕降临，我们的处境愈发凄惨骇人。遭了这许多灾祸，本指望老天爷至少肯站在我们一边；谁知恰恰相反，风浪对我们发作得愈发狂暴，仿佛上天还嫌我们的厄运之杯没有斟满。一场骇人的风暴骤然而起，狂风巨浪盛怒之中把我们的船抛来掷去；船既无法操纵，便全凭它们摆布了。横摇得那样厉害，连水泵都极难操作，加上人人精疲力竭，我们的处境一刻比一刻凶险。一条英国船——后来我们得知是*王子号*（Prince）——试图拖带我们；可它的努力徒劳无功，又怕两船相撞、同归于尽，只好驶开了。这当口连一点吃食也弄不到，我饿得要死；旁的人却仿佛除了眼前的危险对什么都麻木不仁，全不把这件大事放在心上。我连一块面包也不敢讨，生怕显得嘴馋讨嫌；可与此同时——我必须承认，而且并不羞于承认——我眼尖地四处张望，看有没有哪个地方能有指望寻到一点可吃的东西。饥饿壮了我的胆，我放肆地钻进存放硬饼干箱的底舱去查看，你道我吃惊不吃惊——马西亚尔竟已先我一步到了那儿，正把两手够得着的东西往身上塞。老头子的伤不重；虽说一颗炮弹打飞了他的右脚，可那不过是木腿的下截，这场祸事只是叫他比先前更瘸了一点。

“给，加布里埃尔西托，”他说着塞给我一大捧饼干，“拿着。船没有压舱物可开不动。”随后他掏出一瓶酒，心满意足地喝了一大口。我们走出饼干舱时，看见来光顾的可不止我们两个；恰恰相反，明明白白这儿不久前刚遭过一场像样的洗劫。

===Original===
Having recruited my strength I could now think of trying to make myself useful by lending a hand at the pumps or helping the carpenters. They were laboriously repairing some of the damage done, aided by the English, who watched all our proceedings; indeed, as I have since learned, they kept an eye on every one of our sailors, for they were afraid lest we should suddenly mutiny and turn upon them to recapture the vessel; in this, however, the enemy showed more vigilance than common sense, for we must indeed have lost our wits before attempting to recover a ship in such a condition. However, the Greatcoats were everywhere at once, and we could not stir without being observed.

Night fell, and as I was perishing with cold I quitted the deck where I could scarcely bear myself besides incurring constant risk of being swept overboard by a wave, so I went down into the cabin. My purpose was to try to sleep a little while—but who could sleep in such a night? The same confusion prevailed in the cabin as on deck. Those who had escaped unhurt were doing what they could to aid the wounded, and these, disturbed by the motion of the vessel which prevented their getting any rest, were so pitiable a sight that it was impossible to resign one’s self to sleep. On one side, covered with the Spanish flag, lay the bodies of the officers who had been killed; and in the midst of all this misery, surrounded by so much suffering, these senseless corpses seemed really to be envied. They alone on board the *Trinidad* were at rest, to them nothing mattered now: fatigue and pain, the disgrace of defeat, or physical sufferings. The standard which served them as a glorious winding-sheet shut them out, as it were, from the world of responsibility, of dishonor, and of despair, in which we were left behind. They could not care for the danger the vessel was in, for to them it was no longer anything but a coffin.

===Chinese===
补足了气力，我便想着出点力，去水泵边搭把手，或是帮帮木匠。木匠们正吃力地修补几处破损，英国人从旁协助，同时监视着我们的一举一动；的确——这是我后来才知道的——他们盯着我方每一个水手，生怕我们突然哗变，反过来把他们打翻、夺回这条船；不过在这一点上，敌人警惕有余而见识不足，因为要想夺回这样一条船，我们真得先失了心智才行。反正大氅佬们无处不在，我们稍一动弹就落在人家眼里。

夜幕落下，我冷得要死，便离开了甲板——在那儿我几乎站立不住，还时时有被一个浪头卷下海去的危险——下到了舱房里。我本想试着睡上一会儿——可这样的夜里谁睡得着？舱房里同甲板上一般混乱。侥幸无伤的人正尽力救护伤员；而伤员们被船身的颠簸搅得片刻不得安生，那光景实在可怜，叫人怎么也无法安心入睡。一旁，阵亡军官的遗体上覆着西班牙国旗，并排安放；身处这般愁惨之中，被这许多苦痛包围着，这些无知无觉的尸身竟似乎当真令人羡慕。全船之上，惟有他们在*圣特立尼达号*上得了安息；如今什么事对他们都不相干了：疲劳与疼痛也好，败战的耻辱也好，肉体的苦楚也好。那面权作他们光荣裹尸布的军旗，仿佛把他们同这个世界——责任、屈辱与绝望的世界——隔断了，把我们留在了后头。船身遭逢的危险他们无从挂心，因为这船对他们而言已不过是一具棺材。

===Original===
The officers who were killed were Don Juan Cisniega, a lieutenant in the navy, who was not related to my master, in spite of their identity of name; Don Joaquin de Salas and Don Juan Matute, also lieutenants; Don José Graullé, lieutenant-colonel in the army; Urias, lieutenant in command of a frigate, and midshipman Don Antonio de Bobadilla. The sailors and marines whose corpses lay strewn about the gun-decks and upper-deck amounted to the terrible number of four hundred.

===Chinese===
阵亡的军官有：堂胡安·西斯涅加（Don Juan Cisniega），海军上尉，虽与我主人同姓，却非亲非故；堂华金·德·萨拉斯（Don Joaquin de Salas）和堂胡安·马图特（Don Juan Matute），也都是海军上尉；堂何塞·格劳列（Don José Graullé），陆军中校；乌里亚斯（Urias），出任巡防舰舰长的海军上尉；还有见习军官堂安东尼奥·德·博瓦迪利亚（Don Antonio de Bobadilla）。横七竖八陈尸于炮甲板和上层甲板的水手与陆战队员，数目骇人，达四百之众。

===Original===
Never shall I forget the moment when the bodies were cast into the sea, by order of the English officer in charge of the ship. The dismal ceremony took place on the morning of the 22nd when the storm seemed to be at its wildest on purpose to add to the terrors of the scene. The bodies of the officers were brought on deck, the priest said a short prayer for this was no time for elaborate ceremonial, and our melancholy task began. Each wrapped in a flag, with a cannonball tied to his feet, was dropped into the waves without any of the solemn and painful emotion which under ordinary circumstances would have agitated the lookers-on. Our spirits were so quelled by disaster that the contemplation of death had become almost indifference. Still, a burial at sea is more terribly sad than one on land. We cover the dead with earth and leave him there; those who loved him know that there is a spot where the dear remains are laid and can mark it with a slab, a cross, or a monument; but at sea—the body is cast into that heaving, shifting waste; it is lost forever as it disappears; imagination cannot follow it in its fall—down, down to the fathomless abyss; it is impossible to realize that it still exists at the bottom of the deep. These were my reflections as I watched the corpses vanish—the remains of those brave fighting-men, so full of life only the day before—the pride of their country and the joy of all who loved them.

===Chinese===
遵照负责该舰的英国军官之命，尸身被抛入海中——那一刻我永世不忘。这凄凉的仪式是在二十二日清晨举行的，风暴仿佛存心肆虐到最狂的地步，好给这场景再添几分可怖。军官们的遗体被抬上甲板，随舰神父匆匆念了一段祷文——这可不是讲究繁文缛节的时候——随后我们凄惶的活计便开始了。每一具都裹着旗子，脚上拴一颗炮弹，投进波涛里去；四周全无素常那种会令观者心潮翻涌的庄严沉痛。灾祸把我们的心气压得如此服帖，以至对死亡的凝视已近乎麻木。话虽如此，海葬终究比陆上下葬更令人心碎。我们把泥土撒在死者身上，把他留在那儿；爱他的人知道有一方安置至亲遗骨的土地，可以立一块石板、一个十字架或一座坟茔作记认；可是在海上——躯体被抛进那翻腾流动的荒漠，一消失便永远失落了；想象力也无法追随它坠落——坠啊，坠啊，坠向无底的深渊；谁也无法真切意识到它仍旧存在于深海之底。我望着尸身一一消失，心里想的便是这些——那些英勇的战士，前一天还生龙活虎——他们是祖国的骄傲，是所有爱他们的人的欢欣，如今只剩这残躯。

===Original===
The sailors were thrown overboard with less ceremony; the regulation is that they shall be tied up in their hammocks, but there was no time to carry this out. Some indeed were wrapped round as the rules require, but most of them were thrown into the sea without any shroud or ball at their feet, for the simple reason that there were not enough for all. There were four hundred of them, more or less, and merely to clear them overboard and out of sight every able-bodied man that was left had to lend a hand, so as to get it done as quickly as possible. Much to my horror I saw myself forced to offer my services in the dismal duty, and many a dead man dropped over the ship’s side at a push from my hand helping other and stronger ones.

===Chinese===
水手们抛海时仪式就更简单了；按条令，他们应裹进各自的吊床里，可已没有工夫照办。有几个确实按规矩裹扎了，但大多数既无裹尸之物，脚上也没拴坠子，就被扔进了海里，原因很简单——东西不够四百来人分的。为着尽快把这些尸身抬过舷墙、送出视线，凡是还使得动力气的都得搭手。我惊骇地发现自己也被迫在这凄惨的差事里出力，许许多多死者便在我——帮着旁人、帮着更有力气的人——的一推之下，翻出船舷落了下去。

===Original===
One incident—or rather coincidence—occurred which filled me with horror. A body horribly mauled and mutilated had been picked up by two sailors, and just as they lifted it one or two of the bystanders allowed themselves to utter some of those coarse and grim jests which are always offensive, and at such a moment revolting. I know not how it was that this poor wretch was the only one which moved them so completely to lose the sense of reverence due to the dead, but they exclaimed: “He has been paid out for old scores—he will never be at his tricks again,” and other witticisms of the same kind. For a moment my blood rose, but my indignation suddenly turned to astonishment mingled with an indescribable feeling of awe, regret, and aversion, when, on looking at the mangled features of the corpse, I recognized my uncle. I shut my eyes with a shudder, and did not open them again till the splash of the water in my face told me that he had disappeared forever from mortal ken. This man had been very cruel to me, very cruel to his sister; still, he was my own flesh and blood, my mother’s brother; the blood that flowed in my veins was his, and that secret voice which warns us to be charitable to the faults of our own kith and kin could not be silenced after what I had seen, for at the moment when I recognized him I had perceived in those bloodstained features some reminder of my mother’s face, and this stirred my deepest feelings. I forgot that the man had been a brutal wretch, and all his barbarous treatment of me during my hapless childhood. I can honestly declare—and I venture to do so though it is to my own credit—that I forgave him with all my heart and lifted up my soul to God, praying for mercy on him for all his sins.

I learned afterwards that he had behaved gallantly in the fight, but even this had not won him the respect of his comrades who, regarding him as a low sneak, never found a good word for him—not even at that supreme moment when, as a rule, every offence is forgiven on earth in the belief that the sinner is rendering an account to his Maker.

===Chinese===
其间出了一桩令我不寒而栗的事——或者说，一桩巧合。一具被摧残得血肉模糊的尸身由两名水手抬起，就在他们抬起的一刻，旁边看的人里有一两个竟脱口说出那种粗鄙刻毒的俏皮话——这类话平时听着就刺耳，在这种时刻更是令人作呕。我说不清怎么偏偏是这可怜虫，叫他们全然忘了对死者应有的敬畏，只听他们嚷道：“他这是老账遭了清算——再也耍不成把戏了，”诸如此类的妙语。一时间我血往上涌；可当我细看那尸身残破的面目、认出他是我叔叔时，我的义愤霎时化作惊愕，掺杂着一种说不清是敬畏、悔恨还是嫌恶的心情。我打了个寒噤，阖上双眼，直到水花溅上我的脸，告诉我他已永远从世人的眼目中消失，才重新睁开。这个人对我曾非常狠毒，对我母亲也非常狠毒；可他终究是我的亲骨肉，我母亲的兄弟；我血管里流着的血也有他的一份。亲眼见过这一幕后，那声提醒我们对自家人骨肉的过错要存几分慈悲的隐秘心声，再也无法按下——因为认出他的一瞬，我在那张血污的面孔上看出了几分我母亲的影子，这触动了我心底最深的情感。我忘了这人曾是个残暴的恶棍，忘了我那不幸的童年里他加给我的种种虐待。我可以问心无愧地声言——尽管这话于我有光，我也斗胆说出来——我发自内心地宽恕了他，并向天主高擎起我的灵魂，祈求宽宥他的一切罪愆。

我后来听说，他在这一仗里表现得很英勇；可就连这一点也没能赢得同伴们的敬重——他们把他看作卑劣鬼祟的小人，从没有一句好话给他——哪怕在那样最后的时刻。按常理，人到了那一刻，世人念着他正要去造物主跟前交账，什么过节都该一笔勾销的。

===Original===
As the day advanced the *Prince* attempted once more to take the *Santísima Trinidad* in tow, but with no better success than before. Our situation was no worse, although the tempest raged with undiminished fury, for a good deal of the mischief had been patched up, and we thought that if the weather should mend the hulk, at any rate, might be saved. The English made a great point of it, for they were very anxious to take the largest man-of-war ever seen afloat into Gibraltar as a trophy; so they willingly plied the pumps by night and by day and allowed us to rest awhile. All through the day of the 22nd the sea continued terrific, tossing the huge and helpless vessel as though it were a little fishing-boat, and the enormous mass of timber proved the soundness of her build by not simply falling to pieces under the furious lashing of the waters. At some moments she rolled over so completely on her beam ends that it seemed as though she must go to the bottom, but suddenly the wave would fly off in smoke, as it were, before the hurricane, the ship, righting herself, rode over it with a toss of her mighty prow—which displayed the Lion of Castile—and we breathed once more with the hope of escaping with our lives.

===Chinese===
白昼渐深，*王子号*再度尝试拖带*圣特立尼达号*，成效却不见得比先前好多少。我们的处境没有再恶化——虽说风暴毫未减其狂暴——因为破损处已修补了不少；我们想，天气若能好转，这副船壳子总还有救。英国人把这件事看得极重，他们急欲把这条世人从未见过的最大战舰拖进直布罗陀当战利品；于是他们心甘情愿地日夜守着水泵抽水，让我们歇上一阵。二十二日一整天，海况始终骇人，把这巨大而无助的船抛掷得像一条小小渔船；而这一大团木头竟没有在狂涛怒抽之下四散崩解，足证其造工之坚固。有几阵子，船整个儿侧倾到桁端，眼看非沉不可；可倏忽之间，浪头仿佛化作飞沫散在飓风面前，船身自行扶正，昂起它那巍峨的船首——上面饰着卡斯蒂利亚雄狮——一挺便骑过了浪头，我们便又缓过一口气，重新燃起逃得性命的希望。

===Original===
On all sides we could see the scattered fleets; many of the ships were English, severely damaged and striving to gain shelter under the coast. There were Frenchmen and Spaniards too, some dismasted, others in tow of the enemy. Marcial recognized the *San Ildefonso*. Floating about were myriads of fragments and masses of wreck—spars, timbers, broken boats, hatches, bulwarks, and doors—besides two unfortunate sailors who were clinging to a plank, and who must have been swept off and drowned if the English had not hastened to rescue them. They were brought on board more dead than alive, and their resuscitation after being in the very jaws of death was like a new birth to them.

===Chinese===
四面八方都望得见溃散的舰队；许多船是英国的，伤损惨重，正奋力往海岸背风处躲避。也有法国船和西班牙船，有的桅杆尽折，有的被敌人拖曳着。马西亚尔认出了*圣伊尔德丰索号*。水面上漂着数不清的残骸碎块——桅桁、木料、破碎的小艇、舱盖、舷墙和门板——此外还有两个倒霉的水手抱着一块木板，若不是英国人赶忙去救，早被卷走淹死了。他们被救上船时已半死不活；从死神口边还阳，对他们来说无异于再世为人。

===Original===
That day went by between agonies and hopes—now we thought nothing could save the ship and that we must be taken on board an Englishman then again we hoped to keep her afloat. The idea of being taken into Gibraltar as prisoners was intolerable, not so much to me perhaps as to men of punctilious honor and sensitive dignity like my master whose mental anguish at the thought must have been intolerable. However, all the torment of suspense, at any rate, was relieved by the evening when it was unanimously agreed that if we were not transferred to an English ship at once, to the bottom we must go with the vessel, which now had five feet of water in the hold. Uriarte and Cisneros took the announcement with dignified composure, saying that it mattered little to them whether they perished at once or were prisoners in a foreign land. The task was at once begun in the doubtful twilight, and as there were above three hundred wounded to be transferred it was no easy matter. The available number of hands was about five hundred, all that were left uninjured of the original crew of eleven hundred and fifteen before the battle.

===Chinese===
那一天便在苦楚与希冀之间捱了过去——时而觉得这条船断无生理，我们非得被接到英国船上不可；时而又指望能让它继续浮着。被当作战俘押进直布罗陀，这个念头叫人无法忍受——对我而言或许还轻些，对我主人那样荣誉上一丝不苟、尊严上极为敏感的人，一想到这个，精神上的苦刑必定难堪至极。不过，悬而不决的煎熬总算到傍晚有了个了断：众人一致断定，若不立刻把我们转移到英国船上，我们就得随船同沉——底舱里已经积了五英尺深的水。乌里亚尔特和希斯内罗斯闻讯后神态庄重从容，说他们或是顷刻丧生，或是异国为囚，都无足轻重。转移的活计当即在那明暗难辨的暮色里开始；伤员有三百多，这可不是桩轻省事。能够动手的人手约莫五百，是全舰开战前一千一百一十五名船员里侥幸无伤、硕果仅存的全部了。

===Original===
We set to work promptly with the launches of the *Trinidad* and the *Prince*, and three other boats belonging to the English. The wounded were attended to first; but though they were lifted with all possible care they could not be moved without great suffering, and some entreated with groans and shrieks to be left in peace, preferring immediate death to anything that could aggravate and prolong their torments. But there was no time for pity, and they were carried to the boats as ruthlessly as the cold corpses of their comrades had been flung into the sea.

===Chinese===
我们当即动手，动用*圣特立尼达号*和*王子号*的大艇，外加英国人的另外三条小艇。伤员优先；可尽管抬得再小心，一挪动便痛楚万状，有的哼着、惨叫着哀求让他们安静躺着，宁可立时就死，也不愿再受任何加重和延长其痛楚的罪。可是已没有怜悯的工夫，他们被抬上小艇时，同他们战友冰冷的尸身被抛下海时一样冷酷无情。

===Original===
Uriarte and Cisneros embarked in the English captain’s gig, but when they urged my master to accompany them he obstinately refused, saying that he wished to be last to leave the sinking ship. This I confess disturbed me not a little, for as by this time, the hardy patriotism which at first had given me courage had evaporated, I thought only of saving my life, and to stay on board a foundering vessel was clearly not the best means to that laudable end. Nor were my fears ill founded, for not more than half the men had been taken off when a dull roar of terror echoed through the ship.

“She is going to the bottom—the boats, to the boats!” shouted some, and there was a rush to the ship’s side, all looking out eagerly for the return of the boats. Every attempt at work or order was given up, the wounded were forgotten, and several who had been brought on deck dragged themselves to the side in a sort of delirium, to seek an opening and throw themselves into the sea. Up through the hatchways came a hideous shriek which I think I can hear as I write, freezing the blood in my veins and setting my hair on end. It came from the poor wretches on the lowest deck who already felt the waters rising to drown them and vainly cried for help—to God or men—who can tell! Vainly indeed to men, for they had enough to do to save themselves. They jumped wildly into the boats, and this confusion in the darkness hindered progress. One man alone, quite cool in the midst of the danger, remained in the state cabin, paying no heed to all that was going on around him, walking up and down sunk in thought, as though the planks he trod were not fast sinking into the gulf below. It was my master. I ran to rouse him from his stupefaction. “Sir,” I cried, “we are drowning!”

Don Alonzo did not heed me, and if I may trust my memory he merely said without looking round:

“How Paca will laugh at me, when I go home after such a terrible defeat!”

“Sir, the ship is sinking!” I insisted, not indeed exaggerating the danger, but in vehement entreaty.

My master looked at the sea, at the boats, at the men who were blindly and desperately leaping overboard; I looked anxiously for Marcial and called him as loudly as I could shout. At the same time I seemed to lose all consciousness of where I was and what was happening. I turned giddy and I could see nothing. To tell how I was saved from death I can only trust to the vaguest recollections, like the memory of a dream, for in fact I fairly swooned with terror. A sailor, as I fancy, came up to Don Alonso while I was speaking to him; in his strong arms I felt myself lifted up and when I somewhat recovered my wits I found myself in one of the boats, propped up against my master’s knees, while he held my head in his hands with fatherly care and kindness. Marcial held the tiller and the boat was crowded with men.

===Chinese===
乌里亚尔特和希斯内罗斯上了英国舰长的小艇；他们劝我主人同行，他却执意不肯，说自己要最后一个离开这艘下沉的船。我得承认，这叫我心里着实不安：那股起初给我壮胆的血性爱国热忱，到这时早已蒸发干净，我一心只想逃命，而待在一条正往下沉的船上，显然不是通向这个可嘉目标的最佳办法。我的害怕也并非无凭：人员还没撤下一半，一声低沉的惊惶吼啸便滚遍了全船。

“船要沉了——小艇，快上小艇！”有人喊道；众人一窝蜂涌向船舷，个个急切地张望小艇归来。一切工作与秩序尽数撂下，伤员被忘了个干净；几个已被抬上甲板的伤号，发着癔症似的拖着身子挪到舷边，找空子要往海里跳。舱口里传上来一声凄厉的尖叫——我此刻落笔，仿佛还能听见它——它冻住了我血管里的血，令我毛发倒竖。那声音来自最底层甲板上的可怜人：他们已觉出大水涨上来要淹死他们了，徒声呼救——向天主还是向人——谁说得清！向人呼救确实是徒然，因为人人自顾不暇。他们疯狂地跳进小艇，黑暗中的这场混乱更碍了事。只有一个人，身处危险之中全然镇定，仍留在大舱里，对周遭发生的一切置若罔闻，来回踱步，沉入深思，仿佛脚下踏的船板并未正急速沉入脚下的深渊。这就是我的主人。我跑过去，要把他从恍惚中唤醒。“先生，”我喊道，“我们要淹死了！”

堂阿隆索没有理会我；如果我的记忆靠得住，他只头也不回地说了一句：

“打了这么一场可怕的败仗再回家，帕卡还不知要怎么笑话我呢！”

“先生，船在下沉！”我坚持道——并非言过其实地夸大危险，而是声声恳求。

主人望了望海，望了望小艇，望了望那些不管不顾、亡命往海里跳的人；我则急切地寻找马西亚尔，放开嗓子拼命喊他。与此同时，我似乎全然失去了对身在何处、发生何事的知觉。我头晕目眩，眼前一片漆黑。要说我是如何从死亡里逃生的，我只能凭着些最模糊的印象，如同一场梦的记忆，因为实际上我吓得真的昏了过去。依稀仿佛，就在我同堂阿隆索说话的当口，一个水手走上前来；我在他那双强壮的臂膀里觉出自己被高高抱起；等神志稍稍恢复，我发现自己已在一条小艇里，靠在我主人的膝头，他正用双手捧着我的头，满怀为父者的慈爱与体贴。马西亚尔掌着舵柄，艇上挤满了人。

===Original===
Looking up I saw, apparently not more than four or five yards away, the black side of our ship sinking fast; but through the portholes of the deck that was still above water I could see a dim light—that of the lamp which had been lighted at dusk and which still kept unwearied watch over the wreck of the deserted vessel. I still could hear the groans and cries of the hapless sufferers whom it had been impossible to remove and who were within a few feet of the abyss while, by that dismal lamp they could see each other’s misery and read each other’s agony in their eyes.

My fancy reverted to the dreadful scene on board—another inch of water would be enough to overweight her and destroy the little buoyancy that was left her. How far did those poor creatures understand the nearness of their fate? What were they saying in this awful moment? If they could see us safe in our boat—if they could hear the splash of our oars, how bitterly must their tortured souls complain to Heaven! But such agonizing martyrdom must surely avail to purify them of all guilt, and the grace of God must fill that hapless vessel, now when it was on the point of disappearing forever!

Our boat moved away; and still I watched the shapeless mass—though I confess that I believe it was my imagination rather than my eyes that discerned the *Trinidad* through the darkness, till I believe I saw, against the black sky, a huge arm reaching down to the tossing waters—the effect no doubt of my imagination on my senses.

===Chinese===
抬眼望去，只见我们那条船黑色的船帮离着似乎不过四五码远，正飞快下沉；可透过还露在水面上的那层甲板的炮眼，我望得见一点昏黄的灯光——那是入暮时点起的一盏灯，还在不知疲倦地守着这条被遗弃的残船。我还听得见那些无法撤走的可怜人的呻吟与呼号——他们离深渊已只有几英尺；借着那盏凄凉的灯，他们彼此看得见对方的惨状，从彼此的眼里读得出对方的绝痛。

我的思绪又回到船上那可怕的景象——再多进一寸水，就足以把它压垮，毁掉它仅存的那点浮力。那些可怜人究竟明白自己的命运已近到什么地步？在这骇人的时刻，他们在说些什么？他们若能看见我们安坐在小艇里——若能听见我们桨叶的泼溅声，他们受尽折磨的灵魂该怎样向苍天哀号啊！可这样痛彻的殉难必能涤净他们一切罪愆；在这条倒霉的船即将永远消失的此刻，天主的恩宠必定充满了它！

我们的小艇划开了；我仍望着那团不成形状的巨影——不过我承认，在黑暗中辨认出*圣特立尼达号*的，我相信与其说是我的眼睛，不如说是我的想象；直到后来，我仿佛看见，漆黑的天幕上垂下一条巨臂，直探向翻腾的海水——那无疑是我的想象作用于感官而生出的幻象。

## XIII

===Original===
The boat moved on﻿—but whither? Not Marcial himself knew where he was steering her to. The darkness was so complete that we lost sight of the other boats and the lights on board the *Prince* were as invisible through the fog, as though a gust of wind had extinguished them. The waves ran so high and the squalls were so violent that our frail barque made very little way, but thanks to skilful steering she only once shipped water. We all sat silent, most of us fixing a melancholy gaze on the spot where we supposed our deserted comrades were at this moment engaged in an agonizing death-struggle. In the course of this passage I could not fail to make, as was my habit, certain reflections which I may venture to call philosophical. Some may laugh at a philosopher of fourteen; but I will not heed their laughter; I will try to write down the thoughts that occupied me at this juncture. Children too can think great thoughts and at such a moment, in face of such a spectacle, what brain but an idiot’s could remain unmoved.

There were both English and Spaniards in our boat﻿—though most Spaniards﻿—and it was strange to note how they fraternized, helping and encouraging each other in their common danger, and quite forgetting that only the day before they had been killing each other in hideous fight, more like wild beasts than men. I looked at the English who rowed with as good a will as our own sailors, I saw in their faces the same tokens of fear or of hope, and above all the same expression, sacred to humanity, of kindness and fellowship which was the common motive of all. And as I noted it I said to myself: “Good God! why are there wars? Why cannot these men be friends under all the circumstances of life as they are in danger? Is not such a scene as this enough to prove that all men are brothers?”

But the idea of nationality suddenly occurred to me to cut short these speculations, and my geographical theory of islands. “To be sure,” said I to myself, “the islands must need want to rob each other of some portion of the land, and that is what spoils everything. And indeed there must be a great many bad men there who make wars for their own advantage, because they are ambitious and wish for power, or are avaricious and wish for wealth. It is these bad men who deceive the rest﻿—all the miserable creatures who do the fighting for them; and to make the fraud complete, they set them against other nations, sow discord and foment envy﻿—and here you see the consequences. I am certain”﻿—added I to myself, “that this can never go on; I will bet two to one that before long the inhabitants of the different islands will be convinced that they are committing a great folly in making such tremendous wars, and that a day will come when they will embrace each other and all agree to be like one family.” So I thought then; and now, after sixty years of life, I have not seen that day dawn.

===Chinese===
小船继续前行——可是驶向何方？连马西亚尔自己也说不清，他究竟把船往哪里掌。夜黑得严严实实，其余的小船早望不见了；*王子号*上的灯火隔着浓雾，就像被一阵狂风吹熄了似的，全无踪影。浪头那么高，风头那么猛，我们这条脆弱的小艇几乎挪不动步；多亏掌舵的手艺高明，她只有一回灌进了海水。我们都默然坐着，大半的人把忧郁的目光，钉在我们揣想的那片海面上——我们撇下的伙伴，此刻正在那里作垂死的挣扎。在这段航程中，我照老习惯，免不了生出些感想——我斗胆称之为哲学的感想。有人要笑话一位十四岁的哲学家了；可我不理会这笑声；我要把这当口盘踞我心头的念头写下来。孩子也照样能想大事；而在这样的时刻、面对这样的景象，除非是白痴的脑袋，谁的脑子能无动于衷？

我们船上英国人和西班牙人都有——不过多半是西班牙人——眼看着他们亲如手足：同在危难之中，你帮我扶、彼此打气，全然忘了就在前一天，他们还在恶战中互相残杀，那模样与其说像人，不如说像野兽，真叫人称奇。我瞧着那些英国人，他们划桨同我们自家水手一样尽心出力；我看见他们脸上同样流露出恐惧或希望的神色，而尤其难得的，是同样有一种仁爱与同袍之情的表情——那是人性中神圣的东西，是全船人共同的心念。我把这一切看在眼里，暗自说道：“老天爷！为什么要有战争？这些人同处险境时能做朋友，为什么在人生的一切境遇里就做不成？眼前这一幕，难道还不足以证明天下人都是兄弟吗？”

可是「国籍」这个念头忽然浮上心来，打断了这番玄想，也打断了我那套关于岛屿的地理学理论。“可不是，”我对自己说，“各个岛子必定总想抢别家的一块地皮，坏事就坏在这里面。再说，那些岛上一定有许多坏人，为自家的好处制造战争——他们或者野心勃勃，想要权势；或者贪得无厌，想要财富。正是这些坏人欺骗了其余的人——欺骗了那些替他们打仗的可怜虫；为把骗局做圆，他们又挑动这些人去敌视别的国家，播弄不和，煽起嫉妒——喏，后果你这就瞧见了。我敢肯定，”我又对自己说，“这绝长不了；我敢拿二赔一打赌，用不了多久，各岛的居民便会醒悟：这样大动干戈地打仗，是天大的蠢事；总有一天，他们会彼此拥抱，全都愿意亲如一家。”我当时是这么想的；而如今，活了六十年，我还没见那一天露出一丝曙光。

===Original===
The launch labored on through the heavy sea. I believe that if only my master would have consented Marcial would have been quite ready to pitch the English overboard and steer the boat to Cádiz or the nearest coast, even at the imminent risk of foundering on the way. I fancy he had suggested something of the kind to Don Alonso, speaking in a low voice, and that my master wished to give him a lesson in honor, for I heard him say:

“We are prisoners, Marcial﻿—we are prisoners.”

The worst of it was that no vessel came in sight. The *Prince* had moved off, and no light on either side told us of the existence of an English ship. At last, however, we descried one at some distance and a few minutes later the vague outline came in sight of a ship before the storm, to our windward, and on the opposite tack to ours. Some thought it was a Frenchman, others said it was English; Marcial was sure she was a Spaniard. We pulled hard to meet her and were soon within speaking distance. Our men hailed her and the answer was in Spanish.

===Chinese===
大艇在汹涌的海面上艰难前行。我相信，只要我主人点个头，马西亚尔巴不得把那几个英国人掀下海去，把船径直开往加的斯或者最近的海岸，哪怕半路翻船、性命悬于一线，他也在所不惜。我琢磨着，他已低声向堂阿隆索递过这类主意，而我主人有心给他上一堂体面人的课，因为我听见他说：

“我们是俘虏，马西亚尔——我们是俘虏。”

最糟的是，四下里望不见一条船的影子。*王子号*已经开走了，两边都没有一点灯火，告诉我们哪里泊着英国船。不过到后来，我们终于在远处望见了一个影子；几分钟后，一条船模糊的轮廓显现出来——它赶在风暴前头行驶，占着我们的上风，舷向与我们的正相反。有人说是条法国船，有人说是英国船；马西亚尔一口咬定是条西班牙船。我们奋力划桨迎上去，不多时便到了喊话可闻的距离。我们的人朝它喊话，回答的是西班牙语。

===Original===
“It is the *San Agustín*,” said Marcial.

“The *San Agustín* was sunk,” said Don Alonso; “I believe it is the *Santa Ana* which was also captured.” In fact, as we got close, we all recognized the *Santa Ana* which had gone into action under the command of Álava. The English officers in charge immediately prepared to take us on board, and before long we were all safe and sound on deck.

The *Santa Ana*, 112 guns, had suffered severely, though not to such an extent as the *Santísima Trinidad*; for, though she had lost all her masts and her rudder, the hull was fairly sound. The *Santa Ana* survived the battle of Trafalgar eleven years, and would have lived much longer if she had not gone to the bottom for want of repairs in the bay of Havana, in 1816. She had behaved splendidly in the fight. She was commanded, as I have said, by Vice-admiral Álava leader of the van which, as the order of battle was altered, became the rear. As the reader knows, the line of English ships led by Collingwood attacked the Spanish rear while Nelson took the centre. The *Santa Ana*, only supported by the *Fougueux*, a Frenchman, had to fight the *Royal Sovereign* and four other English ships; and in spite of their unequal strength one side suffered as much as the other, for Collingwood’s ship was the first to retire and the *Euryalus* took her place. By all accounts the fighting was terrific, and the two great ships, whose masts were almost entangled, fired into each other for six hours until Álava and Gardoqui, both being wounded (Álava subsequently died), five officers and ninety-seven sailors being killed, besides more than 150 wounded, the *Santa Ana* was forced to surrender. The English took possession of her, but it was impossible to work her on account of her shattered condition, and the dreadful storm that rose during the night of the 21st; so when we went on board she was in a very critical, though not a desperate situation, floating at the mercy of the wind and waves and unable to make any course. From that moment I was greatly comforted by seeing that every face on board betrayed a dread of approaching death. They were all very sad and quiet, enduring with a solemn mien the disgrace of defeat and the sense of being prisoners. One circumstance I could not help observing, and that was that the English officers in charge of the ship were not by a great deal so polite or so kind as those sent on board the *Trinidad*; on the contrary, among those on the *Santa Ana* were some who were both stern and repellent, doing all they could to mortify us, exaggerating their own dignity and authority, and interfering in everything with the rudest impertinence. This greatly annoyed the captured crew, particularly the sailors; and I fancied I overheard many alarming murmurs of rebellion which would have been highly disquieting to the English if they had come to their ears.

===Chinese===
“是*圣阿古斯丁号*。”马西亚尔说。

“*圣阿古斯丁号*沉了，”堂阿隆索说，“依我看，这是*圣安娜号*——它也被俘了。”果然，等靠得近了，我们都认出了*圣安娜号*，它正是在阿拉瓦指挥下投入战斗的那条船。负责该舰的英国军官们立刻张罗着接我们上船，不多时，我们便都安然无恙地站上了甲板。

*圣安娜号*是一百一十二门炮的战舰，受创惨重，只是还不及*圣特立尼达号*那步田地；她虽失去了全部桅杆和舵，船壳却还算完好。特拉法尔加海战之后，*圣安娜号*又存续了十一年；若不是1816年因无人修理而沉没在哈瓦那湾，她本还可以再撑许多年。那一仗，她打得漂亮极了。如前所述，舰上的指挥是海军中将阿拉瓦——他原本统领前卫，只因战列次序一变，前卫成了后卫。读者已经知道，科林伍德率领的英国战列攻打的是西班牙后卫，纳尔逊则直取中军。*圣安娜号*只得到法国船*富盖号*（Fougueux）一舰支援，却要迎战*皇家主权号*和另外四艘英舰；尽管强弱悬殊，双方吃的亏却不相上下——科林伍德的座舰头一个退出战团，换*尤里亚勒斯号*（Euryalus）顶上。据各方所述，那场恶斗凶猛绝伦：两艘巨舰的桅杆几乎绞缠在一处，你轰我、我轰你对轰了六个钟头，直到阿拉瓦和加尔多基（Gardoqui）双双负伤（阿拉瓦后来伤重身亡），五名军官、九十七名水兵阵亡，伤者更在一百五十人以上，*圣安娜号*才被迫投降。英国人接管了她，只因她伤势太重，操驾不动，加之二十一日夜里又刮起那场可怕的风暴；所以等我们上船时，她处境虽危急，却尚未到绝境，只听凭风浪摆布，什么航向也走不成。从那一刻起，看到船上每一张脸都掩不住对逼近的死亡的恐惧，我心里竟大得安慰。众人都凄凄默默，以庄严的神情，忍受着战败的耻辱与身为俘虏的况味。有一桩情形我不能不留意到：管舰的这些英国军官，远不如派到*特立尼达号*上去的那批人客气、和善；恰恰相反，*圣安娜号*上这些人里，有几位既严厉又面目可憎，千方百计折辱我们，把自家的威严权柄摆弄得十足，事事插手，粗横无礼之至。这大大激怒了被俘的船员，尤其是水手们；我恍惚偷听得许多不祥的窃窃私语，声声怀着反意——这些话若传进英国人耳朵里，准叫他们惶惶不安。

===Original===
Beyond this there is nothing to tell of our progress that night﻿—if progress it can be called when we were driven at the will of the wind and waves, sailless and rudderless. Nor do I wish to weary the reader with a repetition of the scenes we had witnessed on board the *Trinidad*, so I will go on to other and newer incidents which will surprise him as much as they did me.

I had lost my liking for hanging about the deck and poop, and as soon as we got on board the *Santa Ana* I took shelter in the cabin with my master, hoping to get food and rest, both of which I needed sorely. However, I found there many wounded who required constant attention and this duty, which I gladly fulfilled, prevented my getting the sleep which my wearied frame required. I was engaged in placing a bandage on Don Alonso’s arm when a hand was laid on my shoulder. I turned round and saw a tall young officer wrapped in a large blue cloak whom I did not immediately recognize; but after gazing at him for a few seconds, I exclaimed aloud with surprise; it was Don Rafael Malespina, my young mistress’s lover.

My master embraced him affectionately and he sat down by us. He had been wounded in the shoulder, and was so pale from fatigue and loss of blood that his face looked quite altered. His presence here filled me with strange sensations﻿—some of which I am fain to own were anything rather than pleasing. At first I felt glad enough indeed to see anyone I knew and who had come out alive from those scenes of horror, but the next moment my old aversion for this man rose up, as strong as ever in my breast, like some dormant pain reviving to torment me after an interval of respite. I confess with shame that I was sorry to see him safe and sound, but I must do myself the justice to add that the regret was but momentary, as brief as a lightning flash﻿—a flash of blackness, as I may say, darkening my soul; or rather a transient eclipse of the light of conscience which shone clearly again in the next instant. The evil side of my nature for a moment came uppermost; but I was able to suppress it at once and drive it down again to the depths whence it had come. Can everyone say as much?

After this brief mental struggle I could look at Malespina, glad that he was alive and sorry that he was hurt; and I remember, not without pride, that I did all I could to show him my feelings. Poor little mistress! How terrible must her anguish have been all this time. My heart overflowed with pitiful kindness at the thought﻿—I could have run all the way to Vejer to say: “Señorita Doña Rosa, your Don Rafael is safe and sound.”

===Chinese===
除此以外，那一夜的「行程」再没有什么可讲——无帆无舵、任凭风浪驱遣，这若也算得行程的话。我也不愿拿我们在*特立尼达号*上已见识过的场面，再来絮絮叨叨搅扰读者，所以这就接着讲些别样新鲜的事：它们当初怎样令我惊异，也便会怎样令读者惊异。

我早没了在甲板和艉楼上闲逛的兴头，一上*圣安娜号*，便随主人躲进舱房，指望弄点吃食、歇息歇息——两样我都急缺。谁知舱里尽是伤员，时刻离不得人照应；这份差事我干得心甘情愿，却害得我那困乏已极的身子捞不着觉睡。我正给堂阿隆索的胳膊上绷带，一只手落在我肩上。我回头一看，是位高个子的年轻军官，裹一件蓝色大氅，一时没认出来；盯着瞧了几秒钟，我惊呼出声——竟是堂拉斐尔·马莱斯皮纳，我那位小女主人的心上人。

主人亲亲热热地拥抱了他，他在我们身旁坐下。他肩上挂了彩，疲惫与失血熬得他面无人色，模样都变了。他在此处出现，我心里涌起种种古怪的滋味——有几样，我不得不承认，实在说不上愉快。起初我确是高兴的：好歹见到一个熟人，一个从那些恐怖场面里活着出来的人；可下一瞬，我对这人的旧日嫌恶便冒了上来，在我胸中还同往常一样强烈，像一处蛰伏的旧痛，喘息甫定，又醒转来折磨我。我羞愧地承认，见他安然无恙，我心里竟觉出几分遗憾；但为我自己说句公道话，那遗憾不过一瞬，短促得像一道闪电——不妨说，是一道黑闪电，罩暗了我的灵魂；或者说，是良心之光遭了一眨眼的亏蚀，下一刻重又明明亮亮。我天性里恶的那一面一时冒了头；可我当即把它压了下去，赶回它冒出来的深渊里。这话，可是人人都说得起么？

经过这番短短的心里交战，我再朝马莱斯皮纳看去时，已能为他活着而喜、为他负伤而悲了；我记得——而且并非没有几分骄傲——我尽了全力，把这些情意表露给他看。可怜的小女主人！这些时日里，她的煎熬该有多可怕啊。一想到她，我满心涌起怜惜的柔情——我恨不得一口气跑到韦赫尔，去对她说：“堂娜·罗莎小姐，您的堂拉斐尔平安无恙。”

===Original===
The luckless Malespina had been brought on board the *Santa Ana* from the *Nepomuceno*, which had also been captured, and with so many wounded on board that it had been necessary, as we learned, to distribute them or they must have perished of neglect. When the father and his daughter’s fiancé had exchanged the first greetings and spoken of the absent ones on shore, the conversation turned on the details of the battle. My master related all that had occurred on board the *Trinidad* and then he added: “But no one has told me exactly what has become of Gravina. Was he taken prisoner, or has he got off to Cádiz?”

“The Admiral,” said Malespina, “stood a terrific fire from the *Defiance* and the *Revenge*. The *Neptune*, a Frenchman, came to her assistance with the *San Ildefonso* and the *San Justo*; but our enemies were reinforced by the *Dreadnought*, the *Thunderer*, and the *Polyphemus*; so that resistance was hopeless. Seeing the *Príncipe de Astúrias* with all her tackle cut, her masts overboard and her sides riddled with balls, while Gravina himself and Escaño, his second in command, were both wounded, they resolved on giving up the struggle which was quite in vain for the battle was lost. Gravina hoisted the signal to retire on the stump of a mast and sailed off for Cádiz, followed by the *San Justo*, the *San Leandro*, the *Montañes* and three others; only regretting their inability to rescue the *San Ildefonso* which had fallen into the hands of the enemy.”

“But tell us what happened on board the *Nepomuceno*,” said my master, deeply interested. “I can hardly believe that Churruca can be dead; and, though everyone tells me that he is, I cannot help fancying that that wonderful man must still be alive somewhere on earth.”

===Chinese===
倒霉的马莱斯皮纳，是从*圣胡安·内波穆塞诺号*上转到*圣安娜号*来的——那条船也被俘了，船上伤员多得照应不过来，据我们后来得知，只得把伤员分送各船，不然他们必会因无人照管而丧命。做父亲的同女儿的这位未婚夫寒暄过头几句，又说了说岸上挂念的人，话题便转到这一仗的种种细节上。我主人讲了*特立尼达号*上的全部遭遇，接着又添上一句：“可谁也没有确切告诉我，格拉维纳后来怎么样了。他是被俘了，还是脱身回了加的斯？”

“海军上将，”马莱斯皮纳说，“顶住了*迪法恩斯号*（Defiance）和*复仇号*（Revenge）一场惊天动地的炮火。法国船*尼普顿号*带着*圣伊尔德丰索号*和*圣胡斯托号*（San Justo）赶来救援；可敌人那边又添了*无畏号*（Dreadnought）、*雷鸣号*（Thunderer）和*波吕斐摩斯号*（Polyphemus），抵抗便全无指望了。眼见*阿斯图里亚斯亲王号*索具尽数砍断、桅杆落水、船身被炮弹打得百孔千疮，而格拉维纳本人和他的副手埃斯卡尼奥又都负了伤，众人只得罢手——再打也是枉然，败局已定。格拉维纳在一截桅桩上升起撤退信号，扬帆驶回加的斯，*圣胡斯托号*、*圣莱安德罗号*（San Leandro）、*蒙塔涅斯号*（Montañes）和另外三条船跟在后头；只可惜*圣伊尔德丰索号*已落入敌手，救它不得，众人引为憾事。”

“快给我们讲讲*圣胡安·内波穆塞诺号*上的事吧，”我主人说，兴致极深。“丘鲁卡死了——我简直不能相信；虽说人人都对我说他死了，我总不由得想，那样一位奇人，必定还在世上什么地方活着。”

===Original===
But Malespina told him that it had been his misfortune to see Churruca killed and said he would relate every detail. A few officers gathered round him while I, as curious as they could be, was all ears in order not to lose a syllable.

“Even as we came out of Cádiz,” said Malespina, “Churruca had a presentiment of disaster. He had voted against sailing out to sea, for he knew the inferiority of our armament, and he also had little confidence in Villeneuve’s skill and judgment. All his predictions were verified﻿—all, even to his own death: for there is no doubt that he had foreseen it as surely as he did our defeat. On the 19th he had said to Apodaca, his brother-in-law, before going on board: ‘Sooner than surrender my ship, I will blow her up or go to the bottom. That is the duty of every man who serves his king and country.’ And the same day, writing to a friend, he said: ‘If you hear that my ship is taken you will know that I am dead.’”

“Indeed it was legible in his sad grave face that he looked forward to nothing but a catastrophe. I believe that this conviction, and the absolute impossibility of avoiding defeat while feeling himself strong enough for his own part, seriously weighed upon his mind, for he was as capable of great deeds as he was of noble thoughts.”

===Chinese===
马莱斯皮纳却告诉他，自己不幸亲眼见到了丘鲁卡之死，并说愿把细节一一道来。几位军官围拢过来；我呢，好奇心不输他们任何一人，竖起耳朵，一个音节也不想漏掉。

“还在加的斯出海的当口，”马莱斯皮纳说，“丘鲁卡就预感到了大祸。出海一事付诸表决时，他投了反对票，因为他深知我方军备之差，也不大信得过维尔纳夫的能耐与判断。他的预言全都应验了——全都应验，连他自己的死也不例外：毫无疑问，他把自己的死，预见得同对战败一样确凿。十九日登舰之前，他对内弟阿波达卡（Apodaca）说：‘与其交出我的船，我宁可炸沉它，或者随它一同沉入海底。这是每一个为国王和祖国效力的人的本分。’同一天，他写信给一位朋友，说：‘如果你听说我的船被夺去了，你就知道我已经死了。’”

“的确，他那张悲怆肃穆的脸上，明明白白读得出：他指望的只是一场浩劫，别无其他。我相信，这一确信沉沉压在他心头——明知败局绝对无从避免，却又自觉尽够一己之职分——因为他这个人，能怀多高尚的思想，便能建多大的功业，两样本事原是一样的。”

===Original===
“Churruca’s was a religious as well as a superior mind. On the 21st, at eleven in the morning, he called up all the soldiers and crew; he bid them all kneel and said to his chaplain in solemn tones: ‘Fulfil your function, holy Father, and absolve these brave souls that know not what this fight may have in store for them.’ When the priest had pronounced absolution Churruca desired them to stand up, and speaking in friendly but audible tones he added: ‘My children all:﻿—In God’s name I promise heavenly bliss to all who die doing their duty. If one of you shirks it he shall be shot on the spot; or, if he escapes my notice or that of the gallant officers I have the honor to command, his remorse shall pursue him so long as he crawls through the rest of his miserable and dishonored days.’

“This harangue, as eloquent as it was wise, combining the ideas of religion and of military duty, filled every man on board with enthusiasm. Alas for all these brave hearts!﻿—wasted like gold sunk at the bottom of the ocean! Face to face with the English, Churruca watched Villeneuve’s preliminary maneuvers with entire disapproval, and when the signal was given for the whole fleet to turn about﻿—a maneuver which, as we know, reversed the order of battle﻿—he told his captain in so many words that this blunder had lost us the day. He immediately understood the masterly plan struck out by Nelson of cutting our line through the centre from the rear, and engaging the whole fleet at once, dealing with our ships in separate divisions so that they could not assist each other.”

===Chinese===
“丘鲁卡的头脑超群出众，同时又是一片虔诚。二十一日上午十一点，他把全体士兵和船员召集上来，命他们一齐跪下，以庄重的声调对随舰司铎说：‘圣父，请履行您的职分，赦免这些勇敢的灵魂吧——他们还不知道，这场战斗会给他们安排下什么。’司铎宣罢赦罪文，丘鲁卡请大家起立，用亲切而人人听得见的声音接着说：‘我的孩子们：我奉上帝的名许诺——凡恪尽职守而死的，必得天堂的至福。你们中若有谁规避职守，就地枪决；倘若他侥幸逃过我的眼睛，或逃过我有幸统率的这些英勇军官的眼睛，那么良心的谴责也要追着他不放，追到他在余下那可怜而不名誉的岁月里，爬完最后一寸为止。’

“这番训话，既明智又有雄辩之力，把宗教的信条同军人的天职熔于一炉，舰上人人热血沸腾。可叹啊，这些勇敢的心！——竟像沉进海底的黄金，白白糟蹋了！同英国人照面之后，丘鲁卡眼看着维尔纳夫的种种预备机动，满心的不以为然；及至升起全舰队掉头转向的信号——我们知道，正是这一机动把战列次序整个颠倒——他直截了当地对他的舰长说：正是这个昏着儿，断送了我们这一天。纳尔逊那套大师手笔的方略，他一眼看穿：从后卫切入，拦腰斩断我们的战列，一上来便逼全部舰只接战，再把我们的船分截成数段各个击破，叫它们彼此不能相救。”

===Original===
“The *Nepomuceno* was at the end of the line. The *Royal Sovereign* and the *Santa Ana* opened fire and then all the ships in turn came into action. Five English vessels under Collingwood attacked our ship; two, however, passed on and Churruca had only three to deal with.

“We held out bravely against these odds till two in the afternoon, suffering terribly, however, though we dealt double havoc on the foe. Our Admiral seemed to have infused his heroic spirit into the crew and soldiers, and the ship was handled and the broadsides delivered with terrible promptitude and accuracy. The new recruits had learned their lesson in courage in no more than a couple of hours’ apprenticeship, and our defence struck the English not merely with dismay but with astonishment.

“They were in fact forced to get assistance and bring up no less than six against one. The two ships that had at first sailed past now returned, and the *Dreadnought* came alongside of us, with not more than half a pistol-shot between her and our stern. You may imagine the fire of these six giants pouring balls and small shot into a vessel of 74 guns. But our ship seemed positively to grow bigger in proportion to the desperate bravery of her defenders. They themselves seemed to grow in strength as their courage mounted, and seeing the dismay we created in an enemy six times as strong, we could have believed ourselves something more than men.”

===Chinese===
“*圣胡安·内波穆塞诺号*排在战列末尾。*皇家主权号*和*圣安娜号*率先开火，随后各舰依次投入战斗。科林伍德麾下有五艘英舰攻打我们这条船；不过其中两艘径直开了过去，丘鲁卡要对付的只有三艘。

“我们顶着这样的众寡之势，英勇支撑到下午两点，自己受创惨重，给敌人造成的杀伤却加倍。我们这位上将，仿佛把他英豪的气概灌注进了全体水兵和士兵身上：船操得灵，齐放射得又快又准，煞是骇人。新兵们只消两个钟头的‘学徒期’，便把勇敢这一课学到了家；我们的抵抗，叫英国人不只是胆寒，简直是惊异。

“他们果然不得不搬取援手，竟至驱六敌一。起先开过去的那两艘，这时折了回来，*无畏号*更贴到我们旁边，同我们舰尾相距不过半个手枪射程。六艘巨人战舰，朝一条七十四门炮的船倾泻实心弹与霰弹，那阵炮火您可想而知。可我们的船，竟像是随着守卫者们拼死的英勇水涨船高，越变越大。他们自己的膂力，也仿佛随着勇气的升腾而增长；眼见六倍于己的敌人被我们搅得心惊胆战，我们简直要相信，自己已是超乎凡人的存在了。”

===Original===
“Churruca, meanwhile, who was the brain of us all, directed the action with gloomy calmness. Knowing that only care and skill could supply the place of strength he economized our fire, trusting entirely to careful aim, and the consequence was that each ball did terrible havoc on the foe. He saw to everything, settled everything, and the shot flew round him and over his head without his ever once changing color even. That frail and delicate man, whose beautiful and melancholy features looked so little fitted to dare such scenes of terror, inspired us all with unheard-of courage, simply by a glance of his eye.

“However, it was not the will of God that he should escape alive from that storm of fire. Seeing that no one could hit one of the enemy’s ships which was battering us with impunity, he went down himself to judge of the line of fire and succeeded in dismasting her. He was returning to the quarterdeck when a cannonball hit his right leg with such violence as almost to take it off, tearing it across the thigh in the most frightful manner. We rushed to support him and our hero sank into my arms. It was a fearful moment. I still fancy I can feel his heart beating under my hand﻿—a heart which, even at that terrible moment, beat only for his country. He sank rapidly. I saw him make an effort to raise his head, which had fallen forward on his breast; I saw him try to force a smile while his face was as white as death, and he said, in a voice that was scarcely weaker than usual: ‘It is nothing﻿—go on firing.’”

===Chinese===
“而这时的丘鲁卡，是我们全船的头脑，他以阴郁的镇定指挥着战斗。他深知唯有细心与技艺能顶替力量，因而惜炮如金，全凭瞄得准下功夫，结果每一颗炮弹都给敌人造成可怕的杀伤。他事事过问，事事裁定；炮弹在他四周、在他头顶呼啸飞过，他连脸色也不曾变过一回。这个羸弱纤秀的人，那副俊美而忧郁的眉目，看上去最不像敢闯这般恐怖场面的人，却只消眼风一扫，便给我们所有人注入闻所未闻的勇气。

“然而，上帝的旨意，没有让他活着逃出那场弹火的风暴。眼见敌舰中有一艘，打我们打得肆无忌惮，谁都奈何它不得，他便亲自下去判明弹道，一举打掉了它的桅杆。他正要回到后甲板，一发炮弹击中他的右腿——力道之猛，几乎整条掀落，把大腿撕得稀烂，惨不忍睹。我们抢上前去搀扶，我们的英雄倒在了我的怀里。那是可怕的一刻。直到今天，我仿佛还觉得，他的心脏在我手底下跳动——那颗心，纵然在那可怕的时刻，也只为他的祖国而跳。他迅速衰弱下去。我看见他挣扎着抬起垂在胸前的那颗头颅；看见他强作笑颜，脸上已白得同死人一般；他说，嗓音几乎不比平常更弱：‘不要紧——接着开火。’”

===Original===
“His spirit revolted against death and he did all he could to conceal the terrible sufferings of his mutilated frame, while his heart beat more feebly every instant. We wanted to carry him down into the cabin, but nothing would persuade him to quit the quarterdeck. At last he yielded to our entreaties and understood that he must give up the command. He called for Moyna, his lieutenant, and was told that he was dead; then he called for the officer in command of the first battery, and the latter though himself seriously wounded at once mounted the quarterdeck and assumed the command.

“But from that moment the men lost heart; from giants they shrank to pygmies; their courage was worn out and it was plain that we must surrender. The consternation that had possessed me from the instant when our hero fell into my arms had not prevented my observing the terrible effect that this disaster had produced in the minds of all. A sudden paralysis of soul and body seemed to have fallen on the crew; they all stood petrified and speechless and the grief of losing their beloved leader quite overpowered the disgrace of surrender.

“Quite half of the men were dead or wounded; most of the guns were past serving; all the masts except the mainmast were gone by the board and the rudder could not be used. Even in this deplorable plight we made an attempt to follow the *Príncipe de Astúrias* which had given the signal to retreat, but the *Nepomuceno* was mortally wounded and could not move nor steer. Even then, in spite of the wrecked state of the ship, in spite of the dismayed condition of the men, in spite of a concurrence of circumstances to render our case hopeless, not one of the six English captains attempted to board us. They respected our ship even when she was at their mercy.”

===Chinese===
“他的魂灵不肯向死亡低头；残躯苦痛万状，他却竭力遮掩，而他的心跳，一刻比一刻微弱。我们要把他抬进舱房，可说什么也劝不动他离开后甲板。最后他拗不过众人的恳求，也明白自己必须交出指挥权了。他唤他的副官莫伊纳（Moyna），得到的回答是：莫伊纳已经阵亡；他又唤第一炮组的指挥军官，那位军官虽也身负重伤，还是立刻登上后甲板，接过了指挥。

“可打那一刻起，众人便泄了气；巨人缩成了侏儒；他们的勇气耗尽了，明摆着，我们只得投降。自我们的英雄倒进我怀里那一瞬起，我便魂飞天外；但这惊愕并不曾妨碍我看清，这场大难在众人心头造成的可怕后果。全船上下，仿佛突然中了灵魂与肉体的麻痹：人人僵立无言；痛失爱戴的统帅之悲，把投降的耻辱整个压了下去。

“死伤的人几乎到了半数；大炮多半瘫了；除主桅外，桅杆全都倒进了海里，舵也使唤不动。纵然落到这般凄惨境地，我们还试图跟随已发出撤退信号的*阿斯图里亚斯亲王号*；但*圣胡安·内波穆塞诺号*已受了致命伤，动弹不得，也无从转向。即便到了那时——不顾船已残破、人心已惶乱、种种情势凑在一处，使我们的处境全无指望——六位英国舰长，没有一人试图跳帮夺船。我们的船纵然已任凭他们摆布，他们仍对它存着敬意。”

===Original===
“Churruca, in the midst of his agony, ordered that the flag should be nailed to the mast, for the ship should never surrender so long as he breathed. The delay alas! could be but brief, for Churruca was going rapidly, and we who supported him only wondered that a body so mangled could still breathe; it was his indomitable spirit that kept him alive added to a resolute determination to live, for he felt it his first duty. He never lost consciousness till the very end, nor complained of his sufferings, nor seemed to dread his approaching death; his sole care and anxiety was that the crew should not know how dangerous his condition was, so that no one should fail in his duty. He desired that the men should be thanked for their heroic bravery, spoke a few words to his brother-in-law, Ruiz de Apodaca, and, after sending a message to his young wife he fixed his thoughts on God, whose name we heard frequently on his parched lips, and died with the calm resignation of a just man and the fortitude of a hero; bereft of the satisfaction of victory but with no angry sense of defeat. In him duty and dignity were equally combined, and discipline was second only to religion. As a soldier he was resolute, as a man he was resigned, and without a murmur or an accusing word he died as nobly as he had lived. We looked at his body, not yet cold, and it seemed all a delusion﻿—he must surely wake to give us our orders; and we wept with less fortitude than he had shown in dying, for in him we had lost all the valor and enthusiasm that had borne us up.

“Well, the ship struck; and when the officers from the six vessels that had destroyed her came on board each claimed the honor of receiving the sword of our dead hero. Each exclaimed: ‘He surrendered to me!’﻿—and for a few minutes they eagerly disputed the victory, each for the ship he represented. Then they asked the officer who had taken the command to which of the Englishmen he had struck. ‘To all,’ he replied. ‘The *Nepomuceno* would never have surrendered to one.’”

===Chinese===
“弥留的剧痛之中，丘鲁卡还下令把舰旗钉死在桅杆上——只要他还有一口气，这条船就绝不投降。唉，这口气终究拖不长：丘鲁卡眼看着快不行了，搀扶他的人只是惊奇，那样一具血肉模糊的躯体，居然还能喘息；支撑他活着的，是他那不屈的魂灵，再加一股执意求生的决心——他把活下去，当作自己的头一桩本分。直到最后，他不曾昏迷，不曾叫一声苦，也不见对逼近的死亡有半分畏怯；他唯一挂心、唯一焦虑的，是别让船员们知道他的伤势有多险，好教谁也不怠慢职守。他吩咐代他感谢弟兄们的英勇，同内弟鲁伊斯·德·阿波达卡（Ruiz de Apodaca）说了几句话，又给年轻的妻子留下口信，然后把心思系于上帝——那个圣名，我们频频听见它落在他焦干的唇上——便怀着义人的恬静顺从与英雄的坚毅死去：未得胜利的甘甜，却也无战败的怨愤。在他身上，天职与尊严合而为一，纪律仅次于宗教。作为军人，他坚毅果决；作为人，他安命顺受；他没有一句怨言、一声指摘，死得同活得一样高贵。我们望着他那尚未冷却的遗体，只觉这一切恍如虚妄——他必定还会醒转来，给我们发号施令；我们哭得远不如他赴死时那样坚忍，因为随着他，支撑我们挺立的一切勇气与热忱，都已随之而去。

“到头来，战舰降了旗；把她打垮的那六艘军舰的军官们上得船来，个个争领受我们阵亡英雄佩剑的荣光。人人抢着喊：‘他是向我投降的！’——有好几分钟，他们为这份战功你争我夺，各人都为自己所代表的军舰力争。随后他们问接过指挥的那位军官，他降的是哪一位英国人。‘向你们全体，’他答道，‘*圣胡安·内波穆塞诺号*决不会向单独一艘投降。’”

===Original===
“The English gazed with sincere emotion on the body of the hapless Churruca, for the fame of his courage and genius was known to them and one of them spoke to this effect: ‘A man of such illustrious qualities ought never to be exposed to the risks of battle; he should be kept to live and serve the interests of science and navigation.’ Then they prepared for dropping him overboard, the English marines and seamen forming a line of honor alongside of the Spaniards; they behaved throughout like noble-minded and magnanimous gentlemen.

“The number of our wounded was very considerable, and they were transferred on board other English or captured ships. It was my lot to be sent to this one which has suffered worse than most; however, they count more on getting her into Gibraltar than any other, now that they have lost the *Trinidad* which was the finest and most coveted of our ships.”

Thus ended Malespina’s narrative which was attentively listened to as being that of an eyewitness. From what I heard I understood that a tragedy just as fearful as that I myself had seen had been enacted on board every ship of the fleet. “Good God!” said I to myself, “what infinite misery! and all brought about by the obstinacy of a single man!” And child as I was, I remember thinking: “One man, however mad he may be, can never commit such extravagant follies as whole nations sometimes plunge into at the bidding of a hundred wise ones.”

===Chinese===
“英国人怀着真挚的哀情，凝视不幸的丘鲁卡的遗体——他的勇武与才略之名，他们早有耳闻；其中一位说了大意如此的话：‘这样一位才华盖世的人，本不该拿来冒战争的凶险；理当让他活着，为科学与航海的事业效力。’随后，他们准备将他交付海葬：英国的海军陆战队员与水手，同西班牙人并肩排成荣誉的仪仗；他们自始至终的举动，都像一派心地高尚、气度宽宏的绅士。

“我们的伤员人数极多，被分送到其他英国船和被俘的船上。我命定被分到这一条——它受的创比多数船都重；不过如今他们丢了*特立尼达号*——我们舰队里最精美、最惹人艳羡的一条船——便把弄进直布罗陀的指望，寄托在这条船上，比哪条都多。”

马莱斯皮纳的讲述到此完结；因为出自亲历者之口，众人都听得聚精会神。从这番话里我明白了：舰队每一条船上，都上演过一场同我亲眼所见同样可怕的悲剧。“老天爷！”我对自己说，“何等无边的苦难！而这一切，竟是一个人的刚愎自用惹出来的！”我记得，尽管还是个孩子，我当时便想：“一个人无论疯成什么地步，也闯不出整族整国的人有时听凭一百个聪明人撺掇、一头扎进去的那等倾国之祸。”

## XIV

===Original===
A large part of the night was spent in listening to Malespina’s narrative and the experiences of other officers. They were interesting enough to keep me awake and I was so excited that I found great difficulty afterwards in going to sleep at all. I could not get the image of Churruca out of my mind as I had seen him, handsome and strong, at Doña Flora’s house. On that occasion, even, I had been startled by the expression of intense sadness on the hero’s features, as if he had a sure presentiment of his near and painful death. His noble life had come to an untimely end when he was only forty-four years old, after twenty-nine years of honorable service as a soldier, a navigator, and a man of science—for Churruca was all of these, besides being a noble and cultivated gentlemen. I was still thinking of all these things when, at length, my brain surrendered to fatigue and I fell asleep on the morning of the 23rd, my youthful nature having got the better of my excitement and curiosity. But in my sleep, which was long if not quiet, I was still haunted by nightmare visions, as was natural in my overwrought state of mind, hearing the roar of cannon, the tumult of battle and the thunder of billows; meanwhile I fancied I was serving out ammunition, climbing the rigging, rushing about between decks to encourage the gunners and even standing on the quarterdeck in command of the vessel. I need hardly say that in this curious but visionary battle I routed all the English past, present, or to come, with as much ease as though their ships were made of paper and their cannonballs were bread-pills. I had a thousand men-of-war under my command, each larger than the *Trinidad*, and they moved before me with as much precision as the toy-ships with which I and my comrades had been wont to play in the puddles of La Caleta.

At last, however, all this glory faded away, which, as it was but a dream, is scarcely to be wondered at when we see how even the reality vanishes. It was all over when I opened my eyes and remembered how small a part I had actually played in the stupendous catastrophe I had witnessed. Still—strange to say—even when wide awake I heard cannon and the all-dreadful tumult of war, with shouts and a clatter that told of some great turmoil on deck. I thought I must still be dreaming; I sat up on the sofa on which I had fallen asleep; I listened with all my ears, and certainly a thundering shout of “God save the King” left no doubt in my mind that the *Santa Ana* was fighting once more.

I went out of the cabin and studied the situation. The weather had moderated; to the windward a few battered ships were in sight, and two of them, Englishmen, had opened fire on the *Santa Ana* which was defending herself with the aid of two others, a Frenchman and a Spaniard. I could not understand the sudden change in the aspect of affairs. Were we no longer prisoners of war? I looked up—our flag was flying in the place of the Union Jack. What could have happened?—or rather what was happening? For the drama was in progress.

===Chinese===
夜里很大一部分时光，都花在听马莱斯皮纳（Malespina）讲他的经历、听其他军官谈他们的见闻上。那些故事足够引人入胜，听得我毫无睡意，而且我兴奋过度，过后想真正入睡更是难上加难。丘鲁卡（Churruca）的形象在我脑中挥之不去，正如我在堂娜·弗洛拉（Doña Flora）家中见到的那样：英俊而健壮。甚至在那时候，这位英雄眉宇间那种深切的哀伤就已令我不安，仿佛他对即将来临的惨死有着确凿的预感。他高贵的生命在不该终结的时候终结了，年仅四十四岁；此前他作为军人、航海家和科学家，已经光荣地效力了二十九年——因为丘鲁卡兼三者于一身，此外还是一位高贵而有教养的绅士。我还在想着这一切，脑子终于向疲惫缴了械，在二十三日清晨睡着了——少年的天性终究压过了兴奋与好奇。可这一觉虽长却不安稳，噩梦仍纠缠不休——以我那过度亢奋的心绪，这也是自然——我听见炮声怒吼、战事喧嚣、波涛如雷；恍惚间又仿佛自己在分发弹药、攀爬桅索、在甲板之间奔走为炮手打气，甚至站上后甲板指挥全舰。不消说，在这场离奇而虚幻的战斗中，我把过去、现在和将来的英国人统统打得溃不成军，轻松得好比他们的军舰是纸糊的、炮弹是面包搓成的丸子。我麾下拥有上千艘战舰，每一艘都比*圣特立尼达号*（Trinidad）还要大，它们在我面前进退转动，精准得就像我和伙伴们从前在卡莱塔（La Caleta）水洼里玩惯了的小船模型。

然而，这一切荣耀终于烟消云散——那不过是一场梦，本也不足为怪，须知连现实本身又是多么容易消散。待我睁开眼睛，想起自己在亲眼目睹的那场惊天大祸里其实只扮演了多么微小的角色，一切便都结束了。可是——说来奇怪——即便完全醒着，我仍听得见炮声和那可怕之极的战争喧嚣，还有喊声与骚动，说明甲板上正有一场大乱。我以为自己准还在做梦；我在睡倒的沙发上坐起身来；我竖起耳朵细听，一声“上帝保佑国王！”的雷霆般的呐喊确凿无疑地告诉我：*圣安娜号*（Santa Ana）又开仗了。

我走出舱房，观察形势。风浪已经缓和了些；上风处望得见几艘被打得残破的船，其中两艘英国船已向*圣安娜号*开了火，而它正借着另外两艘船——一艘法国船、一艘西班牙船——之助自卫。我实在不明白局势怎么突变得这样。我们不再是战俘了吗？我抬头望去——我们的旗子正飘扬在原先挂米字旗的地方。出了什么事？——不如说，正在出什么事？因为这出戏正演到半场。

===Original===
On the quarterdeck stood a man who, I concluded, must be Álava, and though suffering from several wounds he still had strength enough to command this second action, which seemed likely enough to recover the honor his good ship had lost in the disaster of the first. The officers were encouraging the sailors who were serving those guns that could still be worked, while a detachment kept guard over the English, who had been disarmed and shut up in the lower deck. Their officers who had been our jailers were now become our prisoners.

I understood it all. The brave commander of the *Santa Ana*, Don Ignacio de Alva, seeing that we were within hail of some Spanish ships, which had come out of Cádiz in hope of rescuing some of our captured vessels and to take off the survivors from such as might be sinking, had addressed a stirring harangue to his disheartened crew who responded to his enthusiasm by a supreme effort. By a sudden rush they had disarmed the English who were in charge and hoisted the Spanish flag once more. The *Santa Ana* was free, but she had to fight for life, a more desperate struggle perhaps than the first had been.

This bold attempt—one of the most honorable episodes of the battle of Trafalgar—was made on board a dismasted ship, that had lost her rudder, with half her complement of men killed or wounded, and the other half in a wretched condition both moral and physical. However, the deed once done we had to face the consequences; two Englishmen, considerably battered no doubt, fired on the *Santa Ana*; but the *Asís*, the *Montañes*, and the *Rayo*—three ships that had got off with Gravina on the 21st—opportunely came to the rescue, having come out with a view to recapturing the prizes. The brave cripples rushed into the desperate action, with even more courage perhaps than into the former battle, for their unhealed wounds spurred them to fury and they seemed to fight with greater ardor in proportion as they had less life to lose.

All the incidents of the dreadful 21st were repeated before my eyes; the enthusiasm was tremendous, but the hands were so few that twice the will and energy were needed. This heroic action fills indeed but a brief page in history, for, by the side of the great event which is now known as the Battle of Trafalgar, such details are dwarfed or disappear altogether like a transient spark in a night of gloom and horror.

===Chinese===
后甲板上站着一个人，我断定那准是阿拉瓦（Álava）；他虽身负几处伤，仍有足够的气力指挥这第二场战斗——这一仗看来大有希望为他那艘好船在第一场灾难里丢掉的荣誉扳回来。军官们正在给水手们打气，那些人伺候着尚能开火的几门炮；另有一队人看守着英国人——他们已被缴械，关在下层甲板里。他们的军官，先前是我们的看守，如今反倒成了我们的俘虏。

我全明白了。*圣安娜号*那位勇敢的指挥官堂伊格纳西奥·德·阿拉瓦（Don Ignacio de Alva），见我们已驶入几艘西班牙船的喊话距离之内——那些船是驶出加的斯来的，指望夺回几艘被俘的舰只、并从正在下沉的船上接走幸存者——便向他灰心丧气的部下发表了一篇激动人心的训话，全船人以其拼死之力回应了他的热忱。他们一阵突袭，缴了看管船只的英国人的械，重新升起了西班牙旗。*圣安娜号*自由了，但它还得为性命而战——这一仗也许比第一场更加凶险。

这大胆的壮举——特拉法尔加海战中最光荣的篇章之一——是在一艘桅杆尽折、舵已丢失的船上干成的，全舰员额已有一半死伤，另一半身心俱已困顿不堪。然而事已做成，就得承担后果；两艘英国船——无疑也挨得够呛——向*圣安娜号*开了火；但*阿西斯号*（Asís）、*蒙塔涅斯号*（Montañés）和*拉约号*（Rayo）——二十一日随格拉维纳（Gravina）脱身的三艘船——及时赶来救援，它们出海原就是为了夺回这些战利品的。这些勇敢的伤兵投入了这场殊死的战斗，那份勇气或许比上一仗还足，因为未愈的伤口激得他们狂怒，他们似乎越是没剩下多少性命可丢，厮杀得反倒越加激烈。

可怕的二十一日的种种情景在我眼前重演了一遍；士气高涨到了极点，只是人手实在太少，需要双倍的意志和气力才能顶上。这一壮举在历史上确乎只占短短的一页，因为在那桩如今被称为特拉法尔加海战的伟业旁边，这类细节显得渺小，或者干脆湮没无闻，如同阴森恐怖的黑夜里一闪即逝的火花。

===Original===
The next thing that happened to me personally cost me some bitter tears. Not finding my master at once I felt sure he was in some danger, so I went down to the upper gun-deck and there I found him, training a cannon. His trembling hand had snatched the linstock from that of a wounded sailor and he was trying, with the feeble sight of his right eye, to discover to what point in the foe he had better send the missile. When the piece went off he turned to me trembling with satisfaction, and said in a scarcely audible voice:

“Ah ha! Paca need not laugh at me now. We shall return to Cádiz in triumph.”

Finally we won the fight. The English perceived the impossibility of recapturing the *Santa Ana* when, besides the three ships already mentioned, two other Frenchmen and a frigate came up to her assistance in the very thick of the fray.

===Chinese===
接下来落在我个人头上的一件事，让我流了不少苦涩的眼泪。我一时间找不到主人，断定他准是遇了险，便下到上层炮甲板，果然在那里找到了他——他正在给一门炮瞄准。他颤抖的手从一个伤兵手里夺过火绳杆，正凭着右眼那点可怜的视力，琢磨着该把炮弹送到敌人身上的哪个部位。炮一响，他转过身来，满意得浑身发抖，用几乎听不见的声音对我说：

“啊哈！这会儿帕卡（Paca）可没法笑话我了。我们要凯旋加的斯了。”

这一仗我们终于打赢了。英国人眼见——除了先前三舰之外——又有两艘法国船和一艘巡防舰在鏖战最酣之际赶来援助*圣安娜号*，便明白再想夺回它是断无可能了。

===Original===
We were free, and by a glorious effort; but at the very moment of victory we saw most clearly the peril we were in, for the *Santa Ana* was now so completely disabled that we could only be towed into Cádiz. The French frigate *Themis* sent a cable on board and put her head to the North, but what could she do with such a deadweight in tow as the *Santa Ana*, which could do little enough to help herself with the ragged sails that still clung to her one remaining mast? The other ships that had supported her—the *Rayo*, the *Montañes*, and the *San Francisco de Asís*, were forced to proceed at full sail to the assistance of the *San Juan* and the *Bahama*, which were also in the hands of the English. There we were, alone, with no help but the frigate that was doing her best for us—a child leading a giant. What would become of us if the enemy—as was very probable—recovering from their repulse, were to fall upon us with renewed energy and reinforcements? However, Providence thought good to protect us; the wind favored us, and our frigate gently leading the way, we found ourselves nearing Cádiz.

Only five leagues from port! What an unspeakable comfort! Our miseries seemed ended; ere long we should set foot on terra firma, and though we brought news no doubt of a terrible disaster, we were bringing relief and joy to many faithful souls who were suffering mortal anguish in the belief that those who were returning alive and well had all perished.

===Chinese===
我们自由了，而且是凭一场光荣的努力赢来的；可就在胜利的那一刻，我们最清楚地看清了自己的险境，因为*圣安娜号*此刻已彻底瘫了，只能被拖回加的斯。法国巡防舰*忒弥斯号*（Themis）送过来一根缆绳，把船头调向北去；可拖着*圣安娜号*这样一个死沉的累赘，它又能有什么办法？何况*圣安娜号*凭着仅存那根桅杆上挂着的破帆，自己能出的力也微乎其微。先前支援它的那几艘船——*拉约号*、*蒙塔涅斯号*和*圣弗朗西斯科·德·阿西斯号*（San Francisco de Asís）——只得满帆赶去救援*圣胡安号*（San Juan）和*巴哈马号*（Bahama），那两艘也落在了英国人手里。我们就这样孤零零地待着，只有那艘巡防舰在为我们尽力——一个小孩子领着一个巨人。倘若敌人——这委实大有可能——从被击退中缓过劲来，带着新的气力和增援扑到我们头上，我们会落个什么下场？然而上天发了善心要护佑我们；风帮了我们，我们的巡防舰在前头轻轻领路，我们发觉自己离加的斯越来越近了。

离港口只有五里格了！这是何等难以言表的安慰！我们的苦难似乎到了头；不久我们就要踏上陆地，而且尽管我们带来的无疑是一场可怕灾祸的消息，我们同时也给许多忠厚的灵魂带去了宽慰和喜悦——他们正以为那些活着归来、安然无恙的人都已葬身大海，饱受着死亡的煎熬呢。

===Original===
The valor of the Spaniards did not avail to rescue any ships but ours, for they were too late and had to return without being able to give chase to the English ships that kept guard over the *San Juan*, the *Bahama*, and the *San Ildefonso*. We were still four leagues from land when we saw them making towards us. A southerly gale was blowing up and it was clear to all on board the *Santa Ana* that if we did not soon get into port we should have a bad time of it. Once more we were filled with anxiety; once more we lost hope almost in sight of safety, and when a few hours more on the cruel sea would have seen us safe and sound in harbor. Night was coming on black and angry; the sky was covered with dark clouds which seemed to lie on the face of the ocean, and the lurid flashes which lighted them up from time to time added terror to the gloom. The sea waxing in fury every instant, as if it were not yet satiated, raved and roared with hungry rage, demanding more and yet more victims. The remnant of the mighty fleet which a short time since had defied its fury combined with that of the foe was not to escape from the wrath of the angry element which, implacable as an ancient god and pitiless to the last, was as cruel to the victor as to the conquered.

I could read the signs of deep depression in the face not only of my master but of the Admiral, Álava, who, in spite of his wounds, still kept on his feet and signalled to the frigate to make all possible speed; but, instead of responding to his very natural haste, the *Themis* prepared to shorten sail so as to be able to keep before the gale. I shared the general dismay and could not help reflecting on the irony with which Fate mocks at our surest calculations and best founded hopes, on the swiftness with which she flings us from happy security to the depth of misery. Here we were, on the wide ocean, that majestic emblem of human life. A gust of wind and it is completely transformed, the light ripple which gently caressed the vessel’s side swells into a mountain of water that lashes and beats it, the soft music of the wavelets in a calm turns to a loud, hoarse voice, threatening the frail barque which flings itself into the waters as though its keel were unable to balance it, to rise the next moment buffeted and tossed by the very wave that has lifted it from the abyss. A lovely day ends in a fearful night, or, on the other hand, a radiant moon that illumines an infinite sky and soothes the soul, pales before an angry sun at whose light all nature quakes with dismay.

We had experienced all these viscissitudes, and in addition, those which are the result of the will of man. We had suffered shipwreck in the midst of defeat; after escaping once we had been compelled to fight again, this time with success; and then, when we thought ourselves out of our troubles, when we hailed Cádiz with delight, we were once more at the mercy of the tempest which had treacherously deluded us only to destroy us outright. Such a succession of adverse fortune seemed monstrous—it was like the malignant aberrations of a divinity trying to do all the harm he could devise to us hapless mortals—but it was only the natural course of things at sea, combined with the fortune of war. Given a combination of these two fearful forces and none but an idiot can be astonished at the disasters that must ensue.

===Chinese===
西班牙人的英勇除我们这艘之外没能救出任何船，他们来得太迟了，只好空手返回，无法去追赶那些看守着*圣胡安号*、*巴哈马号*和*圣伊尔德丰索号*（San Ildefonso）的英国船。我们离陆地还有四里格的时候，望见它们朝我们驶来。南边一场大风正在刮起来，*圣安娜号*上人人都明白：若不能赶紧进港，我们就要吃大苦头了。我们又一次忧心如焚；又一次在几乎望见安全的当口失去了希望——再多几个小时，这无情的大海本会把我们安安稳稳送进港口的。黑夜挟着一张黑沉沉、怒冲冲的脸压了过来；天空布满乌云，仿佛就压在洋面上，不时照亮它们的惨淡电光更给这昏暗添了几分恐怖。大海每一刻都在增长着狂怒，仿佛还没有吃饱似的，饿着肚子咆哮怒吼，索要着一个又一个、更多的祭品。这支不久之前还敢同时对抗它和敌人之怒的威武舰队的残部，终究逃不出这狂怒元素的暴怒——它像远古神祇一般无法平息、至终毫无怜悯，对战胜者和战败者一样残忍。

不仅我主人的脸上，就连海军上将阿拉瓦的脸上，我也读得出深深沮丧的迹象；他不顾伤痛，仍旧硬撑着站在那里，打信号命巡防舰竭力加速；可是*忒弥斯号*非但没有理会他这再自然不过的急迫，反倒准备收帆减速，好在大风里稳得住船身。我和众人一样惊慌，忍不住思索起命运的讽刺来——它如何嘲弄我们最有把握的盘算和最有根据的希望，如何在一瞬间把我们从幸福的安稳甩进苦难的深渊。我们此刻就在这浩瀚的海洋上，这人生最庄严的象征。一阵风过，它便整个变了样：方才还温柔爱抚着船舷的细细涟漪，涨成一座抽打撞击它的大山；平静时小波浪的柔柔和声，变成嘶哑的大嗓门，威吓着那艘脆弱的小船——它一头扎进浪里，仿佛龙骨再也压不住船身，转眼又被恰恰是那道把它从深渊里托起来的巨浪抽打得东倒西歪。明媚的白昼以可怕的黑夜收场；或者反过来，一轮照亮无垠天空、抚慰人心的皎洁明月，在一轮怒日面前黯然失色——那日光一亮，万物无不惊惶战栗。
